# `engine/` — weight residency (W5 first half)

**The two device experiments that used to stand between this file's launch configurations and
"trusted" have both run**, in the 2026-10-11 foreground, zero-subagent window whose numbers are
recorded in `bench/performance-status.md` §6. Both came back confirming the engine's choice, so the
comments in `kernels_glue.cpp` and `kernels_proj.cpp` now cite a measurement rather than an inference.
One half of E2 is still open and says so at the end of its entry.

* **E1 — RESOLVED: glue's correctness gate at 1024 threads passes at the same floor as 256.** The
  question was real: `glue_kernels.h:83-102` derives `PER = H/THREADS`, so 256 → 4 elements/thread and
  an 8-partial warp tree while 1024 → 1/thread and a 32-partial tree — a **different F32 summation order
  for the mean of squares** — and a norm's legal floor is 1 ULP with no cancellation band, so `ndiff`
  was the only criterion that could tell the two widths apart.
  *Measured:* `784 comparisons`, 0 failures, `g1_n` **gated 0 / raw 1**, both residual adds bit-exact,
  and every negative still caught at its own stage — a legal error profile identical to the 256-thread
  run.
  *What that settles, and in which direction:* the engine's `<256>` is a **measured preference, not a
  numerical requirement**. Nothing in the engine's arithmetic depends on the width, so a move to 1024 is
  purely the performance question glue's §4 sweep raises (≈2.78 vs 3.75 µs/launch, with the
  bimodality caveat `bench/performance-status.md` §6 attaches to launch-bound numbers) and not a
  numerics blocker. This file no longer owes that experiment; `prototype/glue/results.md:170`'s ask is
  satisfied.
* **E2 — RESOLVED (prototype plan): unpacked beats packed at every rows/block.** The engine
  instantiates `PACK_X=false` (`kernels_proj.cpp`), which is both the gated config and — now measured
  again in a clean window — the faster one: cold, on the prototype's 48-launch token plan at rows/block
  32, **unpacked 0.0528 ms/launch = 65.1 % of the read probe** against **packed 0.0593 ms/launch =
  58.0 %**, with unpacked ahead at every rows/block swept. That matters because `PACK_X=false` bills the
  caller ≈48 cast launches/token (≈0.089 ms at the 1.85 µs floor) for handing F32 `x`; the measured gap
  is larger than that bill, so `false` stays the constant and `kernels_proj.cpp` keeps its citation.
  *Still open in E2:* the A/B through **`engine/kernels_proj.cpp`'s own launcher**, with the casts the
  engine actually pays, on the engine's own buffer plan. The prototype ordering is now established; that
  the engine reproduces it is a token-level measurement and belongs with `decode_step`'s timing pass.

Status: **gate PASS — 52 checks, 0 failures** (`engine/loader_test`, built by
`engine/build.sh`). Residency only: no token loop, no per-request state, no kernel calls
(`README.md`). Binary: `engine/loader_test`. Gate: `engine/loader_test` itself; its
per-tensor shape/dtype reference is generated by `engine/weights_reference.py`, which
reuses `tools/weights_report.py`'s own header reader and byte assertion.

```bash
./engine/build.sh && ./engine/loader_test          # from the repo root
./engine/loader_test --no-verify                   # skips sections 8's copy-back+hashing
```

**Every timing here is provisional.** `tools/gpu_lock.sh util` read 95–99 % at both ends of
every run: `flash_serve` — the server producing this session's own tokens — polls the iGPU
and is outside the lock (`gpu_lock.sh` records util and deliberately does not gate on it).
Correctness claims are unaffected; bandwidth numbers move by more than 2× between runs.

## What is resident

| claim | value |
|---|---|
| text tensors loaded / vision skipped / total | **335 / 153 / 488** (re-derived from the checkpoint; the G0 split, not the 344/144 miscount) |
| payload | **1 545 696 960 B = 1.5457 GB = 1.440 GiB** — §01.7's text-only total to 4 dp, and `tools/weights_report.py`'s own `text-only totals` line agrees |
| allocations | **335** (one `hipMalloc` per tensor); `lm_head.weight` is an *alias* of `lm.embed_tokens.weight`, same device pointer, so the tied 485.0 MiB table is counted once |
| name table | 335 checkpoint→engine entries, 336 engine→tensor lookups (335 + the alias); both directions asserted bijective |
| pool cost | free 34.354 → 32.174 GiB: **1.705 GiB given up for 1.440 GiB of weights**, the 0.265 GiB gap being per-allocation rounding across 335 allocations (+0.476 GiB more for the 485 MiB pinned staging buffer). At 4 % of a ~34 GiB pool this is an observation, not a problem |
| special cases | 36 F32 tensors (§01.6 `A_log`/`linear_attn.norm.weight`, kept F32 per A-02), 36 tensors whose leaf is not `.weight`, **0** tensors whose size is not a multiple of 16 B — so the warm kernel's byte-tail path never fires |

## Phase table (best of 3 gated runs; the run quoted is `gate_run3`)

| phase | time | rate | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| parse | 0.4 ms | — | 59 840 header bytes, 1 shard |
| staging buffer | 124.6 ms | — | `hipHostMalloc` of the 485.0 MiB staging buffer — not free, and it is device-visible |
| alloc | 148.8 ms (range 148–731) | — | the 335 `hipMalloc`s: page reservation, no transfer |
| stage | 82.0 ms | 18.8 GB/s | file → pinned staging (page-cache bound) |
| **H2D cold** | 195.4 ms (range 76–483) | **7.9 GB/s** | destination pages fresh; first touch is the whole cost here |
| **warm pass** | 8.6 ms | **180.4 GB/s** | first GPU *read* over 1.440 GiB. Not ~35 GB/s: `hipMemcpy` already materialised those pages, so the faulting happened during the upload, not here. Non-zero checksum asserted (a zero would mean a no-op kernel) |
| H2D steady | 19.9 ms | **76.7 GB/s** | 1.421 GiB re-uploaded with pages committed — inside the devices doc's 76–82 band, which is what validates the rest of the table |
| **cold load** | **559.8 ms of phases vs 794.0 ms wall** | | parse+staging+alloc+stage+H2D+warm; the gap is the steady probe and bookkeeping |
| D2H (verify only) | 55.8 ms | 27.7 GB/s | copy-back of 1.440 GiB in 335 chunks, sync per chunk — see the discrepancy below |
| verify (opt-in) | 7 627.9 ms | | D2H + SHA-256 of both sides, dominated by ~3.1 GB of hashing at ~400 MB/s. `--no-verify` skips the copy-back and the hashing entirely, which is why it is off by default in the loader |

`--no-verify` end to end, one run, util 95 %→29 %: cold load **1 258 ms of phases / 1 552 ms
wall** (alloc 730.8 ms, H2D cold 157.5 ms = 9.8 GB/s, warm 9.1 ms = 169.7 GB/s, steady H2D
63.7 GB/s). So the startup cost the engine will pay is ~0.5–1.5 s, and the spread is
allocation + first touch under contention, not the transfers.

## The D2H discrepancy: measured 27.7 GB/s vs the doc's 63–70 GB/s

`devices/strix-halo-unified-memory.md` records `hipMemcpy H2D / D2H = 76–82 / 63–70 GB/s`.
The loader's copy-back measures 18–28 GB/s. **The doc is probably right and my number is a
different quantity.** The old claim comes from `tools/bw_probe_hip.cpp:155-166`: *one* bulk
`hipMemcpy` of the whole buffer into a pinned host allocation, best-of-N, on a quiet box.
Mine is 335 chunked copies (64 B … 485 MiB) with a `hipDeviceSynchronize()` after each, at
95–99 % util. The arithmetic fits that reading: at 63 GB/s the bytes need 24.5 ms, the
measured 55.8 ms leaves ~31 ms over 335 syncs ≈ 93 µs per sync — and the same window is
where H2D steady still reached 76.7 GB/s, i.e. H2D at spec and D2H at 2.3× below it is hard
to blame on the bus. What would settle it: a chunked-vs-bulk D2H sweep (same total bytes,
(a) one transfer, (b) the loader's 335 chunks with a sync each, (c) 335 chunks with one
sync at the end) at util ≈ 0 and util ≈ 98 %. If (c) ≈ (a) and (b) is ~2× slower, it is the
per-copy sync and the doc's row should be labelled "bulk, per-transfer"; if (a) falls with
util, the row needs a contention caveat instead.

Two notes for whoever runs it: the loader's D2H does **not** need to be fast (verification is
one-time and off by default), and dropping the per-copy sync would change the timing
instrument, not the guarantee — the verify pass would still hash every byte.

## What is deliberately not measured here

Nothing in `prototype/` was called (other agents own those files), so no kernel result
depends on this loader and nothing here depends on a kernel. The dependency is the
checkpoint file only.

# W5b on disk: `state.{h,cpp}` + the launcher layer — proven, deferred, open

Everything in this section is **compiled, never run on a device**. No allocation has happened, so
no number here is a measurement of anything except the compiler's willingness.

## Proven on CPU, and — for the one kernel this phase added a launcher for — on the device

* `state_sizes.py` re-derives §08.1's shapes from `state.h`'s own constants and reproduces §00.5's
  rows (18.0 MiB GDN `S`, 12288 B/token KV). PASS.
* §04.5's gated RMSNorm is bit-exact against the pins on `dec8` (144 cases / 294 912 elements,
  ndiff 0) by the CPU mirror in `prototype/gdn_gated_norm/`, and so is `specimpl.rmsnorm_gated`
  called the way speccheck calls it (BF16 weight). That is the only new arithmetic this phase
  committed to, and it is the one the engine will call.
* **That kernel has since run on the device and its gate passes**: worst case ndiff 1/2048, max ULP 1
  (the reduction-order floor `neg_probe.py` predicted before the run), zeros 0 / NaN 0, six negatives
  localising, 2.936 µs/launch at spread 1.4 % → ≈0.053 ms/token for the 18 launches. That is
  `prototype/gdn_gated_norm/run.py --gate`, whose first two attempts failed on the gate's own fixture
  writer rather than on the kernel — see `prototype/gdn_gated_norm/results.md` §7–§9 and
  `bench/performance-status.md` §6. **None of `engine/`'s own code has run on a device**: no buffer in
  `state.cpp` has been allocated and no launcher in `kernels_*.cpp` has been launched from the engine.

## Deferred, with the price named

1. **Unpacked projections.** `proj_gemv` is called once per projection (q, k, v, o, in_proj_qkv,
   in_proj_z, in_proj_b, in_proj_a, out_proj…) rather than through the packed multi-weight form the
   kernel supports. Now that `decode_step` exists the real bill is known rather than estimated:
   **424 launches/token, 114 of them projection GEMVs** (`gate.py` section (d) asserts the split).
   The packable part is `in_proj_a`+`in_proj_b` (16 rows, 32 KiB each, same input row) alongside
   `in_proj_qkv`/`in_proj_z`: one packed launch instead of four per GDN layer, so 54 launches ≈
   **0.10 ms/token** at the ≈1.85 µs floor (`tools/launch_overhead_hip.cpp`). The earlier "≈358
   launches, ≈0.12 ms removable" in this file was arithmetic over a plan the loop does not use.
2. **Dtype adapters: 115 cast launches/token.** `mlp_gemv` and `gdn_decode`'s conv/gates/rec take
   F32 activations, while `glue`/`proj_gemv`/`gdn_gated_norm` emit BF16, so the engine widens: 5 per
   GDN layer, 4 per attention layer, 1 before `lm_head`. 48 of the 115 are the MLP's (its input row
   and its `act`, twice across 24 layers). `kernels_cast.cpp` now exists, and the GDN layer's three
   widenings (`in_proj_qkv`, `in_proj_a`, `in_proj_b`) are already folded into ONE launch
   (`cast_bf16_to_f32_3`), which is why a GDN layer costs 5 casts and not 7. The real fix is still a
   BF16-staged form of `mlp_gateup`/`mlp_down` — `proj_gemv` proved that is bit-identical for a dot
   product — which would delete 48 of the 115. Not done: it means touching a graduated kernel and
   re-gating it, and `mlp_gemv`'s gate belongs to another phase. What was done here instead is the
   other half of the old estimate: `conv1d.weight` and `dt_bias` are widened **once per request** by
   `prepare_weight_adapters`, not once per token, so those 36 rows cost 36 launches per request.

Both deferrals are launch-count, not bandwidth: at ≈240 GB/s the token's memory floor is well under
1 ms and 424 launches is ≈0.78 ms, so they are the first thing a perf pass should attack — after the
token matches.

## Open questions this phase did not decide

* **Conv window dtype — corrected in the spec, 2026-10-11.** `state.h` stores it F32 (1.266 MiB)
  where §08.1's cell used to say BF16 (0.633 MiB), because the graduated `gdn_decode` conv kernel
  takes F32 in and out and 36 cast launches per token is the wrong price for 0.6 MiB on a device
  using 4 % of its pool. The stored *values* are BF16-exact either way. §08.1 and §00.5's conv row
  now say F32 / 1.27 MiB, naming the old cell; `state_sizes.py` greps both pages for that figure, so
  the doc cannot drift back to BF16 without the size check failing.
* **§08.1 / §00.5 doc slips — corrected, 2026-10-11** (all four were found by `state_sizes.py`, and
  its lines are now `FIXED …` rather than `OPEN …`): the "4 KiB/token/layer" KV cell contradicted the
  same table's 12 KiB/token total (2 KiB/layer is what the shapes give, and what the audit's proposed
  8 KiB was not); the MTP KV row had the same 2× slip; §00.5's 3.07 GiB at full context is 3.00 GiB
  from its own 12288 B/token. **Still open:** KV layout is head-major `[head][slot][dim]` per the
  kernel, not §08.5's `[slots][2][256]` — §08.5 says so no more than it did, and `state.h` carries
  the departure.
* **§04.5 / A-01 still state the affine in F32** in `specification/`, and that is a reading the
  pins refuse: with the weight rounded to BF16 and widened back to F32 it misses 74 860 of 294 912
  elements, with the raw F32 checkpoint weight 105 683 (`prototype/gdn_gated_norm/results.md` §1).
  `speccheck.specimpl.rmsnorm_gated` is **not** among those: called the way speccheck calls it it
  is bit-exact, 0 of 294 912, because `Weights` hands it a BF16 weight. An earlier revision of this
  file reported the oracle as 105 683 elements off — that was the mirror probe handing the oracle
  an F32-widened weight, retracted in `fc444d0`, and the probe now asserts its oracle row at 0.
  Correcting the spec wording is a decision for the spec owner, not this phase.
* **`lm_head` full + host argmax**, not the fused single-kernel form: the fused form resolves ties
  to the highest id and §03.4 fixes ties to the lowest. Costs ≈1 MB D2H + a host scan (≈0.04 ms)
  per token and gives the gate its logits. Worth revisiting only with a tie-corrected fused kernel.

# W5b's token loop on disk: `decode_step` + `decode_run` + `gate.py` — compiled, none of it run

2026-10-11. `decode_step.{h,cpp}`, `decode_run.cpp`, `gate.py`, and the four launcher TUs that were
missing (`kernels_cast`, `kernels_mlp`, `kernels_attn`, `kernels_lmhead`). All twelve TUs compile
clean at `-O3` and `engine/build.sh` links `engine/build/{loader_test,decode_run}`. **No engine
binary has been executed**, so nothing in this section is a measurement.

What is settled without a device:

* `check_weights(store)` pins every shape and §01.6 dtype this loop depends to the checkpoint it
  actually loaded — including `lm_head.weight` being the embedding blob rather than a second 485 MiB.
  **This bullet used to claim more than it had earned.** It listed `dt_bias`/`conv1d.weight` as pinned
  BF16, and the first device run proved the `conv1d.weight` entry wrong: the table claimed rank 2
  [6144,4] and the shard holds rank 3 [6144,1,4] (it is one of the checkpoint's 18 Conv1d weights —
  every rank-3 tensor in the file). The store was right, the claim was a guess, and the type system
  made the guess inevitable: `Expected` was `{d0, d1, dtype}` with `d1 == 0` meaning rank 1, so rank 3
  was inexpressible. Fixed in 92d8e5f — `Expected` now carries `rank` explicitly, and the
  [6144,1,4] → flat [6144][4] collapse that `conv_kernel`'s `C[c*K + j]` wants is asserted in
  `prepare_weight_adapters`, where the flattening is spent, not in the checker. `dt_bias` was the
  other guess and was already right: BF16 rank-1 [16].
* `engine/weights_contract.py` is why that class of bug is over. It reads the shard header, `state.h`
  and the `Expected` table as three independent sources and fails on any disagreement — 320 names
  clean, plus the rank/F32/§03.5-18-6 census — and it bites when handed a mutated copy (rank-2 conv,
  dt_bias as [16,1], A_log as BF16 each FAIL at their own row alone). Shape claims are now checked on
  CPU, which is the only place they can be checked cheaply; the alternative was one GPU window per
  wrong rank.
* `gate.py --no-engine` proves the `dec8` fixture pairs with itself — 26 assertions, including that
  the bare `logits_last.npy` is the prefill (argmax 760) and the bare `input_ids.npy` is decode7's —
  and that `state_sizes.py`'s bytes are the bytes `decode_run` will report. That half is green.
* The block plan is agreed between two independent implementations: `gate.py` counts one token's
  launches from §07 in Python, `planned_launch_counts()` derives the same numbers from §00's
  constants in C++, and the gate fails on any mismatch. glue 49 · proj 114 · gdn 72 · attn 6 ·
  mlp 48 · cast 115 · other 20 = **424**.

What a first device run is most likely to break, in the order to look at them:

0. **The weight table.** It already broke once, before a single kernel launched: `check_weights`
   aborted on `conv1d.weight`'s rank. `engine/weights_contract.py` now retires that failure mode on
   CPU, so if it reappears the shard changed rather than the claim.
1. **The KV append inside the fused attention launch.** `stage_head()` gates the §05.7 cache write on
   `q_rope != nullptr` — the same null check as the three stage dumps — so `kernels_attn.cpp` refuses
   null stage pointers rather than silently skipping the append. If the fused path turns out not to
   append at all, every step after the prompt reads empty KV and the first wrong token appears at
   decode step 0 with the prompt steps also wrong.
2. **The q/k/v slice order handed to `rec_kernel`.** `mixed_qkv` is [q|k|v] in 2048-wide blocks
   (§04.1) and `decode_step` passes `g_u`, `g_u+2048`, `g_u+4096`. A k/v transposition shows up as a
   prompt that samples correctly and a chain that diverges at step 1.
3. **The conv-window roll-back.** `conv_kernel` writes the rolled window to scratch and the layer's
   window is updated by one D2D copy; if the copy's size or the slot indexing is off, the drift is
   gradual and shows up several tokens in, not immediately.
4. **The logits band.** `cos ≥ 0.9995` is §10.1 G5's published figure for incremental-vs-batch
   logits, not a number measured against this engine. If the ids come out exactly right and the band
   fails, that is a policy question about which §10 figure governs a token-level logits vector —
   report it, do not loosen the constant. `ndiff` and max-ULP are already excluded on principle
   (§10.1 F1–F4).

* **Fixture: `dec8`, not the `prefix24dec8` `bench/build-queue.md` W5 proposed.** `dec8` is what the
  task named, it is the fixture whose per-step dumps (`decode{s}.input_ids`,
  `decode{s}.logits_last`) make the pairing provable, and its prompt is 17 tokens rather than 24 — so
  the attention stages run at ≤25 lanes against `MAXSC` 512. Same assertions would run against
  `prefix24dec8`; `gate.py` hard-codes the `dec8` path and nothing else, so that is a second call, not
  a flag.

# Two device runs in: the loop runs, and six failed checks had one cause — §07.5 armed during the prompt

2026-10-11, two operator-run foreground device passes (workers do not touch the GPU, `AGENTS.md`).
Nothing here is a timing except where it explicitly says it is not one.

**What the runs established.** The loader loads: 335 tensors, 1.5457 GB resident, ≈1.0 s.
`check_weights: PASS` — the rank-3 `conv1d.weight` claim that aborted the very first attempt is closed
by `92d8e5f` and `engine/weights_contract.py`, which is the CPU-side reason it cannot come back.
The `state:` and `LAUNCH` lines verify against §00/§07 item by item (gate sections (c) and (d) pass),
so the 424-launch plan and the per-request byte counts are agreed between the two independent readings
and the binary. Risk 0 above ("the weight table") is retired, and so is the part of the hand-off that
said no engine code had ever touched the device.

**The six failures, and they were one bug — a phase bug, not numerics.** The run log:

    prompt 6 | fed 9338 | sampled 248046 | top 19.3246193 | P 6 -> 7 [STOP]
    stopped at step 6 (§07.5: id 248046)
    - one step per prompt token: 7 vs 17
    - 8 generation steps: 0
    - all 8 generated ids match, in order: engine [] / pinned [6511, 314, 9338, 369, 2972, 57590, 159034, 271]

`decode_run` applied §07.5's stop check to *every* step, prompt steps included. The fixture prompt is
the manifest's `sources[0].ids` — a rendered chat template — so it legitimately contains the
end-of-turn id **248046 at index 8** (and the think-start/end ids 248068/248069 later); at prompt index
6 the engine's own argmax happened to be 248046, which ended a 17-token prompt after 7 steps. During
the prompt phase the sample is discarded and the next token fed comes from the fixture, so a stop match
there is meaningless. Fixed in `5e7114b`: **the check is armed only in the generation phase**; a
prompt-phase match is still printed, with its own marker, and the `STEP` line gained `stop=0|1` meaning
"the §07.5 check fired" rather than "the sample equals `kStopToken`". Two consequences worth keeping in
mind: `st.stopped` is decode_step's raw match (either phase) while `RESULT stopped=` is the run's
decision, and **no claim about the numerics follows from either run** — nothing was generated, so the
eight pinned ids are still neither matched nor contradicted.

One diagnostic the runs did give for free: the prompt path runs end to end and its samples are ordinary
token ids, not zeros or NaN — `[846, 198, 3710, 2614, 314, 279, 248046]` at top logits 14.70–23.07
(`tmp/engine_gate/sampled.txt`). They are *not* the template's own next tokens (steps 2, 3, 5, 6 are
not), which is expected twice over: the reference prefills the prompt as one chunked forward while the
engine feeds it token-at-a-time through the recurrence (§04.7 vs §04.4, and the README's "What this is
NOT yet"), and a model is free to want something else mid-template. A gate-side note while reading the
log: section (b) can pass on **stale** `logits*.bin` if a `--dry-run` wrote that work dir earlier — the
first run stopped at index 6, so it wrote `logits0..6` only, and its six failures were all in section
(a); `gate.py` now clears the dir so a missing dump reads as missing.

**And the gate is now written to name this bug itself** (`b7b6766` and the stale-dump follow-up):
"one step per prompt token" reports the last STEP's kind/index/sample/stop plus the RESULT line and
says which of the two causes it is; a new check fails if any prompt-phase step fires §07.5;
`--selftest`'s fourth case replays the truncation (`--break-prompt-stop 6`) and requires both checks to
bite at that step. `SELFTEST PASS` (clean dry run + four injections), CPU-only.

## The first ms/step figure, recorded as *not* a measurement

**Superseded the same day, twice over.** The `14.631 ms/step` below stayed a non-measurement — it did,
and `bench/performance-status.md` §6 now carries the real row at **11.978 / 12.053 / 12.232**.
And item 1 below named HIP graphs as the first perf item, which the measured dispatch curve refutes:
§1's probe replayed 64 captured launches at 1.78 µs against 1.85 µs eager, so a one-token graph buys
≈4 % of the dispatch bill (≈0.06 ms) and not the gap. Both corrections live in
"the first engine throughput row", at the end of this file.

The second run printed `RESULT … ms_per_step=14.631`. **This is an observation, not a number.** It is a
gate run's by-product, which `bench/performance-status.md` §6 refuses as a measurement; it is one
unwarmed first pass over freshly allocated state and scratch, i.e. the first-GPU-touch regime
`devices/strix-halo-unified-memory.md` measures at ~35 GB/s instead of ~240; there is no burst and no
best-of-N; and no `util` was recorded for the window. Do not quote it and do not diff it against
anything as if it were a measurement.

What it is good for is sizing the first perf item, which comes **after** correctness is green. The
ledger's §6 op-sum is **8.03 ms → ~124 tok/s** (7.975 ms before the `gdn_decode` re-take — same six
families, one replaced row), so the unwarmed engine is ~1.8× that. The two most likely causes, in order
of expected size, are both launch-side:

1. **424 launches per token plus one device sync per step.** At the measured 1.85 µs dispatch floor
   (`tools/launch_overhead_hip.cpp`) the launch bill alone is ≈0.78 ms/token, and that assumes a warm,
   deep queue; the step then syncs at the logits, so the host waits on all of it every token. **HIP
   graphs** — capture one token's 424 launches once, replay them — are the tool that removes the
   per-launch cost, and they are now the first perf item, because nothing about correctness still needs
   the launches to be individually visible.
2. The two deferrals named above (packed projections ≈0.10 ms, a BF16-staged MLP to delete 48 of the
   115 casts), which shrink that same bill rather than its overhead.

The honest caveat on the comparison: 8.03 ms is a **sum of per-op cold bursts measured separately**, and
`bench/commit-review-2026-10-11.md` already graded that sum "overstated as a description of the engine",
so 14.631 − 8.03 is not a measured gap and nobody should defend or attack it as one. The first real
engine timing is a warm burst in a zero-subagent, `gpu_lock.sh`-recorded window, with the same best-of-N
discipline as every other §6 row, and it owes both the ms/token and the tok/s rows the ledger has never
had for the engine.

## The three flagged risks, checked statically — no device, no gate loosened

1. **The q/k/v slice order handed to `rec_kernel`: correct, and worth noting that nothing had ever
   checked it.** `speccheck/specimpl.py:270-272` slices the conv output as `q = u[:, :KEY_DIM]`,
   `k = u[:, KEY_DIM:2*KEY_DIM]`, `v = u[:, 2*KEY_DIM:]` with `KEY_DIM = H_K*D_K = 16×128 = 2048` — the
   same 2048 as `state.h`'s `kValueDim` — so `[q|k|v]` at float offsets 0 / 2048 / 4096, exactly what
   `decode_step.cpp` hands `gdn_rec` (`sc.g_u`, `+kValueDim`, `+2*kValueDim`). Inside a slice
   `rec_kernel` indexes `x[h*D + j]` (`prototype/gdn_decode/gdn_decode_kernels.h:90-120`), i.e.
   head-major with `D = 128`, which matches the reference's `.reshape(T, H_K, D_K)`. Why reading was the
   only way to know: `prototype/gdn_decode`'s own driver fed `rec_kernel` from the **already-sliced**
   pins (`q_post_conv`/`k_post_conv`/`v_post_conv`), so §04's device gate could not have seen a
   transposition in the engine's offsets whatever they were. The first run whose ids come out exact is
   the empirical counterpart of this reading.
2. **The conv-window roll-back: size, roll order and slot indexing all check out.** Reference and kernel
   roll the same way — `new_win = padded[-(K-1):]` (`specimpl.py:260`) is `[win₁, win₂, raw]`, and the
   kernel writes `new_win[j] = win[j+1]` then `new_win[K-2] = raw` (`gdn_decode_kernels.h:82-86`), with
   `K-2` the last valid row so nothing runs past the buffer. The engine's scratch is
   `kConvWindow × kConvChannels` floats (3 × 6144 = 18 432) and the roll-back copy is
   `kConvWindow*kConvChannels*sizeof(float)` = 73 728 B, exactly one layer's stride; and
   `conv_window_layer()` strides by `gdn_slot(layer)` — the *slot*, the same index that selects the
   widened `conv1d.weight`, so the two cannot drift apart by layer (`state.cpp:76-81`). The copy is a
   `hipMemcpyAsync` on the default stream, enqueued between the conv launch and the launches that read
   `g_u`, and the engine uses one stream, so there is no race — a second stream would break it, which is
   worth remembering if a later pass parallelises layers. The rank-3 → flat `[6144][4]` widening of that
   weight is refused unless the rank is exactly what the shard holds (`prepare_weight_adapters`).
3. **The logits band: the citation is the soft spot, not the arithmetic — and the constant stays put.**
   Three separate facts, none of them a gate edit:
   * *What G5's 0.9995 actually is.* §10.1 G5 is "prefill `T` then decode `D` equals one prefill of
     `T+D`" — the reference against **itself**, no engine in the loop. The engine-vs-reference
     comparison this gate makes is strictly harder (a different implementation of every op), and §10's
     published ceiling for a whole-cascade comparison is §10.2's `cos ≥ 0.999, meanrel ≤ 5 %` — whose
     own measured worst cascade cos on `dec8` is **0.99923**, *below* the floor this gate hard-gates.
     So a run with eight exactly-right ids can legitimately land in [0.999, 0.9995) and fail here. When
     it does, the open question is which §10 figure governs engine-vs-reference token logits — an owner
     decision, not this file's and not a constant to edit. `BAND` was not touched.
   * *What a token-level cos can see.* cos over 248 320 entries is dominated by the mass of the
     vocabulary: a 1-ULP-level error profile (bf16 ≈ 0.2 % per element) puts cos at ≈ 1 − σ²/2 ≈
     0.999 998, so the floor is nowhere near binding when the arithmetic is right — while still
     discriminating gross errors (the §10.5 q/gate mis-read measured cos −0.14). What it cannot see is a
     **wrong token**: at a token level the informative statistic is the top-1/top-2 margin against the
     worst element error, which is what `gate.py`'s `metrics()` prints as `gap12` and `margin` next to
     the separate argmax check. When an id is wrong, read those, not the cos.
   * *The other two figures are already in the file.* G6's 0.9999 is reported-not-gated, and speccheck's
     own G6 gate meets it on these same pinned `decode{s}.logits_last.npy`, so the reference side is not
     the risky side; the engine adds what speccheck does not have — bf16 kernels, and a prompt fed
     token-at-a-time through the recurrence rather than the chunked form (§10.1 G3 self-checks
     chunk-vs-recurrent at cos ≥ 0.9999). A first run landing between G5's floor and §10.2's cascade
     ceiling is a reportable finding, not a surprise.

**Hand-off, unchanged and ready:** `./engine/build.sh && .venv/bin/python engine/gate.py`, in a quiescent
window (`tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire`, zero subagents). Sections (0)/(c)/(d) should be green as before,
and (a) will be the first token-level correctness result this repo has had.

---

# First end-to-end pass: `ENGINE GATE PASS` at 93 checks — and the first engine token costs 11.978 ms

2026-10-11, third device attempt, operator-run in the foreground zero-subagent window that also produced
`bench/performance-status.md` §6. The two failures before it were the gate's and the runner's, not the
engine's: the weight-rank contract (`92d8e5f` + `engine/weights_contract.py`) and §07.5 armed during the
prompt phase (`5e7114b`). Nothing in this section was measured by a worker — `AGENTS.md` forbids it — and
the device figures are quoted, not re-made.

## (1) What the pass established

The three lines that make this a token result rather than a load test:

    one step per prompt token: 17 vs 17
    8 generation steps: 8
    all 8 generated ids match, in order: [6511, 314, 9338, 369, 2972, 57590, 159034, 271]

§10.1 G7 exactness holds on `dec8`, and it holds through the two places no prototype gate could reach:
the §05.7 cache append that lives behind `stage_head()`'s null test inside the fused attention launch
(risk 1), and the `[q|k|v]` slice offsets handed to `rec_kernel` (risk 2). Each had predicted a specific
signature — a wrong id at decode step 0, or a chain that diverges at step 1 — and neither appeared, so
both risks are retired by the ids rather than by reading. Risk 4 (the logits band) is answered below.

Per-step logits against the pins, recomputed from the dumps that pass wrote (`tmp/engine_gate/logits17…24.bin`)
with `gate.py::metrics()`'s own arithmetic, so these are the gate's numbers and not a second metric:

| step | cos (F64 accumulation, §10.2's definition) | meanrel | ref top1−top2 gap ÷ largest element error |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.999954 | 0.940 % | 23.0× |
| 1 | **0.999886** | 1.451 % | 13.4× |
| 2 | 0.999961 | 0.890 % | 33.7× |
| 3 | 0.999909 | 1.273 % | 18.5× |
| 4 | 0.999935 | 1.095 % | 21.3× |
| 5 | 0.999903 | 1.413 % | 26.8× |
| 6 | 0.999905 | 1.412 % | 11.2× |
| 7 | 0.999968 | 0.730 % | **7.4×** |

`ndiff` is 248 316–248 319 out of 248 320 at every step — what a chain this long is expected to produce,
and correctly excluded (§10.1 F1–F4: a chained dot's legal ndiff is 29–47 %, so ndiff there proves
nothing). The decisive column is the last one: **at every step the reference's own top-1/top-2 margin is at
least 7.4× the largest element error the engine produces** — the error had to grow almost an order of
magnitude before a token could flip, and after eight tokens none flipped. Token identity is what §10 makes
the correctness gate (G7), and this is what supporting evidence looks like next to it.

### The band question the pass raised, the decision, and the floor measured under it

Step 1 landed at cos 0.999886 — above the gated G5 floor (0.9995), below G6's advisory 0.9999. Two
readings were possible: a token-level logits vector legitimately carries the whole 24-layer cascade, so
G6's figure simply does not belong to this comparison; or the engine has a small real error that the
margin column happens to be hiding. **Decided: G5's 0.9995 stays the hard floor and G6 stays advisory** —
an end-to-end quantity must not be judged by a per-layer or per-operation bar, because the error it is
allowed to carry is the accumulated error of all 24 layers and the whole chain. G3i (0.9999, one isolated
layer, "no upstream cascade can enter"), G5 (0.9995, two routes to one position) and G6 (0.9999, one
softmax form inside an already-full cascade) measure three different things; the reasoning is recorded in
`engine/gate.py`'s BAND comment, not here, because the band comment is what a future reader edits. The
advisory note still prints, and step 1 stays a documented, non-failing observation.

Which reading is true is now measured, on CPU, by `engine/logits_floor.py`: it rebuilds each step's logits
from the reference's own inputs (`decode{s}.L23.s_out` + `lm.norm.weight` + the tied table — the rebuild
reproduces the pin at cos 0.999999999, which is a per-step pairing proof with no last-writer-wins name in
it) and asks how far *legal* reimplementation can push cos:

| what may differ | worst cos over the 8 steps |
|---|---|
| summation order of the logits dot (K permuted; split-K 8 and 32 partials, forward and reversed) | 0.999999999 |
| the norm's mean-of-squares summed in another order | 0.999999999 |
| the pin's own BF16 rounding of the logits, which the engine does not replicate (`lm_head_full` writes F32) | 0.999998622 |
| **legal floor for this comparison** | **0.99999862** |

**No step reaches that floor — and for an end-to-end vector it is the wrong floor to judge by.** Distance
from 1, per step: step 1 **1.14e-4 (83× the floor's 1.4e-6)**, then 4.1e-5, 3.7e-5, 3.5e-5, 3.5e-5, 3.4e-5,
3.2e-5 (23–35×) and 4.8e-5 at the last step. The shortfall is **systemic — 23–83× at every step** — with
step 1 about 3.5× above the plateau rather than a lone violator. (This section first read that as "below the
legal floor, therefore a real discrepancy"; that was a single-op floor being used as an end-to-end one, and
it is corrected below the same day rather than left standing.)

What 0.99999862 *is* a floor for, precisely: **reassociation-only change at one op**, given the reference's
own `L23.s_out`. It is not a floor for a token-level logits vector produced by a different whole-network
implementation, because it holds everything upstream fixed by construction — which is also exactly what makes
it worth having. It kills summation order as an explanation (two legal orders of the same products differ by
~1e-9: every BF16 value is exact in F32 and a 1024-term F32 sum is short), and it says that if the engine
replicated the pin's BF16 output rounding and nothing else differed, no step could sit below 0.99999862.
Every step does, so the difference is upstream of the last op at every step — which is what needed knowing.

The floor the end-to-end comparison actually needs was then measured **inside the reference, with no engine
and no device**: `engine/route_price.py` (~90 s torch CPU) runs the dec8 prompt and its 8 greedy continuation
tokens twice through `specimpl`, once with the chunked GDN prefill (§04.7, the form G3 canonicalises) and
once with the recurrent form (§04.4 — the form an engine with no prefill necessarily takes, since it fed the
prompt one token per decode step), same weights, same tokens, compared with `gate.py::metrics`' F64 cos:

| step | 1 − cos, route vs route | engine's own 1 − cos against the pin |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **1.900e-04** | 1.14e-4 |
| 2–6 | 4.73e-05 … 6.99e-05 | 3.4e-5 … 4.1e-5 |
| 7–8 | 1.025e-04, 1.080e-04 | 3.2e-5, 4.8e-5 |

**The reference's own two legal routes are further apart from each other than the engine is from the
reference, at every step**, and the route spread peaks at step 1 — the same shape the engine shows. All eight
argmaxes agree between the two routes, so this is a numerically visible, token-identical difference of exactly
the kind G7 exists to tolerate. That retires the "small real discrepancy" reading of step 1: the deficit sits
*inside* the band the spec leaves open, and no bug is needed to account for it.

What it does not do is prove the engine's difference *is* only the route — the route spread is a lower bound
on what a legal reimplementation may be doing, not an identity. Separating any remainder still wants the
engine's stage dumps against the per-step pins: `decode_run --dump-stages` already writes
`norm_out`/`q_rope`/`k_rope`/`gate_out`, `speccheck/specimpl.py::stage_pairs` already names the pins, and
`engine/gen_watch.py --all` is already the comparator; nothing currently connects the first to the last.

Bands unchanged either way — and this exchange is the argument for leaving them alone: a per-stage bar applied
to an end-to-end quantity produced a non-failing flag that measurement then put inside the legal band.

## (2) The first throughput row, and the ~4 ms between it and the op-sum

Cited from `bench/performance-status.md` §6, which carries its provenance (three runs, blocking calls,
zero subagents, spread **2.1 %**, `util` 76/71/69 % recorded and discounted per §6): **11.978 / 12.053 /
12.232 ms/step = 83.5 / 82.9 / 81.7 tok/s**, over 81 steps each — `RESULT steps=81`, the 17-token prompt
fed one token per step plus 64 requested generation steps, generation ending when the engine emitted id
248046 at generation step 54. Against the op-sum ledger (**8.03 ms**, 7.975 before the `gdn_decode`
re-take) the gap is **3.95 ms**.

Framed as bandwidth, which is the honest frame: one token reads ≈1.608 GB (1.5457 GB weights + 0.0566 GB
of GDN state pass + ~6 MB of activations/copies). The engine sustains **134 GB/s = 57 % of the 237 GB/s
probe**; the sum of its parts implies **200 GB/s = 84 %**. The gap is worth 0.94 GB at probe rate — about
60 % of the checkpoint, read once more and not seen.

### The part of the gap that is plan structure — priced, not guessed

The ledger's 8.03 ms is a sum of per-family cold bursts, each measured on **its own** launch plan. Pricing
the plan `decode_step` actually runs (424 launches, cited per row) accounts for **1.27 ms** of the gap:

| item | ledger §6 bills | the engine pays | Δ | basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| projections | 3.039 ms — the **packed 48-launch** plan at rows/block 8 | **3.600 ms** — 114 launches, unpacked (`PACK_X=false`), rows/block 8 | **+0.56** | `prototype/proj_gemv/results.md` per-shape cold table summed over the engine's plan (3.541 ms), scaled by the 1.0166 clean/contended ratio §6 measures on the same plan |
| cast launches | **not billed at all** | **0.431 ms** — 115 launches × 3.75 µs | **+0.43** | 3.75 µs/launch is glue's measured cost for one 256-thread block (glue §4; §6's re-take reads 2.545 µs at the same width, which would price it at 0.293 ms — this is the least certain row in the table, and §6 already says dispatch-bound numbers here are bimodal). Their traffic is 1.74 MB = 0.007 ms, so this row is dispatch and nothing else |
| `gdn_conv` 18 + `gdn_gates` 18 | not billed | ≈0.130 ms | +0.13 | **estimate** — 5 µs/launch for a 24-block conv, 2.2 µs for `gates_kernel<<<1,16>>>`. Neither has a measured per-launch cost anywhere in this repo |
| the 19 D2D copies (embedding row + 18 conv-window rolls) | not billed | ≈0.057 ms | +0.06 | **estimate** at 3 µs/copy; the conv rolls alone are 1.33 MB/token of traffic, which is nothing, so this is dispatch |
| GDN recurrence | 0.0646 ms — the prototype's **one-launch 18-layer** form | 0.115 ms — 18 separate launches × the re-taken 6.4 µs | +0.05 | §6's `rec alone 0.0064 ms` |
| glue | 0.150 ms @512 threads | 0.185 ms @256 (the width `kernels_glue.cpp` launches) | +0.035 | §6 |
| **GPU side, priced** | **8.030** | **9.295** | **+1.27** | |

Then the host tail, which is serial with the GPU because the step syncs once at the logits, measured on
CPU (no device) at the arithmetic `decode_step` does: fresh `vector<float>(248320)` + first touch + the
strict-`>` argmax = **0.128 ms/step**, of which the warm scan alone is 0.121 ms and the allocation plus
page faults only 0.007 ms; plus **0.014–0.016 ms** for the 0.993 MB D2H at the doc's bulk 63–70 GB/s;
plus **0.254 ms** if the run writes the per-step logits dump (`--out`). So **0.14 ms always, 0.40 ms in a
gate-shaped run**. Total accounted for: **≈1.4–1.7 ms of 3.95**.

### The ~2.5 ms no existing measurement can attribute, and what would settle it

That residue is 21 % of the token, and it is the finding: **the sum of the parts is not the whole on this
box, and nothing in `bench/` can say why.** The candidates, in the order I would test them:

1. **Per-layer serialization between the wide GEMVs and the small kernels.** Every launch in a token
   depends on the one before it, and 237 of the 424 are short (49 glue launches of *one block each*, 115
   casts, 18 `<<<1,16>>>` gates kernels, 19 memcpys, 36 16-row `in_proj_a`/`_b` GEMVs). In a family burst
   those launches queue behind each other with a deep pipeline; in the engine each one is a rendezvous
   between two bandwidth-bound kernels that have to drain around it. At ~3.75 µs each they cost 0.89 ms —
   priced above. At ~10 µs of exposed bubble each they cost 2.4 ms, which is the residue. Nothing on this
   box distinguishes those two numbers today.
   *Confirm with:* per-family clocks inside `decode_step` — a `--time-stages` flag that syncs and reads
   `steady_clock` at the family boundaries and prints ms/token per family from inside a real step. That
   instrument does not exist; it is ~40 lines and it is the one thing that would turn this list into a
   table. It is not written here because it inserts syncs into the path it would be measuring, which is a
   trade-off for the owner to accept, not for a worker to assume.
2. **The GDN state stops being cache-resident.** The ledger bills the 56.6 MB state pass at the re-taken
   warm figure (876 GB/s, 0.0646 ms) because a burst touching 18 MiB repeatedly keeps it resident. In the
   engine, 1.55 GB of weight traffic streams past between two touches of that state. Priced at the
   prototype's own cold row (255 GB/s) the same bytes cost 0.222 ms: **+0.16 ms**, inside the residue.
   *Confirm with:* the same stage clocks — the 18 `rec` launches measured inside a step against the 0.115
   ms the launch-split arithmetic predicts.
3. **Interleaving cost per se** (DRAM page locality, TLB walks across 335 allocations in a per-layer order
   no prototype reproduces). Unquantified; it is what's left if 1 and 2 come back clean. The same stage
   clocks would show it as the three big families each running slower inside the engine than in their own
   burst, with no change in their launch counts.

### Levers, ranked by expected ms/token

| # | lever | expected | bit-identical? | how to confirm |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **`proj` rows/block 8 → 32** — all 114 engine projections launch at 256 threads (`kRowsPerBlock = 8` × 32 lanes), the width the §4 curve prices at 3.75 µs against 2.776 µs at 1024, and the block-starved narrow shapes are the ones that care | **−0.50 ms** — a clean-window pair on the same 48-job plan: 3.039 ms at rpb 8 (§6) vs 2.534 ms at rpb 32 (E2's 0.0528 ms/launch × 48, same window as the engine's 11.978) | **yes, by construction**: one wavefront owns a row (`proj_gemv_kernels.h:116-133` — `row = blockIdx.x*rpb + threadIdx.x/ws`, the K loop strides by lane, `reduce_row` is a within-warp butterfly), so `rpb` moves rows per block and nothing that touches a value | re-take the rpb ladder in one clean window, then A/B the engine (commands below). It is one constant in `engine/kernels_proj.cpp`, and it is a decision, not a fix: 8 is the op's gated default |
| 2 | **Pack the GDN input projections** — `in_proj_qkv` + `z` + `b` + `a` in one launch per GDN layer, −54 launches. The deferral in this file priced it at ≈0.10 ms from launch count; the per-shape table prices the *same bytes* 4× faster merged | **−0.11 ms** (the `b`/`a` fold, as `proj_gemv` measured it) to **−0.57 ms** (the full four-into-one; cross-`tile` comparison, so treat as the upper bound) | same kernel, fewer launches — the outputs are the same dots | `prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --layers all --steps all --time --sweep`; the gap between `PACK_inproj` (181.9 GB/s) and the four separate shapes (141 GB/s) *is* the price |
| 3 | **The 115 cast launches** — upper bound −0.43 ms, and the cheap deletion is not the one this file has been carrying. Folding the widening into the *producer* (`glue_add_norm` holds the norm row in registers and could also write the F32 copy for a 4 KB store) removes 49 of them for near-zero marginal traffic. Folding them into the *consumer*, which is the old `mlp_gateup`/`mlp_down` plan, has a measured precedent against it: `PACK_X` cost **+0.278 ms** over 48 launches to save 48 casts | −0.18 … −0.43 ms, and plausibly net-negative if done the consumer way | yes (every BF16 is exact in F32) | anchor the µs/launch at 256 threads (command below), then decide which producer to touch — both are graduated kernels, so both cost a re-gate |
| 4 | **The per-step logits tail** — 0.128 ms of host scan/alloc + 0.015 ms D2H, always serial with the GPU, plus 0.254 ms when a run dumps logits. The sync itself is **not** the lever: §03.4's lowest-id tie rule put the argmax on the host, and a tie-correct fused argmax would shrink the D2H from 0.993 MB to 4 B but could not remove the wait | −0.14 … −0.40 ms | n/a — no value changes | A/B `decode_run` with and without `--out` (command below); the CPU pieces are already measured above |
| 5 | ~~HIP graphs~~ | **−0.06 ms** | — | **retired by measurement**: §1's probe replays 64 captured launches at 1.78 µs against 1.85 µs eager. This file's earlier "graphs are the first perf item" line is superseded (note added there) |
| — | per-step allocation churn | −0.007 ms | — | measured; not a lever |
| — | stage dumps written every step | −0.254 ms, **only** when `--out` is passed | — | measured on CPU; a serving loop never pays it, so this is a note about how the throughput figure was taken, not an engine item |

**So: the biggest single lever is neither the casts nor the per-step sync. It is the 114 projections at 256
threads** — with the packed-plan fold immediately behind it, the two together worth −0.6 … −1.1 ms, i.e. as
much of the gap as everything else combined. The casts are an upper bound with a measured counter-example,
and the sync is 1.3 % of the step. The biggest *unknown* is the 2.5 ms residue, and it needs one instrument,
not one more measurement of a family in isolation.

**Commands for the top three** (operator-run; a worker may not open a GPU window):

```bash
# 1 · the projections' block width, and the packing question, in one clean window.
.venv/bin/python prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --layers all --steps all --time --sweep

# 2 · the engine A/B once `engine/kernels_proj.cpp`'s kRowsPerBlock says 32 (bit-identical per the
#     kernel geometry above; the proj gate should still be re-run because it owns the default).
#     Same shape as the throughput row: 17 prompt tokens one per step, then 64 generation steps.
IDS=248045,846,198,760,6511,314,9338,369,248046,198,248045,74455,198,248068,271,248069,271
./engine/build/decode_run --ids "$IDS" --steps 64            # no --out: driver's dump excluded
./engine/build/decode_run --ids "$IDS" --steps 64 --out tmp/engine_gate_nodump   # with it

# 3 · the dispatch anchor every small-kernel row above is priced from, at the widths the engine uses
#     (256 threads: glue, casts, the narrow projections; 1024 is the width glue prefers).
.venv/bin/python prototype/glue/run.py --time --threads 256 --iters 300
.venv/bin/python prototype/glue/run.py --time --threads 1024 --iters 300
```

### Q6 (2026-10-13): the 424-launch bill, priced at the widths each site actually uses

The question this section owes `bench/plan-2026-10-12.md` Q6 is whether the step is launch-bound:
**12.949 ms/token at 128 lanes against a 6.52 ms floor for 1.5457 GB of weights at the 237 GB/s probe**
(all three figures are §6's, and the effective rate is 124 GB/s — half the probe). Everything below is
either a §6 figure or arithmetic on one, and each row says which. Nothing here is a new device timing:
the only device work in this pass was correctness-shaped (launch counters, byte-identity, the eight
fixtures), and the by-product `ms_per_step` that `decode_run` prints in those runs is not quoted.

First, what does *not* explain it, because the arithmetic is quick. At 128 lanes the §05 attention read
is 128 × 12 KiB = 1.5 MB, which is 0.006 ms at the probe. The 18.66 MiB of per-request state is per
request, not per token. The per-token GDN state pass is 0.054 GB = 0.228 ms of cold roofline, of which
this file already priced +0.16 ms as candidate 2 above. Sum: 0.23 ms of a 6.43 ms gap.

**The observed bill, not the planned one.** Two `decode_run` runs on a one-token prompt (`--steps 2` and
`--steps 6`, `LAUNCHSTAT` differenced and divided by 4, so the prefill body and the `finish_token` tail
cancel), lock `q6-launchcount`, `util` 94 % recorded and discounted, no timing taken:

| family | launches per one-row step | block shape it launches | where the count comes from |
|---|---|---|---|
| `glue` | 49 | 1 block × 256 t | counter: `calls` 147 → 343 over 4 steps |
| `proj` | 114 | `ceil(rows/8)` blocks × 256 t (now 32 / 1024 t, below) | counter: 342 → 798 |
| `gdn` | 72 = conv 18 + gates 18 + rec 18 + gated_norm 18 | conv `dim3(24,1)` × 256 t; gates 1 × 16 t; rec 16 × 128 t; gated_norm 16 × 128 t | counters: 54 of the 72 — `rec` has no token width and is not a counted family (`engine/launch_stats.h`) |
| `attn` | 6 | 8 blocks × 256 t, one fused site per attention layer (`split = 1` under `MAXSC`) | counter: 18 → 42 |
| `mlp` | 48 = 24 gate_up + 24 down | 448 and 128 blocks × 256 t | counters: 72 → 168 each |
| `cast` | 115 = 5 per GDN layer + 4 per attention layer + 1 final norm | ≤ 14 blocks × 256 t | `LAUNCH` plan line only — casts are not a launchstats family |
| `other` | 20 = 1 embedding memcpy + 18 conv-window memcpys + 1 `lm_head` | — | plan line |
| **total** | **424** | | matches `planned_launch_counts()` and `gate.py` section (d) |

Now price it with §6's width curve — 2.776 / 3.06 / 3.75 / 5.30 µs per launch at 1024 / 512 / 256 / 128
threads (`prototype/glue/results.md` §4), each family at the width its own launcher uses, which is the
correction this task was asked for rather than a single guessed µs/launch:

| family | launches | width | dispatch bill (derived) | §6 measured cost | roofline at 237 GB/s | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lm_head` | 1 | 256 t | 0.004 | **2.127** | 2.146 | at the ceiling (101 %). No lever |
| MLP | 48 | 256 t | 0.180 | **2.594** | 2.230 | traffic-bound at 86 %; its +0.36 over roofline is kernel efficiency, not dispatch |
| projections, engine's 114-launch plan | 114 | 256 t → 1024 t | 0.428 → 0.317 | not measured as the engine runs it; the 48-launch packed plan is **3.081**, and the per-shape table summed over this plan gives 3.54–3.60 (derived, §6's 1.0166 clean/contended ratio applied) | 1.973 | **the biggest named inefficiency: +1.6 ms over its own roofline**, and it is a shape problem — `in_proj_a`/`_b` 5 %, `k_proj`/`v_proj` 40 %, `o_proj`/`out_proj` 46–49 % of the probe |
| `glue` | 49 | 256 t, one block each | 0.184 | **0.1904** | 0.002 | pure dispatch — and the only family where the curve can be checked against itself: derived 0.184 against measured 0.190, 3 % apart |
| casts | 115 | 256 t | 0.431 (0.293 if §6's 2.545 µs re-take is the anchor) | never measured | 0.007 | dispatch and nothing else, whatever the anchor |
| GDN recurrence | 18 | 16 blocks × 128 t | 0.095 | 0.0646 for the prototype's one-launch form; 0.115 as 18 launches (derived, 18 × the re-taken 6.4 µs) | 0.228 cold | the warm-burst figure understates an in-step cold pass; priced at +0.16 above |
| §04.5 gated norm | 18 | 16 × 128 t | 0.095 | **0.053** | 0.0016 | pure dispatch — and the curve's weak spot: it prices this launch at 5.30 µs while the kernel's own measured cost is 2.958 µs, i.e. the curve over-prices small multi-block launches by up to 1.8× |
| `gdn_conv` + `gdn_gates` | 36 | 256 t / 1 block × 16 t | ≈0.13 | never measured | ~0.01 | dispatch (the estimate this file already carried) |
| attention | 6 | 8 blocks × 256 t | 0.023 | 0.114 at 128 lanes, derived from the re-taken 0.89 µs per lane per token (at the older ≤327-lane fit of 2.014 µs/lane the same 128 lanes price at 0.258) | 0.006 | latency-bound at low lane counts |
| 19 D2D copies | 19 | — | ≈0.057 | never measured | 0.006 | dispatch |
| host tail (argmax + alloc + D2H) | — | — | — | 0.14 (CPU-measured) | — | serial with the GPU, always paid |
| **sum of the rows** | **424** | | **≈1.63 ms of dispatch** | **≈9.35–9.55 ms** | 6.52 | |

**So the honest answer to Q6.1/Q6.2 is: launch overhead is not the gap.** All 424 launches, priced at
the width each one uses, are ≈1.63 ms of a 12.949 ms token — 12.6 % — and most of that is already
inside a §6 family cost (glue's 0.190 *is* its dispatch bill; so is gated norm's 0.053). The
unaccounted launch items are the casts and the copies, ≈0.35–0.49 ms. The width curve is also biased
high on the small multi-block launches, as its own `gdn_gated_norm` comparison shows, so the true bill
is nearer 1.3 ms than 1.6.

What the sum of the parts leaves over is **≈3.4–3.6 ms, 26–28 % of the token** — or ≈2.4–2.6 ms of
*context-free* step if it is charged against §6's own fit (intercept 11.924 ms at zero context), which
is the figure this file earlier called "the ~2.5 ms no existing measurement can attribute". Same residue,
two framings. And this file's two candidate explanations above (an exposed bubble at each of the 237
short launches, and state losing cache residency) are the only ones on the table. Note what that now
implies: a bubble theory has to produce ≈8 µs of exposed stall per short launch to cover 3.4 ms, while
the same launches price at 2.9–3.8 µs each when they are merely *queued*. That is a step-shape question,
and the family bursts cannot answer it in either direction — which is what makes Q6.1's one full-plan
burst (424 launches in dependency order, one sync) the cheapest number that changes strategy. It is a
timing instrument that inserts a sync per stage or replaces the step, so it stays the main agent's to
build.

**What shipped from this pass: `proj` rows/block 8 → 32** (`engine/kernels_proj.cpp`, commit
`engine/proj: launch at rows/block 32 …`). It is lever 1 above, and it is the only item in this file's
lever list that is bit-identical *and* has a measured basis — `prototype/proj_gemv/results.md` swept
2/4/8/16/32 on the cold plan and got 2.991 ms at 8 against 2.785 ms at 32, then left the default alone
because "moving it is a separate, measurable decision". Proof on device, all eight fixtures, old binary
against new: identical `LAUNCHSTAT` counters, `dec8` at 64 steps byte-identical across all 40 output
files, `gate.py` check counts and failures identical (dec8 168/0 · dec64f 650/0 · dec64t 647/0 ·
dec300f 1156/0 · mt32f 391/0 · p128 197/0 · wide520f 327/0 · chunk64b 372 PASS with the same three
D-9 red lines), and `tools/margin_delta.py` **x1.0000** on every column of every fixture, including
`dec300f` step 86 (0.9992 filled) and `wide520f` step 17 (0.9996). And because the engine now runs a
width that op's gate had never been run at, the gate ran there too: `prototype/proj_gemv/run.py
--fixture dec8 --layers all --steps decode0` gives **114 comparisons, 0 failures at `--rows 32`** and
the same 114 / 0 at `--rows 8` — one verdict at both widths, which is that gate saying out loud that
the width is not what decides the values. The honest caveat: §6 also records
that the 71 %-of-probe rows/block-32 figure "never reproduced clean", and E2's same-window numbers
(65.1 % at rpb 32) sit level with the 63–65 % this family produces at rpb 8 — so the −0.50 ms priced
above may be noise, and the A/B in the command list below decides it. If it is slower, the revert is
one constant (D-9).

**Deliberately not changed, and why** (each of these is *not* free, which is the bar):

- **`glue` 256 → 1024 threads** (≈−0.05 ms by the width curve). Thread count divides `PER = H/THREADS`
  and therefore reorders the norm's F32 mean-of-squares, so it is not bit-identical: the raw ndiff
  against the pins differs with the width (2 of 384 norm rows at 256 t, 1 at 1024 t — E1, §6), both
  inside band. Moving bits for 0.4 % of the token, with `dec300f` step 86 at 0.9992 and `wide520f`
  step 17 at 0.9996 filled, is a margin re-price, not a free win.
- **Folding the casts into a producer** (49 of 115, −0.18 … −0.43 ms). `glue_add_norm` holds the norm
  row in registers and could write the F32 copy for a 4 KB store, but `prototype/glue/glue_kernels.h`
  is graduated code and editing it costs that op's device gate (≈2 min — outside a worker's allowance,
  so it is written here rather than done). The consumer side already has a measured counter-example:
  `PACK_X` cost **+0.278 ms** over 48 launches to save 48 casts.
- **Packed multi-weight projections** (114 → 48 launches, −0.11 … −0.57 ms). The packed form writes
  one `[tokens, 8224]` tile, so `gdn_conv`, `gdn_gates` and the cast sites would each need a row-stride
  argument — and at `tokens = 1` the stride happens to collapse, which is exactly how a second, only-
  ever-decode-tested code path gets born (P4's two-branch launcher is the cautionary precedent).
  This is a plan change with a kernel-signature change and its own gate, not a constant.
- **`mlp` rows/block**: 48 launches of 448/128 blocks in a family measured at 86 % of the probe, with
  0.18 ms of dispatch inside 2.594 ms. Neither launch count nor traffic justifies touching it, and its
  width belongs to that op's gated sweep — a width change has to be justified by launch count or
  traffic, not by the fact that a knob exists.
- **The 19 conv-window memcpys** (≈0.06 ms): batching them means changing §08 conv-window state
  semantics for a rounding error of a saving.

### Commands for the main agent, in the order that changes strategy

Every line is a timing or a ≥1-minute device run, so none of them was run in this pass. `prototype/glue/run.py`,
`prototype/proj_gemv/run.py` and `prototype/gdn_gated_norm/run.py` **take the lock themselves** — do not
hold it across them (AGENTS.md trap row).

```bash
# 0 · A/B the width change on the same day, before anything else. `bc61dce` is the width commit, so
#     the before-binary is one rebuild away and the tree is put back by the same four lines
#     (`git diff --stat` must be empty afterwards). Both sweeps take --binary, and the WAVE path
#     changed width too, so prefill has to be re-taken in the same window as decode.
mkdir -p tmp/q6
git show bc61dce~1:engine/kernels_proj.cpp > tmp/q6/proj_rpb8.cpp
cp engine/kernels_proj.cpp tmp/q6/proj_rpb32.cpp
cp tmp/q6/proj_rpb8.cpp  engine/kernels_proj.cpp && engine/build.sh && cp engine/build/decode_run tmp/q6/decode_run_rpb8
cp tmp/q6/proj_rpb32.cpp engine/kernels_proj.cpp && engine/build.sh
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q6-rpb-ab && tools/gpu_lock.sh util
.venv/bin/python tools/context_sweep.py  --binary tmp/q6/decode_run_rpb8  --lanes 128,512,1024,2048,4032 --steps 32 --runs 3
.venv/bin/python tools/context_sweep.py  --binary engine/build/decode_run --lanes 128,512,1024,2048,4032 --steps 32 --runs 3
.venv/bin/python tools/prefill_sweep.py  --binary tmp/q6/decode_run_rpb8  --lanes 64,256,1024,3072 --runs 3
.venv/bin/python tools/prefill_sweep.py  --binary engine/build/decode_run --lanes 64,256,1024,3072 --runs 3
tools/gpu_lock.sh release q6-rpb-ab

# 1 · the 4032-lane point, which §6 quotes at a 37.8 % spread and a median. Five runs, stated as
#     median + spread again; if it stays bimodal the split attention path at 8 blocks/site is the
#     suspect, not the memory system.
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q6-4032 && .venv/bin/python tools/context_sweep.py --lanes 4032 --steps 32 --runs 5 && tools/gpu_lock.sh release q6-4032

# 2 · ledger §2's two "not measured on their own" rows, now selectable (this commit's --only).
#     GDN layers are 81 % of the projection bytes and own the 5 %-efficient shapes; if the GDN row
#     comes back at the family's 63 % and attention at 85 %, the narrow-shape work has a target.
#     run.py takes the lock itself.
.venv/bin/python prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --layers all --steps all --time --only gdn
.venv/bin/python prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --layers all --steps all --time --only attn

# 3 · the rows/block ladder in ONE clean window, which is the only way to settle whether the -0.50 ms
#     above is real: the 8-vs-32 pair has so far only ever been compared across windows.
.venv/bin/python prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --layers all --steps all --time --sweep --rows 8

# 4 · the dispatch anchor the small-kernel rows are priced from, at the two widths the engine now
#     uses. This is the number that would replace the curve's 1.8× over-price on small launches.
.venv/bin/python prototype/glue/run.py --time --threads 256  --iters 300
.venv/bin/python prototype/glue/run.py --time --threads 1024 --iters 300

# 5 · the correctness re-runs on the committed tree, since the engine now ships a width that op's gate
#     defaulted away. Both were run in this pass (all-24-layer `--rows 32` and `--rows 8`: 114/0 each;
#     the negatives and the eight engine fixtures as counted above, x1.0000 margins); they are here so
#     the tree is re-checkable by anyone, and `--steps all` makes the first line the full 912-comparison
#     gate rather than the 114-comparison slice this pass had time for.
.venv/bin/python prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --layers all --steps all --negative
.venv/bin/python prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --fixture dec8 --layers all --steps all --rows 32

# 6 · Q6.1, the full-plan burst against the engine's own step: BUILT (see the Q6.1 section below).
#     `decode_run --time-stages` is that experiment and one more besides — it prices the same launches
#     at burst, per-stage and per-kernel submission in one process, with the per-family clock inside
#     `decode_step` that this line asked for if the burst came back under the step.
```

### Q6.1 (2026-10-13): the stage clock exists — what it measures, and the one experiment to run

A worker's shift, and it took no timings: there is no millisecond from this pass anywhere in this
section, in either direction. What shipped is the instrument the Q6 table above named as "the cheapest
number that changes strategy" — `engine/stage_clock.{h,cpp}`, the `decode_step.cpp` boundary markers, and
`decode_run --time-stages` — plus the experiment specification and the command list at the bottom. Every
number it can print belongs to a clean window, which means the main agent's.

**Why a family burst cannot answer the question, which is why this had to be built.** The residue is
≈3.4–3.6 ms of a 12.949 ms token (§6's differential row), and the two candidate stories need opposite
fixes: exposed submission latency (fix: fewer, wider launches, or a graph) versus kernels that are
simply slower in situ than their isolated bursts said (fix: memory behaviour, and no submission work at
all). No per-family burst can see the first story in either direction, because a burst submits the same
kernel hundreds of times back to back, so *its* queue is never empty and an exposed stall can never
appear in it. The only measurement that separates them is the real step at different submission
granularities.

**What it measures.** One process, three extra passes over the run's own generation steps, replayed from
a rebuilt state (§08.4 reset + the same prompt wave) with the *same* fed tokens, so the three passes price
identical work at the same lanes and the only difference between them is how often the queue is drained:

| `gran=` | what it is | what its number is allowed to be called |
|---|---|---|
| `burst` | the production shape: every launch of the step submitted to one stream, one wait (the logits D2H in `finish_token`) | the ledger's ms/token, warm |
| `stage` | the same launches, drained at every family boundary (323 waits per step, measured on `dec8`) | the sum of the stages, each measured alone |
| `kernel` | the same launches drained after every submission (425 waits per step) | the per-launch in-situ cost |

plus `calib`, which prices the instrument's own wait (`idle_sync_us`, an idle `hipDeviceSynchronize`
budged over 200 of them). Timings are `steady_clock` around a real wait, never events —
`prototype/README.md` rule 7: `hipEventElapsedTime` returns exactly 0 below ~1 ms on this box, so a
per-kernel event timing would report a column of zeros and look like fast kernels.

The stages are the families the launch bill already names, so every row can be set against a row of the
Q6 table: `glue` `proj` `gdn` `attn` `mlp` `cast` `copy` `lm_head` `host_tail`. And the clock *counts*
submissions as well as timing them, so it audits its own segmentation against the gate-policed plan:
`launches_per_step=424 plan_per_step=424 plan_match=1` with the family counts matching §07 line for line
(glue 49, proj 114, gdn 72, attn 6, mlp 48, cast 115, copy 19, lm_head 1, host_tail 0 — the last is the
D2H plus the argmax, which is not a launch). Above the attention lane staging an `attn` site legitimately
issues 2 kernels where the plan says 1, and the totals then exceed the plan and say so rather than
quietly agreeing.

Lines it prints, in one go: `STAGEPASS gran=… steps=… ms_mean=… ms_min=… ms_max=… spread_pct=…`,
`STAGEPARITY gran=… tokens_identical=… logits_bitidentical=… first_mismatch=…`, one
`STAGE gran=… family=… ms_per_step=… ms_total=… launches_per_step=… segments_per_step=… us_per_launch=…`
per family that ran, a `STAGE … TOTAL` line, `STAGESTEP gran=… step=… ms=…` per step, and for each
serialized granularity
`STAGEGAP gran=… burst_ms_mean=… serialized_ms_mean=… exposed_ms=… ratio=…`.
`STAGEPARITY` is the load-bearing one: each pass must reproduce the run's sampled ids and the bit
pattern of each step's top logit, or it prints `note=COMPARISON_VOID` and the gap numbers are meaningless.

**The arithmetic that makes it a discriminator.** In the serialized passes a launch costs its host
dispatch *plus* its execution *plus* the wait, so with N = 424 launches, per-launch dispatch h (§6's width
curve, ≈2.9–3.8 µs, total **D ≈ 1.63 ms** per Q6's own table) and idle-wait cost s (the `calib` line):

    K ≈ Σ exec_in_situ + D + N·s          S ≈ Σ exec_in_situ + D_stages + n_stages·s
    B ≈ Σ exec_in_situ + (whatever dispatch the step fails to hide)

so, for the per-kernel pass,

    K − B ≈ D + N·s − (dispatch hidden by the production step).

That gives two independent readings, which should agree:

1. **`exposed` (the `STAGEGAP` line).** If `K − B` comes back near `D + N·s` (≈1.6–2.5 ms at the widths
   the sites use), the production step was already hiding dispatch behind execution, so there is nothing
   submission-side left to win and the residue is inside the kernels. If `K − B ≈ 0`, the queue is already
   empty in the real step: every launch pays its dispatch in the open and the fix is submission-side.
2. **In-situ execution vs the isolated sum.**
   `Σ exec_in_situ ≈ K − (D + N·s)` compared against Q6's ≈9.35–9.55 ms of §6-measured family cost. A
   match means the kernels are *not* slower in situ and the residue is dispatch or the never-measured
   families; a match with B (≈12.9) instead means they are, by about the size of the residue. Read this one
   with the asymmetry that makes it informative rather than alarming: those §6 numbers came from **isolated
   warm bursts**, and this repo already knows both ways that misprices an in-step pass — the dispatch curve
   over-prices small multi-block launches by up to **1.8×** (the gated-norm row: curve 5.30 µs against the
   kernel's own 2.958 µs), and a warm burst *understates* an in-step cold pass (the GDN recurrence row, which
   is deliberately priced +0.16 above its warm figure). So `Σ exec_in_situ` landing *above* 9.55 ms is partly
   expected in-situ cost, not a new leak, and the excess has to be attributed family by family before it is
   called a finding.

   The polarity in (1) and (2) is worth stating plainly because the first sketch of this experiment, in
   `bench/plan-2026-10-12.md` Q6 item 1, had it **inverted** ("burst ≈ sum of stages ⇒ the residue is inside
   the kernels; burst ≪ sum ⇒ the stall is exposed, fix submission-side"). Caught before the run, agreed,
   and corrected in place there and in `stage_clock.h`'s header; §07.4 is the precedent for correcting a wrong
   claim where it stood rather than leaving it and dissenting downstream. The instrument is neutral between the
   two readings — that is its whole design — so nothing about the measurement depends on which one wins.

**Pre-registered predictions, written before the window.** So that the answer cannot be fitted to the
number afterwards. Anchors, all from this repo: N = 424 launches; E_iso = 9.35–9.55 ms (Q6's own column
sum); **D = 1.1–1.63 ms** — 1.63 is Q6's dispatch column as written, and 1.1 is the same column with the
small-multi-block rows divided by the curve's own 1.8× over-price, which is the honest lower end; s = the
`STAGECAL idle_sync_us` line the same window prints; B must land on §6's 12.7–13.0 ms or nothing below
means anything.

| quantity | story A (dispatch exposed) | story B (kernels slow in situ) | mixed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `exposed = K − B` | ≈ `N·s` only → **≤0.2 ms** if the calib line reads sub-µs, ≤1.3 ms if it reads 3 µs | ≈ `D + N·s` → **1.1–1.7 ms** plus `N·s` | between the two bands |
| `Σ exec_in_situ = K − (D + N·s)` | ≈ E_iso, 9.4–9.6 | ≈ B, **12.7–13.0** | between |
| `K` itself | ≈ B + `N·s` → **12.8–14.3** | ≈ B + `D` + `N·s` → **13.9–16.0** | between |
| consequence | fund submission work first (fold the 115 casts, pack the 114 projections) | stop submission work; fund shape/memory (Q6 lever 4) and the two unpriced families | **report it as a split, never as the nearer story** |

And the split, if it lands in the middle — which the arithmetic above makes the *likely* outcome, because
`D ≤ 1.63 ms < 3.4 ms`, so **no outcome of this experiment can make the residue entirely a dispatch
story**: that is a pre-registered conclusion, independent of the measurement. Compute both halves from the
same log and report both:

    exposed_dispatch = B − (K − (D + N·s))        # what the step failed to hide
    in_kernel        = B − exposed_dispatch − E_iso   # everything else, incl. cast/copy never priced
    identity:  B − E_iso ≈ exposed_dispatch + in_kernel   # must hold to ~noise, else the model is wrong

Two sub-cases the family rows decide and the aggregate cannot, also pre-registered now:

- **B1 vs B2.** If story B wins, the `in_kernel` excess must show up *inside the named families* (their
  `stage`-granularity rows exceeding their §6 burst rows). If instead it sits in `cast` and `copy` — the two
  rows Q6 marks "never measured", ≈0.43 + ≈0.06 ms of dispatch by Q6's own estimate — then nothing got
  slower in situ; two families were simply never billed, and the fix is folding casts into producers, which is
  Q6 lever 2 and *is* submission-side. Same headline story, opposite lever, and only the rows can tell them.
- **Contention manufactures story B.** A sync in a contended window waits for somebody else's work, so
  `K − B` inflates in exactly the direction story B predicts. A window with any contender — including this
  session's own serving — does not merely add noise to this experiment, it produces the story-B answer as an
  artifact. That is why every number here is the main agent's. A contended worker smoke pass of this same
  three-granularity comparison did in fact show the serialized totals well above the burst total; it is
  deliberately not recorded, and anyone tempted to read it as a preliminary result should note it is the one
  direction contention can *create*.

**The experiment, and the if/then table.** One locked window, a fixed 128-lane context (cheapest point
that is pure one-row decode: under the 512-lane staging the attention site is one fused kernel and the
plan is exactly 424 launches), three runs, medians and spread quoted the way §6 quotes everything. The
4032-lane point goes in the same window because that is the one §6 could not settle (16.9 % spread over
five runs, and more repetitions did not move it).

| outcome (128-lane point, medians of 3) | reading | what to do |
|---|---|---|
| `K−B` within ~±0.4 ms of `D + N·s`, and `Σ exec_in_situ` ≈ B (12.7–13.0) | dispatch is fully hidden; the step's own kernels cost ~3.2–3.7 ms more in situ than the isolated bursts said | **Story B. Stop all submission work** (packed projections, cast folding, graphs — §6's graph result of ~4 % is then explained, not a missed opportunity). The next move is the shape/memory work: Q6 lever 4 (the 5 %-efficient `in_proj_a/b`, 40 % `k/v_proj`, 46–49 % `o/out_proj`), and a re-examination of the burst-vs-situ cache asymmetry — `prototype/README.md` rule 8 is the measured case: 6 back-to-back replicas of one kernel re-read cached bytes and reported 344 GB/s for a kernel that streams 237, so an isolated burst whose repetitions touch the same weights is *expected* to flatter the step. **Then split B1/B2 with the family rows before funding anything:** if the excess is inside the named families it is in-situ cost; if it is in `cast`/`copy`, nothing slowed and the lever is folding casts, which is submission work — the opposite instruction. |
| `K−B ≲ 0.3 ms` (sub-µs `s`) | the queue is already empty in production: dispatch is exposed, launch for launch | **Story A, capped at D ≈ 1.1–1.6 ms of the 3.4** — go submission-side in Q6's own order: fold the 49 of 115 casts a producer can write, then packed projections (114 → 48 launches). The remaining ≥1.8 ms is in-kernel by the arithmetic above and needs its own finding; do not spend the fusion work expecting the residue to close. |
| `0.3 ms ≲ K−B ≲ D + N·s` | partly hidden — the expected case, given `D < residue` | Compute both halves (`exposed_dispatch`, `in_kernel`, above) and report the **split**, never the nearer story. Fund whichever half is larger first, and say in the ledger row that the other half exists. |
| `S−B > K−B`, or `plan_match=0`, or any `STAGEPARITY` not `1`, or the split identity off by more than ~0.3 ms | the instrument or the window is broken — per-kernel serialization is a superset of per-stage, so it cannot be the cheaper one, and a failed identity means the three-term model does not describe the step | **Stop and fix the instrument.** Do not interpret any number from that run. |
| any doubt about window exclusivity (`gpu_lock.sh util` high at acquire, a subagent alive, serving in flight) | contention inflates every sync, i.e. it *manufactures* the story-B signature | Void, not noisy. Re-take with zero subagents. This is the one failure mode that fakes the answer rather than blurring it. |
| `burst` does not reproduce §6's 128-lane row (12.7–13.0) within ~5 % | contaminated window, or the warm-pass assumption is wrong | re-take; every serialized number is conditioned on the same warm state as the burst. |
| 4032-lane `spread_pct` large while 128-lane is tight | the bimodality is the split attention path (8 blocks/site), not the memory system | the wide-context story becomes the attention split, and §6's "we do not yet know which" resolves in that direction. |

Also free from the same run: `cast` and `copy` have never been measured at all (Q6's table says "never
measured" for both, ≈0.35–0.49 ms of the residue is theirs by elimination). Their `stage`-granularity rows
price them directly, for the first time, in the same window.

**Third reading — the graph A/B, specified but not built.** A captured step removes the per-launch host cost
without changing one kernel, so it tests "is host submission exposed?" more directly than the drain test
does; and the drain test has a blind spot worth naming — serializing removes *overlap*, it does not add host
work, so any host cost spent building arguments before enqueue is invisible to `K − B` and visible to a graph.
This one was not built, and the reason is budget, not an oversight: capturing the step needs a stream handle
threaded through the launch sites (the engine submits on the default stream today, and stream capture does not
capture the legacy stream), plus an exclusion for the logits D2H + argmax, which are host work in
`finish_token`. That is a second bench change with its own correctness A/B, and the experiment above does not
need it to be decision-grade. It is the next thing to build **if `K − B` lands in the middle band**, because
it is the only reading that can contradict the other two.

What is already on the record, and what it predicts: §1 measured 64 captured launches replaying at **1.78 µs
per kernel against 1.85 µs eager** — no gain, because the limit is the GPU's own dispatch rate — which is why
the plan says a graph around the decode step buys ~4 % of the dispatch bill, ≈0.06 ms. Pre-register against
that: a captured step should win **≈0.06 ms under story B**, and up to **`D` ≈ 1.1–1.6 ms under story A**
(≈9–13 % of the step). If `K − B` says the dispatch was hidden and the graph nonetheless buys ≥5 % of the
step, that contradiction is the result and gets reported as one — the likely reading being host work ahead of
enqueue, which neither of the other two readings can see. Do not quote whichever of the two is cleaner.

**Proof it does not perturb the step.** Two directions, both byte-level, both on device:

- the markers present, clock unarmed: all 50 files of a `dec8` run (32 steps, `--prefill-logits all`,
  `--out`) are byte-identical to the same run on a binary built from `58580fd` — i.e. the parent commit,
  before this file existed — and stdout is identical apart from the two lines that carry by-product ms;
- the flag on versus off: all 50 files byte-identical, and stdout identical once the `STAGE*` lines are
  stripped — including the `LAUNCHSTAT` counters, which is why the extra passes are placed after that
  line is printed (§(h)'s reading of the counters must keep meaning the run alone).

Structurally the off-path claim is one null-pointer read per marker (`stageclock::note` returns immediately
