# D-1 and D-2 of `bench/plan-2026-10-12`: the tie policy keeps to the case it exists for, and the route margin became per-fixture

Two gate-policy decisions the operator recorded in `bench/plan-2026-10-12.md` §3, implemented and re-gated
the same day they were written. **Neither touched one line of the engine**: both are about what the gate is
entitled to call a failure, which is why every number below comes from a gate run or a shipped allowance
file rather than from a kernel change. No timing is quoted anywhere in this section — `decode_run` prints
its own `ms_per_step` and none of it is repeated here, per `AGENTS.md`.

## D-1 — a step where the engine and the pin picked the same id is not a tie case

The shape `mt32f` step 11 had (Q2 section above, the one red line there):

```
pinned max 24.25, payload 0x41c2, shared by ids [4016, 15048]      <- a 2-way exact tie on the BF16 payload
engine picked 4016                                                 <- the PIN'S OWN top-1: G7 satisfied outright
engine's F32 at 4016 = 24.391083 -> bf16 0x41c3 != 0x41c2          <- old clause 3, evaluated at the PIN's top-1
```

So the exception was refusing a step that never asked for an exception, and it was doing it with a half-ULP
test — the very kind of band the policy's own comment says it will not invent. The predicate now has three
outcomes (`tie_verdict` returns a kind), and only the third arms a check:

| kind | when | what the gate does |
|---|---|---|
| `no-tie` | the pin has one winner | nothing; the ordinary id check judges it |
| `match` | the engine's id **is** the pin's top-1 id | prints an `[info]` line naming the tie, counts a `[TIE-NOTE]`, **does not consult the rule** |
| `tie` | the engine picked a different id at a tied step | admissible iff (i) ≥2 ids share the max on the payload, (ii) the engine's id is one of them, (iii) RNE(the engine's F32 **at that id**) == the shared tied payload — else FAIL loudly |

Clause (iii) moved from the pin's top-1 to the engine's own chosen id for the same reason: the exception is
about the id the engine stands on, and all the tied ids share one payload (that is what a payload tie *is*),
so it is still exactly one check, not one per tied id.

**`dec64f` 17/18 still resolve through the tie path** — that is what the policy was built for, and the numbers
at the engine's own id are the ones that now have to hold:

```
step 17: pinned 17.25 / 0x418a tied among [2018, 10407]; engine picked 10407; engine's logit at 10407
         = 17.237261 -> 0x418a == the tied payload     -> ADMITTED
step 18: pinned 19.75 / 0x419e tied among [1379, 64704]; engine picked 64704; engine's logit at 64704
         = 19.785622 -> 0x419e == the tied payload     -> ADMITTED
```

and an engine landing on a third, untied id at either step still fails there. Both directions, plus the new
regression, are selftest injections aimed at a step the fixture really ties at:

```
.venv/bin/python engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture dec64f     # ties at step 17
.venv/bin/python engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture mt32f      # ties at step 11
  [PASS] engine sampled the pin's other tied id at tied step 11 — must be ADMITTED …
  [PASS] engine sampled an UNTIED id at tied step 11 — must fail loudly
  [PASS] engine kept the pin's own top-1 4016 at tied step 11 with its logit one BF16 ULP above the tied
         payload (the mt32f step 11 shape) — must PASS as an ordinary id match, rule not consulted
```

The third is the new one, `--dry-run --tie-same-ulp STEP`: it bumps the engine's logit at the pin's top-1 to
the next BF16 value up (so the payload check would fail under the old predicate) while leaving the sampled id
alone, and requires GREEN. `bf16_bump()` is the helper, and it earned its keep the way helpers do: the first
version forgot the `<< 16` when widening the payload back, produced `2.35e-41` instead of `17.375`, silently
moved the argmax to the *other* tied id, and the selftest reported a wrong-argmax FAIL where it wanted a
PASS. A guard now refuses any bump that does not land strictly above the value it started from.

On the device, `mt32f` is green for the right reason:

```
[info] step 11: the pin is an exact 2-way tie at 24.25 (payload 0x41c2) among ids [4016, 15048], and the
       engine picked the pinned top-1 4016 — an ordinary id match, so the tie policy is not consulted
[PASS] generation step 11 samples greedy_continuation[12] — 4016 vs 4016
[TIE-NOTE] 1 step(s) had an exact tie in the pin that the engine matched outright — ordinary id matches,
           tie policy not consulted (D-1): step 11 -> 4016
```

`dec64f` still prints its two `[TIE]` lines (steps 17 → 10407, 18 → 64704), so the policy's real work is
visible and counted exactly as before.

## D-2 — the route margin is keyed per fixture, because a global max let one fixture move everybody's band

The retired rule was `margin = max over every priced fixture and step of engine deficit / route spread`.
`dec300f`'s pricing (Q2 section) showed what that means in practice: its worst ratio is **7.98** while its
route spread is *tighter* than the 64-step fixtures' (median route 1−cos 4.09e-5 vs `dec64t`'s 6.75e-5), so
pricing it globally would have set the shared constant to 7.99 and loosened `dec64f` and `dec64t` by
2.3× — while leaving it unpriced sat it on the flat floor with 3.7e-6 of headroom at step 86. Neither is a
measurement of the fixture being judged, which is the only thing a margin may be.

Now each `data/refs/<fixture>/route_allowance.json` carries its own margins, derived as the max ratio
**inside that fixture only**, rounded up at 2 dp, and hash-checked as before. Re-derived from the shipped
files (not from memory — `tmp/d5_allowance_report.py` recomputes every figure in this table from the
`route_*`, `engine_*_when_priced` columns of the four files):

| fixture | margin / margin_rel | own derivation from its own rows | steps judged tighter than flat G5 (cos / meanrel) | worst-ratio step | engine 1−cos at that step | its cap | headroom |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dec64f` | **3.50 / 0.42** (carried, pre-D-2 file) | 3.0368 at 32 / 0.3344 at 54 → **3.04 / 0.34** | 54 / 48 of 64 | 32 | 2.1555e-3 | 2.4844e-3 | 13.2 % of the cap |
| `dec64t` | **3.50 / 0.42** | 3.4939 at 54 / 0.4111 at 4 → **3.50 / 0.42** | 64 / 52 of 64 | 54 | 2.3281e-4 | 2.3322e-4 | 0.17 % of the cap |
| `dec300f` | **7.99 / 0.72** (new) | 7.9837 at 86 / 0.7198 at 72 → **7.99 / 0.72** | 110 / 98 of 128 | 86 | 4.6322e-4 | 4.6358e-4 | 0.08 % of the cap |
| `mt32f` | **2.93 / 0.24** (new) | 2.9221 at 19 / 0.2303 at 3 → **2.93 / 0.24** | 32 / 30 of 32 | 19 | 3.7257e-4 | 3.7358e-4 | 0.27 % of the cap |

Three things that table says, and one thing it does not.

1. **`dec64f`'s margin is unchanged at 3.50 / 0.42** — measured in its gate log, not asserted: "this
   fixture's margin in force 3.50". Its own derivation would be 3.04 / 0.34, i.e. *tighter*; D-2 recorded
   keeping the carried numbers, and the tool enforces that: `engine/route_allowance.py --table
   tmp/route_dec64f.ra.json` **refuses** (`exit=2`) with "this measurement derives 3.04/0.34 but the file on
   disk carries 3.50/0.40… re-pricing would make the band tighter. This tool does not move a band on its
   own", and names the file to delete deliberately if the new number is the one to believe.
2. **The wide fixture is now priced and green**: `dec300f` at 7.99 and `mt32f` at 2.93, both from their own
   steps. Per fixture, the allowance is *tighter than the flat floors on the overwhelming majority of steps*
   (110 of 128 and 32 of 32 on cos) and opens only where two legal reference forms open first — the property
   that made option 1 defensible in the Q1 section is intact at the new breadth.
3. **The structural guarantee**: pricing or re-pricing one fixture cannot change any other fixture's margin,
   because `route_allowance.py:derive()` takes one table and reads nothing else. `--selftest` in that tool
   proves it and proves the proof is falsifiable: it prices a synthetic fixture whose own worst ratio is
   3.4939 alongside one whose own worst ratio is 7.9837, requires the first to stay at **3.50** and the
   second to be **7.99**, and asserts that a global max over the same rows would have answered *differently*
   — so a revert to the old rule fails the test rather than passing it. The refusal that used to enforce the
   old scheme ("pass every priced fixture each time") is gone, and replaced by a printout of the files this
   run did not touch and why they cannot be affected. One property of where these files live, stated because
   it changes how the table above is reproduced: `data/` is gitignored, so **no** `route_allowance.json` is
   tracked — not these two, not `dec64f`'s. They are regenerable artifacts (`engine/route_price.py` then
   `engine/route_allowance.py`, both CPU-only), which is why every figure quoted here is re-derived from the
   files on disk by `tmp/d5_allowance_report.py` instead of from a commit.
4. What the table does **not** say: that the rule is now honest about the engine. **The self-reference is
   still there, and per-fixture keying makes it more visible, not less.** A margin defined as
   `max(engine deficit / route spread)` puts that fixture's worst observed step exactly on the boundary *by
   construction*: `dec300f` step 86 now passes with 3.6e-7 of a 4.6e-4 cap left, which is 0.08 %, and
   `dec64t` step 54 with 0.17 %. The gate says so on its summary line ("Worst step 86: deficit = 7.984x its
   own measured route spread, this fixture's margin in force 7.99"), because a band whose worst case is its
   own calibration point is a fitted constant wearing a measurement's clothes. **D-3 / Q4b is the fix** — a
   multiplier derived from legal-route disagreement alone (a third legal variant, `route(a,b)` vs
   `route(a,c)`), which does not reference the engine. It is scheduled, not attempted here, and if it comes
   out smaller than the engine needs that is a finding about the engine, not a licence to loosen.

## What the gate prints now, per step

The rule in force is still named on every step line, and now names the fixture and its own margin:

```
step 0: metrics — … | rule: route allowance for dec64f (this step's measured spread=5.2616e-05,
        dec64f's own margin=3.50 -> cos deficit <= 1.8415e-04; meanrel spread=6.1065%, margin_rel=0.42
        -> <= 2.5647%)
```

and a failed band line still carries the flat-G5 counterfactual ("the flat §10.1 G5 floor (5.0000e-04) would
have PASSED this step — the route allowance is what caught it", or "it is outside the flat … too").
`load_allowance` also now checks each cached file against itself — the file must name the fixture it is read
for, every step's cap must equal its own margin × its own spread, and the margin must admit the engine
deficits recorded in the same file — so a hand-edited number fails the gate instead of quietly becoming the
band. `dec64f` and `dec64t` carry no `margin_basis` field because they predate D-2; the gate says so out loud
("carried in this file from the retired global-max scheme… may be looser than this fixture's own derivation")
rather than pretending they set their own constants.

## The pass, and what a worker ran

All correctness-only, each inside the `<60 s` worker allowance, under `tools/gpu_lock.sh` (tag
"engine gate D-1/D-2 last worker pass (correctness only)"), `gpu_lock.sh util` **92 %** at the first read and
**60 %** at the last — discounted as usual, the serving engine that renders this text is the load and it
busy-polls the iGPU. **No timing taken**: the wall figures below are the cost of running a check, printed only
to show each one fit the worker allowance, and none of it goes near `bench/performance-status.md`. The whole
pass is one command — `tmp/d6_final_pass.py` — and it left the ten logs in `tmp/d6_*.log`. Both helpers live
under `tmp/`, which is gitignored: they are how these figures were made, the tables above are the record.

```
engine/route_allowance.py --selftest                                   CPU    ~0 s   PASS (6 checks)
engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture dec8                               CPU    1.1 s  PASS (tie + allow cases SKIPped out loud)
engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture dec64f                             CPU    5.0 s  PASS (10 cases incl. 3 tie injections)
engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture dec64t                             CPU    3.5 s  PASS
engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture dec300f                            CPU    6.1 s  PASS (5 % noise SKIPped: inside its own allowance)
engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture mt32f                              CPU    3.2 s  PASS (9 cases incl. 3 tie injections)
engine/gate.py --fixture dec8       device  1.1 s  PASS — 94 checks, flat G5 (still unpriced)
engine/gate.py --fixture dec64f     device  2.0 s  PASS — 550 checks, 2 steps TIE, margin 3.50 (unchanged)
engine/gate.py --fixture dec64t     device  2.2 s  PASS — 547 checks, margin 3.50
engine/gate.py --fixture dec300f    device  6.8 s  PASS — 1059 checks, margin 7.99, worst step 86 at 7.984x
engine/gate.py --fixture mt32f      device  4.1 s  PASS — 291 checks, 32/32 ids, margin 2.93, step 11 an ordinary match
engine/route_price.py --fixture mt32f --engine-log … --out …  CPU  3.8 s  the table that priced mt32f
speccheck/run_all.py                                       CPU  7m50s  26 gates run, 0 failed
```

`dec300f` was **not** re-priced on the CPU: `tmp/route_dec300f.json` (from the Q2 pass) was checked step by
step against a fresh gate log with `route_price.py`'s own parser and the engine column is identical at all
128 steps (`tmp/d2_dec300f_still_valid.py`), so the shipped table was cited rather than re-made — the same
rule `AGENTS.md` gives for any measurement already on disk whose inputs have not moved.

## What this pass left open, named

1. **`dec8` is still unpriced**, so its 8 steps are judged by the flat floors and every step line says
   `flat G5`. Under D-2 pricing it would write only its own margin, so the old objection ("it would move the
   shared constant") is gone; what is left is that no question here needs its answer.
2. **D-3 / Q4b**, the engine-independent multiplier, above. D-2 keyed the margin per fixture; it did not make
   the margin engine-independent.
3. **No fixture has a 3-way-or-wider tie**, so "all tied ids share one payload" is exercised at 2-way only.
   It is structural in `pin_tie` (membership *is* payload equality), but it is untested at width.
4. **`margin_basis` is absent on `dec64f`/`dec64t`'s files**, on purpose. If either is ever re-priced the tool
   refuses first, so keeping the carried constants stays a decision rather than an oversight.
5. **Why `dec300f`'s ratio is ~8 while the 64-step fixtures' are ~3 is still unexplained.** D-2 accommodates
   it per fixture; it does not explain it. The candidate cause named in the Q2 section stands — the route
   instrument prices only the GDN prompt-form choice, while the engine's end-to-end deficit includes an
   attention path that grows with lanes — and the missing instrument is still the engine's own per-step state
   moments next to the pinned ones.

# D-4 of `bench/plan-2026-10-12`: the engine free-runs the corpus, and every one of its divergences is a tie

Q2's item 3 closed by saying the repo had no divergence-depth measurement on a prompt whose next token is
genuinely uncertain, and named the corpus run as the thing that would give one. This is that run: the same
49 plain-text prompts × 24 tokens A-18 quotes (`speccheck/fixtures/corpus.txt`, pinned as
`data/refs/corpus48`), the engine free-running on all of them, judged against the reference's own pinned
continuations and against `corpus48sdpa` (the chaos floor, reproduced at **0.8776** by
`tools/corpus_agreement.py corpus48 corpus48sdpa` before anything else was touched, so the baseline in this
section is A-18's number and not a new one).

Two instruments were needed and both are new. **`tools/corpus_margin.py`** re-derives, on CPU, the
reference's decision margin at every step of its own greedy chain — `gap12`, the same quantity
`engine/gate.py::metrics()` reports, plus the top-8 ranking and the gap to the first *distinct* value below
the top. The corpus fixtures pin ids only (24 steps × 49 prompts of full logits is ~700 MB and buys nothing),
so the margin had to be re-derived; it is checked, not assumed — **49/49 prompts reproduce their pinned
continuation 24/24**, which also confirms `corpus48` is the eager route. **`tools/corpus_freerun.py`** drives
`engine/build/decode_run --session` over the corpus (reset / feed prompt / gen 24 per prompt, one resident
process so 49 prompts pay the 1.55 GB load once), writes an engine manifest in `corpus_dump.py`'s schema so
`corpus_agreement.py` can read it, and then judges it. Alignment is the session's direct one — `gen[i]`
pairs with `greedy_continuation[i]` — which is the *other* index from run mode's `decode[s]` ↔ `greedy[s+1]`;
both are named in the tool's header because getting this wrong reads as total divergence (§10.5).

## The numbers, against the chaos floor rather than against 1.0

Budget 24 tokens, same as A-18, so the metrics are comparable to its 0.8776.

| reading | ENGINE free run | reference vs reference (`corpus48sdpa`) |
|---|---|---|
| mean prefix agreement (matched-prefix / 24, averaged) | **0.7015** | **0.8776** (= A-18, reproduced) |
| same, tie-aware (an exactly-tied decision resolved inside the tie set counts as agreement) | **0.7236** | — |
| first-divergence index: mean / median / min | 16.84 / 24 / 0 | 21.06 / 24 / 1 |
| prompts that never diverged in 24 tokens | **27 / 49** (tie-aware 28) | 40 / 49 |
| tokens agreed in total | 825 of 1176 | 1032 of 1176 |
| engine free runs that emitted stop id 248046 | 0 / 49 (no early truncation to explain anything away) | — |

The distribution is the interesting part and it is **bimodal, in both columns**: the engine agrees all 24
tokens on 27 prompts, and the 22 that diverge are scattered with no tail structure — 1 at 0 tokens, 3 at 1,
1 at 3, 2 at 4, 1 at 5, 2 at 7, 1 at 8, 3 at 9, 2 at 10, 1 at 11, 2 at 12, 1 at 15, 1 at 16, 1 at 23. A
mean over a bimodal distribution like this one is a description of neither mode, which is why the median is
quoted at the budget (a prompt that never diverged is quoted at 24 = "at least this far", never at 0 and
never dropped).

Conditioned on the reference's own certainty (per-prompt **median** `gap12`, logits units, over its 24
steps), the expected pattern is there but it is not clean:

| median `gap12` | prompts | engine mean tokens agreed (never-diverged) | floor mean tokens (never-div) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.0–0.5 | 1 | 7.00 (0) | 24.00 (1) |
| 0.5–1.0 | 8 | 13.75 (2) | 16.88 (4) |
| 1.0–2.0 | 19 | 16.63 (10) | 20.53 (15) |
| 2.0–4.0 | 17 | 19.94 (13) | 22.76 (16) |
| 4.0–8.0 | 4 | 13.25 (2) | 24.00 (4) |

Agreement does grow with the margin — except in the top bucket, which is four prompts and two divergences
and proves nothing either way. But the median margin is the wrong conditioner, and the reason is the finding:
**what matters is the margin at the step that flipped, not the prompt's typical margin.**

## Every first flip is a step where the reference had not made a decision

Judged against the reference's own ranking at the flip step (`gap12` there; whether the divergent id was
itself tied at the top in the reference's logits; how far below the top the first *differently-valued*
candidate sat):

```
ENGINE: 22 prompts diverged. gap12 at the flip: median 0.000, min 0.000, max 0.000. At an exact tie: 22.
  Of those, its own id was tied at the top in the reference: 22. Id beyond the reference's top-8: 0.
  First DISTINCT value below the top at the flip: median 0.344 (min 0.125) — the logit error a flip there needed.
FLOOR (sdpa vs eager): 9 prompts diverged. gap12 at the flip: median 0.000, min 0.000, max 0.125.
  At an exact tie: 8. Of those, its own id was tied at the top in the reference: 8.
  First DISTINCT value below the top at the flip: median 0.250 (min 0.125).
```

So: not one of the 22 engine divergences overruled a decision the reference had actually made. Every one of
them sits at a step where two ids hold the **same BF16 payload** in the reference's own logits — `gap12` = 0
— where greedy's convention is to take the lowest id, and where the engine picked an id from that same tied
set. That is D-1's tie policy and §10.1 G7's tie clause applied per step instead of per fixture, and it says
such a step is not a failure; `mt32f` step 11 is the same object (a pinned tie between ids 4016 and 15048).
Ties at the argmax are not exotic at BF16 and a 248 320-wide vocabulary: **28 of these 49 prompts contain at
least one exactly-tied step inside 24 tokens** (per-prompt minimum `gap12`: median 0.000, max 0.625). The
reference-vs-reference floor flips for the same reason in 8 of its 9 cases, the ninth at `gap12` = 0.125 =
one BF16 ULP at that magnitude.

## What this licenses, and what it does not

**What it licenses.** On the prompt set A-18 was measured on, at the same budget, the engine free-runs to a
mean prefix agreement of 0.7015 strict / 0.7236 tie-aware against the reference's own greedy continuation,
and **not one of its 22 first divergences was a decision the reference had won by a nonzero margin** — all
22 were steps where the reference's own top-1 and top-2 held identical BF16 payloads, and in all 22 the
engine's token was one of the ids in that tie. Divergence *depth* on uncertain prompts therefore turned out
not to be the quantity worth reporting: on this checkpoint the first flip is governed by tie frequency, not
by a growing error, and "how deep does an engine agree on an uncertain prompt" has no answer that is not also
"how many exactly-tied steps did that chain contain". Two consequences are safe to state: the old fear that
the engine diverges early because it accumulates something (`dec64t` 64/64, `dec300f` 128/128, 27 of 49
corpus prompts full 24/24, and zero flips at a decided step among the 825 tokens the free runs reached) is
not supported, and a strict
token-equality corpus gate applied to the engine without the tie clause would reject correct code — which is
the A-18 argument, now demonstrated on the engine rather than only on the sdpa variant of the reference.

**What it does not license.** It does not license "0.7015 is worse than the floor, therefore the engine is
worse than a legal rounding of the reference": the floor's own 9 divergences are 8 ties and one 1-ULP step,
so the two numbers differ mostly in tie-breaking luck (the engine picks a tied id by a different arithmetic
path, greedy breaks the tie by index), and a tie-aware recount puts the engine at 0.7236 against a floor of
the same kind. It does not license *anything* about the steps after a first flip: prefix agreement is
consumed by the first divergence, so those steps are unmeasured here, and this is a prefix instrument, not a
divergence-depth instrument, for exactly that reason. It does not license a corpus gate number — no band, no
`BAND`, no `speccheck/specimpl.py`, and no gate predicate was touched here; a corpus gate for the engine
would need the tie clause written into it as a decision, not inferred from this table. And it does not
license treating the margin column as an engine-error bound: `gap12` and `gap_distinct` are properties of
the reference's logits, and the corpus has no pinned logits, so nothing here says how large the engine's
logit error is — the instrument that would (the engine's own per-step logits against pins at corpus scale)
is still the one named as missing in Q1, Q2 and Q3.

## What this pass ran, and how to extend it

One device call, the whole corpus, correctness only, under `tools/gpu_lock.sh` (tag "D-4 corpus free run";
`gpu_lock.sh util` read **93 %** at the acquire and the lock showed `free` after the release — discounted per
`AGENTS.md`, the serving engine that renders this text is the load). Wall for the whole command including the
1.55 GB load was 18 s, comfortably inside the worker allowance; **no timing was taken and nothing here
belongs in `bench/performance-status.md`** — the wall figure is the cost of running a check, not a
measurement. The margin pass is CPU-only (~45 s) and the report pass touches neither.

```bash
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire "D-4 corpus free run"; tools/gpu_lock.sh util
.venv/bin/python tools/corpus_freerun.py                      # device, 18 s: all 49 prompts, then the report
tools/gpu_lock.sh release "D-4 corpus free run"
.venv/bin/python tools/corpus_margin.py --out data/refs/corpus48-margins.json   # CPU, ~45 s
.venv/bin/python tools/corpus_freerun.py --report-only --json bench/corpus-freerun-2026-10-12.json
```

`--start`/`--limit` chunk the corpus across several calls if a longer budget is ever wanted (a 128-token
corpus budget would be ~4× the steps and still one session; `--steps 128` overrides the corpus' own 24,
which only changes what this tool prints — the pins stay 24 and the comparison caps at the pin length).
The full per-prompt records — first divergence, both margins, the flip's rank in the reference's top-8,
the tie flag — are committed as `bench/corpus-freerun-2026-10-12.json`; the engine manifest it reads lives
in the gitignored `data/` like every other regenerable fixture.

## What this left open, named

1. **Tie-aware prefix agreement is a metric with no gate behind it.** If a corpus gate for the engine is ever
   wanted, the tie clause has to be written into it deliberately (this pass measured with it and reported it
   beside the strict number; it changed no predicate).
2. **The steps after a first flip are unmeasured.** Measuring them needs a different question — replay the
   engine along the *reference's* chain at corpus scale (forced, like `dec64f`), which prices per-step
   agreement without chaos, instead of a free run that burns its budget on the first tie.
3. **Tie frequency is now a measured property of this checkpoint and nothing consumes it.** 28/49 chains
   contain a tie inside 24 tokens; any future gate written in "tokens equal" is silently a tie-frequency
   gate too, and the number to quote beside it is this one.
4. **49 prompts × 24 tokens is A-18's budget, not a bigger one.** The corpus was never widened (§10's G7
   intent was "hundreds cheap"); the run costs 18 s of device, so widening it is a fixture-regeneration
   decision (`tools/corpus_dump.py --tag … --steps …` on a wider prompt list), not an engineering one.

---

## D-3 — the route margin cannot be made engine-independent, and the reason is a reference-side baseline

Task: `bench/plan-2026-10-12.md` Q4b / decision **D-3** — stop fitting the margin to the engine's own errors,
calibrate the multiplier from **legal reference-side routes alone**, and if that multiplier turns out smaller
than what the engine needs, report it as a finding about the engine rather than as a licence to loosen.

**Outcome: keep.** No band was moved, no fixture re-priced, nothing under `data/refs/` was written. The
engine-independent multiplier is **1.19–1.96** on the primary column (up to 2.40 if a second legal axis is
counted), while the engine-fitted margins in force are **2.93–7.99**. The instrument is
[`route_margin.py`](route_margin.py) — CPU-only, reads the pins and the priced allowances, writes only `tmp/`,
imports neither `route_allowance.py` nor `band_history.py`, and refuses by construction to turn this measurement
into a band move in either direction.

## The third legal route, and the two constraints that shaped it

The two routes the gate already prices are (a) the chunked GDN prompt form of §04.7 — the family the pins came
from — and (b) the token-by-token recurrent form of §04.4. D-3 needed a **third**, independent of both.

**Route c: a different GDN chunk width under §04.7.** The legality is verbatim §04.7: *"Any prefill algorithm is
correct iff, for every t, it returns the `o[t]` and final `S` of §04.4 applied token-by-token … vLLM-ref and the
engine may differ in chunk size or fusion strategy"*. The gate that licenses that sentence already exists —
`speccheck/gates/run_chunkcheck.py` (chunk vs recurrent ≥ 0.99999 per layer, both equidistant from the pinned
reference; A-08 closed). It is not a knob: it re-associates the same F32 sums with a different block width,
nothing re-typed or dropped, which is `prototype/mlp_gemv/floor_probe.py`'s reassociation argument (§10.1 F1–F4)
applied to the whole prompt→state map rather than to one dot. Several widths are priced per fixture so the
answer cannot be one width's artifact.

**Route d/e: §07.3's blocked `(max, sum)` decode softmax**, a second and orthogonal legal freedom
(`Forward(attn_softmax="online")`), legal by §07.3 verbatim — *"the blocked form is not banned … it stays
available in the oracle"* — with its band measured in A-17. It is priced and reported, but **kept out of the
conservative multiplier**, because the shipped engine uses the one-pass form (§07.3's decision, since that one
is bit-exact). Pricing a freedom nobody took would make the gate greener for nobody's benefit, which is exactly
the failure D-3 exists to catch.

Two constraints decided how this is built. First, **`speccheck/specimpl.py` may not be edited**:
`engine/gate.py:allowance_inputs` pins the oracle's sha256 into every priced fixture, so an oracle edit would
invalidate all four cached `route_allowance.json` files at once (`dec8` is not priced — it runs on the flat
floors — so five fixtures are gated and four are priced). The width is therefore rebound **in this
process only**, as a module-attribute swap on `specimpl.gdn_chunk_scan`, and the rebind is counted so a silent
no-op cannot pass; `--check-patch` runs route a through the rebind at the shipped width and requires it
**bit-identical** to the unpatched route a. It is, on every fixture. Second, **a width that does not repartition
the prompt is not a third route**: `gdn_chunk_scan` right-pads to a multiple of `C`, so a 17-token prompt is one
chunk at `C=32` and at `C=64`, and the deficit comes back exactly 0.0. Such widths are reported INERT and
excluded, detected by **bit-equality rather than a magnitude threshold** — F64 `cos` carries ~2e-16 of noise, so
a threshold test would have fed a run of zeros into a max and understated the multiplier, which is a loosening.
`dec64f`/`dec64t` (17/27-token prompts) therefore have two live widths, `C=8` and `C=16`; `dec300f` and `mt32f`
have all four.

## The instrument, and what proves it is measuring what the gate measures

`engine/route_margin.py` computes, per step, each legal route's deficit **against route a** — the same reference
the engine is judged against, since the pins came from the chunked family — and divides by `def_b`, the spread the
gate prices today:

```
fitted  = max_j engine_def(j) / def_b(j)      today's basis: the engine sits in the numerator
M_1     = max_j def_c(j)      / def_b(j)      the same functional with a legal route in the engine's seat
M_wide  = max over every axis priced, §07.3 included
ENVELOPE= max_j max_x def_x(j) / def_b(j)     widest legal deficit the priced set can make at that step
```

One functional with three numerators, so comparing them is arithmetic rather than argument. The statistic is a
**max**, not a p95 or a median, because the rule it calibrates is a per-step cap that every step must clear — a
p95 multiplier leaves the p95+1 step red by construction, and D-2's whole point was that a margin is the
tightest constant admitting *every* measured step. p95 and median are printed beside it so a reader can see
whether a given max is one outlier or the whole tail; they are not offered as the constant.

Three properties were checked rather than assumed:

- **The engine column is faithful.** Re-deriving `fitted` from the recorded gate logs reproduces the shipped raw
  maxima **bit-exactly** on all four fixtures (`3.036751330619`, `3.4938884843377`, `7.98371554188016`,
  `2.92207154233073`), so route b and the log reader are the same computation the allowance was priced with.
- **The margin in force is read, not copied.** The comparison uses `engine/gate.py`'s own allowance reader, and
  `verdict()` refuses to call a looser multiplier an improvement: `--selftest` case 4 fails if anyone makes it
  possible to adopt a smaller number as a way of turning a gate green, and case 9 refuses a step where two
  legal routes agree bit-for-bit rather than skipping it (a skipped step is a silently shrunken max, and a zero
  denominator is the same event seen from the other side).
- `--selftest` is 12 cases and passes, including the chunk-count arithmetic that makes an inert width inert
  (cases 10–11) and the meanrel definition check below (case 12).

## The numbers

Engine column from the re-verified gate runs (`tmp/d6_gate_*.log`, the same runs D-2 was re-verified on); no
device work was done for this section. `M_1` is the §04.7-width axis only — the legal freedom the shipped engine
is actually entitled to take.

| fixture | steps | engine-fitted | in force | M_1 lo | M_1 hi | M_wide | ENVELOPE | RED if M_1 lo adopted | RED at 1.00 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dec64f` | 64 | **3.0368** @32 | 3.50 | 1.4481 (C8) | 1.7982 (C16) | 2.3971 (d) | 2.3971 | **10 of 64** | 45 of 64 |
| `dec64t` | 64 | **3.4939** @54 | 3.50 | 1.5004 (C8) | 1.7173 (C16) | 1.7173 (C16) | 1.7173 | **25 of 64** | 58 of 64 |
| `dec300f` | 128 | **7.9837** @86 | 7.99 | 1.4948 (C16) | 1.9622 (C128) | 1.9622 (C128) | 1.9622 | **85 of 128** | 125 of 128 |
| `mt32f` | 32 | **2.9221** @19 | 2.93 | 1.1899 (C128) | 1.5446 (C32) | 1.7591 (e) | 1.7591 | **21 of 32** | 29 of 32 |

The trap fires on every fixture, and the answer to "which multiplier, max or p95 or median" does not change it:
the widest legal route anywhere in this measurement reaches 2.40, the engine needs 2.92–7.99. **The engine sits
1.7×–4.1× outside what any legal prompt route produces at the step that sets its own margin** (1.3× on `dec64f`
alone if §07.3's axis is counted). Per the task's own instruction, that is a finding about the engine: nothing
was loosened, no band touched, no fixture re-priced.

It is not a small-denominator artifact either. The step that sets each margin is one where the *priced spread is
large*, not where it collapsed — `dec64f` step 32 sits at the 95th percentile of that fixture's spreads
(7.098e-04 against a 4.930e-05 median) and `dec300f` step 86 at the 83rd. And at those steps the legal routes do
open (at `dec64f` step 32 the worst legal excursion is 1.276e-03 against the priced 7.098e-04) — the engine's
2.156e-03 there is simply 1.7× beyond the widest legal excursion at the same step, in absolute magnitude, not
merely beyond the ratio.

## The meanrel column is worse than that: the shipped constant mixes two statistics

§10.2 fixes the definition, and `engine/gate.py:metrics` follows it: meanrel is **mean|Δ| / mean|ref|**, a ratio
of means, with the BAND comment saying explicitly that the mean-of-ratios form "explodes on the near-zero logits
that most of a 248 320-wide vocabulary is". But `route_price.meanrel` — the denominator of every cached
`margin_rel` — **is** that mean of ratios. So `margin_rel` is a ratio of two different statistics. The gate's
arithmetic is internally consistent (the same two quantities are used when pricing and when gating, so no gate
result is wrong as applied), but the constant cannot be read as "the engine's relative error is 0.42× the legal
route spread"; the legal denominator is on a different scale from the engine numerator. `--selftest` case 12
prices the definitional gap directly: on a vocabulary-shaped tensor (one real logit, 999 near-zero ones) the same
absolute perturbation prices **11× higher** as a mean of ratios than as a ratio of means.

Priced both ways, on the same routes:

| fixture | as shipped (engine rom / route mor) | M_1, same basis | consistent (§10.2 both sides) | M_1, consistent | in force |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dec64f` | 0.3344 @54 | 1.8408–1.8755 | **1.8284** @32 | 1.3211–1.7040 | 0.42 |
| `dec64t` | 0.4111 @4 | 2.1256–2.5169 | **2.2522** @56 | 1.4653–1.5502 | 0.42 |
| `dec300f` | 0.7198 @72 | 2.2911–5.1284 | **3.2000** @86 | 1.3832–1.7411 | 0.72 |
| `mt32f` | 0.2303 @3 | 1.5945–2.0397 | **1.8907** @19 | 1.1878–1.3460 | 0.24 |

Read as shipped, adopting a route-derived meanrel multiplier would **loosen** the line by 3–7× (0.24 → 1.59 on
`mt32f`, 0.72 → 2.29 on `dec300f`), which the same anti-loosening rule refuses. Read consistently — the column
on the right, which is the only one that means what it appears to say — the picture matches the cos column
instead: the engine needs 1.83–3.20 and the legal width axis reaches 1.19–1.74, so it is tight in the same
direction, for the same reason.
Both readings end at `keep`. The mismatch itself is reported, not fixed: correcting it would multiply every
`margin_rel` by ~4–5×, which is a band move and therefore the operator's call with a `tools/band_history.py`
line, not an artefact of this measurement.

## Why the route spread cannot price the engine's deficit — the baseline that decides it

The engine's deficit is measured against the **pinned HF dump**; every legal route's deficit above is measured
against **specimpl's route a**. Different references, so two more quantities are priced, both on the pin: how far
the oracle's own route a sits from the dump the engine is judged on (`ORACLE` below, in margin units), and — the
only comparison with both sides on the same dump — the per-step ratio of the engine's deficit to the oracle's
(`ENGINE/ORACLE`, a max over steps of a per-step ratio, not a ratio of two maxima).

| fixture | engine vs pin, fitted | oracle a vs pin, in margin units | engine ÷ oracle, per step | oracle worst deficit | engine worst deficit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dec64f` | 3.0368 @32 | 3.7758 @34 | **3.4801** @32 | 3.170e-03 | 2.156e-03 |
| `dec64t` | 3.4939 @54 | 2.2535 @54 | **1.6795** @56 | 1.794e-04 | 2.410e-04 |
| `dec300f` | 7.9837 @86 | 3.2254 @89 | **3.2274** @86 | 4.926e-04 | 4.632e-04 |
| `mt32f` | 2.9221 @19 | 1.9357 @23 | **2.4615** @19 | 1.810e-04 | 3.726e-04 |

Both halves of that table matter and neither is a fact about the engine alone. The oracle's own chunked route — a
legal route, one of the two the gate already prices — needs **1.94–3.78** in margin units all by itself, which is
the same order as the entire fitted margin, so a large part of what the band is paying for is the distance between
the reference implementation and the HF dump it is pinned against (A-18's chaos floor, 0.8776 sdpa-vs-eager, one
level up). No pair of specimpl-internal routes can exhibit that term, both members of such a pair being
specimpl. **But it does not absorb the engine**: measured per step against the same dump, the engine's deficit is
**1.68–3.48×** the oracle's own deficit at that step. The engine's error is on top of the reference's, not
inside it.

That is the honest shape of the answer to D-3. The self-referentiality the row complained about is real — the
current denominator is a route pair, and the numerator contains a term neither member of that pair can produce.
It is also not a licence to treat the engine's deficits as noise: they are against the dump the gate is defined
on, and they exceed the oracle's own gap at the same step on every fixture. Two wrong readings are both worth
naming, because the numbers invite them. (1) Re-denominating on the oracle-vs-pin column does not make the margin
engine-free in the way D-3 hoped — it moves the required number from 2.92–7.98 to **1.68–3.48**, which is smaller
and no longer self-referential, but it is still a number measured on the engine. (2) It is per-fixture and
per-prompt in exactly the way D-2 already found unsafe to pool, and it would have to be priced and cached for
every prompt the engine is ever gated on.

## Recommendation

**Keep the margins as they are: 3.50 / 3.50 / 7.99 / 2.93 (cos) and 0.42 / 0.42 / 0.72 / 0.24 (meanrel).** The
engine-independent multiplier D-3 asked for exists, it is 1.19–1.96 (§04.7 widths) or at most 2.40 (any legal axis
priced here), and it is 1.7×–4.1× smaller than what the engine needs on every fixture priced; adopting it would
turn 10, 25, 85 and 21 of the respective fixture's steps RED. The reason is now identified rather than
speculated about: the numerator contains an oracle-vs-dump term that no legal-route pair inside the oracle can
produce, so the basis cannot be replaced by route disagreement at this rule shape.

If the margins are ever to be replaced rather than re-fitted, two candidate rule shapes were priced here and
neither adopted. The first, an **absolute legal envelope** per step (`allow(j) = max over legal routes of
def_route(j)` measured against route a), was checked at all four margin-setting steps and **fails at every one**:
the widest legal excursion at `dec64f` step 32 is 1.276e-03 where the engine's deficit there is 2.156e-03, and
the same holds on `dec64t` (8.339e-05 vs 2.328e-04), `dec300f` (9.577e-05 vs 4.632e-04) and `mt32f` (1.737e-04 vs
3.726e-04). The second is the only one worth carrying forward: **denominate the allowance on the oracle's own
gap to the pin** — `allow(j) = margin_pin × def_pin_vs_route_a(j)` — which puts the denominator on the same dump
as the numerator and removes the term no route pair can produce. The multiplier it needs is **1.68–3.48** instead
of 2.92–7.98. That is still a number measured on the engine, so it does not settle D-3's original objection; it
shrinks the self-reference rather than removing it, and it would need a third column in every
`route_allowance.json` plus a rule change in `engine/gate.py`. Both are decisions about what the band means, not
calibrations, and they belong in the plan's decision log with a band-history line before any code follows.

## What this pass ran, and what was left for the device pass

Everything here is CPU-only: the torch reference passes plus the already-recorded gate logs. No device work was
done and **no timing was taken**; the gates themselves were not re-run, because nothing that feeds them changed
(this section adds one read-only instrument and documentation; no band, no allowance file, no `specimpl`, no
`gate.py`). The recorded D-2 re-verification remains the green state.

```bash
.venv/bin/python engine/route_margin.py --selftest                    # 12 cases, no files touched
.venv/bin/python engine/route_margin.py --fixture dec64f --engine-log tmp/d6_gate_dec64f.log \
    --check-patch --out tmp/route_margin_dec64f.json                  # repeat per fixture
```

## The handover: what this pass could not run, and the sequence if a band move is ever decided

Two things are device-bound and were therefore not run here, and one is deliberately not this pass's to run.

**Re-verifying the fixtures green** (main agent only, blocking, zero subagents — `engine/gate.py` drives the
built binary on the device this session is being served from):

```bash
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire "D-3 keep: re-verify"; tools/gpu_lock.sh util
for f in dec8 dec64f dec64t dec300f mt32f; do .venv/bin/python engine/gate.py --fixture $f; done
tools/gpu_lock.sh release "D-3 keep: re-verify"
```

Expected, from the recorded D-2 pass: 94 / 550 / 547 / 1059 / 291 checks, zero failures, and the rule line
naming each fixture's own margin (3.50, 3.50, 7.99, 2.93). Nothing here should change that — no input to the
gate moved — so if any of those five goes red after this commit, the cause is something else in the tree and
`route_margin.py` is a useful witness: it will show whether the reference's own routes moved.

**Moving a margin, if a decision is ever taken to do one** (the documented way, which `route_allowance.py`
enforces — it refuses a re-price that differs from the shipped constant until the file is deleted deliberately,
and it writes its own `band_history` line for both the write and the refusal):

```bash
# ONLY under a recorded decision (plan §3, revisit clause (a) or (b)) — D-3 as measured says do not.
rm data/refs/<fixture>/route_allowance.json                       # the deliberate step; it is what makes it visible
.venv/bin/python engine/route_price.py --fixture <fixture> \
    --engine-log tmp/d6_gate_<fixture>.log --out tmp/route_<fixture>.json
.venv/bin/python engine/route_allowance.py --table tmp/route_<fixture>.json --dry-run
.venv/bin/python engine/route_allowance.py --table tmp/route_<fixture>.json
.venv/bin/python tools/band_history.py check                      # must come back clean
```

and the same in `engine/results.md` about what moved and why, which is the half the tool cannot write for you.
If the change decided is instead a *rule shape* (the pin-side denominator above), that sequence is not enough:
it needs a third per-step column in the allowance schema and a matching rule in `engine/gate.py`, which is a
change to what the gate means, not a constant.

Left open, named:

1. **The meanrel definition mismatch is unfixed on purpose.** Making both sides of `margin_rel` use §10.2's
   definition changes four shipped constants by ~4–5×; that is a band move. The measurement needed to expose it
   is in this section and in `route_margin.py`'s two meanrel columns.
2. **`dec64f` and `dec64t` still carry 3.50 / 0.42** from the retired global scheme, while their own derivations
   are 3.04 / 0.34 and 3.50 / 0.42 — i.e. the carried number is *looser* than their own measurement allows.
   Pre-existing, recorded under D-2, untouched here.
3. **`dec64f` step 17 is unexplained by any of this, and it is reference-side.** **Every** route — including
   route a, the pin's own chunked family — picks a different id than the pin at that one step and agrees
   everywhere else (8/8 routes, 63/64). So it is a place where `specimpl` and the HF dump themselves part
   company, not an engine event; it is the single clearest illustration of the oracle-vs-dump term above, and
   related to §07.5's stop-check and D-4's tie finding without being resolved by either.
4. **The pin-side baseline is priced for route a only, and only as a diagnostic.** If the second candidate rule
   above were ever adopted, every legal route's distance to the pin needs pricing per step and caching in the
   allowance file (cheap — the pins are already on disk), and the interaction with A-18's depth decay has to be
   stated rather than assumed.
5. **A 4k-context fixture will want a bigger number, and this section does not make one available.** The honest
   statement for Q5 is that the band there is engine-fitted, a large part of its numerator is the oracle's own
   distance from HF eager, and the two rule shapes that could change what the band means are both priced and
   both rejected here for a reason: the envelope because it fails at every margin-setting step, the pin-side
   denominator because it shrinks the self-reference without removing it.

## Q5 / P1: the §08 state is now observable, and the end-condition assumption survives contact with the pins

`decode_run --state-fp` emits the engine's GDN state, conv window and KV-tail fingerprints after a run,
computed by `tools/state_fp.py` — the same helper the dumper uses, extracted rather than re-implemented,
which is what makes the comparison meaningful instead than coincidental. `engine/gate.py` section `(g)`
judges them against the `state_fp` / `conv_fp` / `kv_fp` rows already pinned on the four deep fixtures.
Package as planned: **a measurement and a tripwire, not a band** (prefill plan §2.6).

What it proves, re-run by the main agent on device after the worker expired (util 1 % at acquire, 29 % at
release, discounted; five fixtures, **no timing taken**):

| fixture | checks before P1 | after P1 | `(g)` result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dec8` | 94 | 94 | no state pins kept (smoke fixture) |
| `dec64f` | 550 | **576** | PASS |
| `dec64t` | 547 | **573** | PASS |
| `dec300f` | 1059 | **1082** | PASS |
| `mt32f` | 291 | **317** | PASS |

§08.6 invariants, all five fixtures: **inv 1** every state row moved between prompt-end and run-end (a Δ²
of exactly 0 would be a state left behind); **inv 2** same for the conv rows; **inv 3** the KV's
already-written lanes did **not** move — Δ² exactly 0.0 over the common lane extent, K vs K and V vs V
separately. All pass, with the offending-row list empty everywhere.

Engine-vs-pin distances, **reported and not thresholded**: state `n2` ratio 0.995–1.005 with
mean |Δ|/RMS 1.8e-04 to 4.3e-04; KV `n2` ratio 0.9991–0.9996 with mean |Δ|/RMS ~1e-07 (the written lanes
are effectively exact); conv, compared at its **own extent** — newest `k_conv−1` columns — `n2` ratio
**0.9968–0.9980**. The raw whole-buffer conv ratio is ~0.70 and is an artefact of extent mismatch (the
engine keeps a wider padded window), which `(g)` now says out loud instead of reporting it as a 30 %
error.

What `(g)` **cannot** yet prove, stated so nobody over-reads the PASS: no threshold is adopted, so the
distances are witnessed and not judged — adopting one needs the route spread at the same position, i.e.
`engine/route_price.py` extended to state fingerprints, then a decision and a `band_history` line
(**D-6** in the plan's decision log). And on `dec300f` specifically, `(g)` prints real engine-vs-pin
numbers but **cannot prove its own ruler**, because that fixture was pinned with `--deep-steps none` and
keeps no full per-layer state tensors; the conv family there also cannot be priced at its own extent. The
only way past that is a re-pin with `--deep-steps`, and re-pinning a shipped fixture is a main-agent call
(prefill plan §5.1). `dec64f`, `dec64t` and `mt32f` do carry that proof, so the helper is not unverified —
it is unverified on exactly one fixture.

## Q5 / P2: the prompt is now a wave, and the seam that made it possible is proven arithmetic-free

`prefill_wave()` (`engine/prefill.{h,cpp}`) submits one `submit_token_body()` per prompt token and calls
`finish_token()` at the end — §06.5's shape, one submission stream and one vocabulary D2H instead of T of
each. It is enabled by a **decomposition-only seam** in `engine/decode_step.{h,cpp}`: `decode_step()` is
now literally body-then-finish, so §07.1 still has exactly one implementation and the wave is the only
caller that splits the two. The seam was not in the plan's file list; the worker stopped and asked rather
than editing outside its package, and the file list was wrong (`decode_step.cpp:455-471` puts
`lm_head` + the blocking 1 MB D2H + the host argmax inside the per-token call, so a wave that called
`decode_step` could not have one sync without a seam). P4/P5/P6 reuse that seam, which is why it is cut
now, alone, with no arithmetic in the same commit.

**Byte-identity, re-proven by the main agent against the pre-P2 binary** (a worktree built at `358a3fb`,
the P1 state; device run under the lock, util 93 %→35 %, **no timing taken**): `dec8` prompt + 6 steps,
`--prefill-logits all` versus the old per-token path —

| artifact | result |
|---|---|
| `logits0.bin` … `logits6.bin` (7 × 993 280 B) | **`cmp`-identical** |
| `sampled.txt` | **identical** (same ids, same top logits to 7 digits, same `prefix_len_before`) |
| `RESULT` line | same `last_sampled=4058`, `prefix_len=7` |

Five fixtures after the change (my run, not the worker's): 94 / 576 / 573 / 1082 / 317 checks, **zero
failures**, unchanged from P1, with the new `STEP kind=prefill` lines appearing once per prompt row —
17 / 17 / 27 / 295 / 232, i.e. exactly the prompt lengths. `engine/gate.py --selftest` passes, including
the §07.5 injections.

Two deliberate choices worth recording. **§07.5 is now structural, not a conjunct:** the prefill loop has
no `stop_fired` variable at all, so a stop id inside the prompt cannot fire even in principle — the old
`stop_fired = stop_match && !from_prompt` is replaced by the absence of the code path. Because
`--prefill-logits last` (the default) does not even price the earlier rows, `--break-prompt-stop` is aimed
with `--prefill-logits all` explicitly, and refuses an out-of-range index under `last` instead of passing
vacuously. And **`RESULT ms_per_step` keeps its old denominator** (prompt rows + generation steps) so the
ms/token figures already in this file stay comparable across the change; a prefill that amortises launches
will show up there as it should, not as a relabelled denominator.

What P2 does **not** do: nothing is batched. The wave calls the same kernels per token, so it costs the
same launches — the win is the removed T−1 `lm_head`s and syncs and the fact that P4–P6 now have somewhere
to land. The honest open item is a protocol detail: `PREFIX … stopped=` no longer shows a per-prompt-token
stop observation, since under `last` those rows are never priced.

## Q5 / P4 + P4b: the batched kernels are proven to run, and batching cost exactly nothing numerically

P4 landed in three pieces — `46d95c0` (glue: `blockIdx.x` is the row), `eb089a4` (token-batched gateup/down),
`48d469a` (the engine side: `[T_w]` wave scratch plus the batched call sites) — and the five fixtures came
back with worst per-step deficits **bit-identical** to the pre-P4 binary (`dec64f` 2.1555e-03 @32, `dec64t`
2.4099e-04 @2, `dec300f` 4.6322e-04 @86, `mt32f` 3.7257e-04 @19, every ratio **x1.000**). That is the
desired numerical result, and it is also worthless as evidence of execution: a wave that quietly fell back to
one row per launch reproduces those numbers exactly. **P4b's job was the missing proof, and the answer is
yes — the batched kernels run.** The proof is a counter, not an inference from outputs.

**The instrument** (`engine/launch_stats.{h,cpp}`) counts *launches as the host submits them*, at the four
call sites in `kernels_glue.cpp` / `kernels_mlp.cpp` that can batch, and prints one line per family before
`RESULT`:

```
LAUNCHSTAT family=glue        calls=6321 calls_batched=49   rows_total=20727 rows_max=295
LAUNCHSTAT family=mlp_gateup  calls=3096 calls_batched=24   rows_total=10152 rows_max=295
```

`calls_batched` only moves on a launch that carried more than one row, `rows_max` is the widest launch seen.
`--wave-rows N` (new, validated `1..kMaxWaveRows`, echoed back as `PREFILL … wave_rows=`) is the discriminating
knob: the same binary, same kernels, same scratch, one row at a time. `engine/gate.py` section **(h)** runs
both sides of the A/B with `--prefill-logits all`, re-derives the launch plan from §07's arithmetic
(`wave_expect`) and requires the counters to match it exactly, requires `rows_max > 1` on the batched side and
`rows_max == 1`/`calls_batched == 0` on the one-row side, requires `rows_total` equal on both sides (fewer
launches, *not less work*), requires identical sampled ids, and requires the two logits dumps to be
`cmp`-identical. Two injections in `--selftest` (`--break-wave`, `--break-ab-logits`) prove each half bites:
a refactor that drops to per-row launches fails the gate, and so does a difference that shows up only in the
logits.

**The A/B, counted** (`dec300f`, 295-token prompt + 128 steps; wave_rows 512 vs 1):

| | batched wave | `--wave-rows 1` | the count, not a duration |
|---|---|---|---|
| prompt-wave glue+MLP launches | **97** | **28 615** | **295× fewer** for a 295-token prompt |
| whole run, three families | 12 513 | 41 031 | the 128 generation steps are one token wide either way |
| glue, whole run | 6321 calls, 49 batched, rows_max 295 | 20 727 calls, 0 batched, rows_max 1 | `rows_total` 20 727 both sides |
| `mlp_gateup` / `mlp_down`, whole run | 3096 each, 24 batched, rows_max 295 | 10 152 each, 0 batched, rows_max 1 | `rows_total` 10 152 both sides |
| sampled ids / logits | 423 rows | 423 rows | ids equal, **423 files, 420 157 440 bytes, 0 differing** |

`mt32f` (232-token prompt): 97 prompt-wave launches batched vs **22 504** one-row, **232×**; 264 logits
files, 262 225 920 bytes, bit-identical. And it is not a long-prompt effect — the ratio is the prompt
length on every fixture, because a wave costs 97 glue+MLP launches however wide it is: `dec8` 97 vs
1649 (17×, 25 files identical), `dec64t` 97 vs 1649 (17×, 81 files), `dec64f` 97 vs 2619 (27×, 91
files). So the same arithmetic arrives in 97 submissions instead of 28 615, and the bits it arrives
with are the same bits.

Five fixtures after the instrumentation, unchanged verdicts and unchanged margins: **119 / 601 / 598 / 1107 /
342** checks, zero failures (the +21 per fixture is section (h)). The §6.0 margin preflight still binds at the
same steps with the same ratios — `dec64f` step 32 = 3.037 of a 3.50 cap, `dec64t` step 54 = 3.494/3.50,
`dec300f` step 86 = 7.984/7.99, `mt32f` step 19 = 2.922/2.93 — i.e. P4 consumed no route allowance anywhere.

### P4b per-op: the prototype-side stage the WIP commit owed

The engine's launcher and the prototype's are the same instantiation, which is what makes the prototype stage
the engine's per-op gate: `kernels_mlp.cpp` launches `mlp_gateup_tokens<1024, 4>` / `mlp_down_tokens<3584, 2>`
with `kRowsPerBlock = ROWS_PER_BLOCK = 8` and 256 threads, and the prototype host picks `tpb = in == HID ? 4 :
2` with the same `rows_per_block` and `grid(rows/8, ceil(tokens/tpb))`. Same for glue: one launch per call,
`blockIdx.x` the row, 256 threads.

| stage | fixture | result |
|---|---|---|
| `glue/run.py --batched` | `p64` | **PASS**, 784 comparisons 0 failures. Tile plan 7 launches vs 322 per-row; `g1_s`/`g2_s` bit-exact (0/47 104 per layer); every norm within 1 ULP with 0 large elements over tolerance; **tile vs one launch per row: 612 352 elements, 0 differ**; the `--neg plainw` falsification still moves only `g1_n` |
| `glue/run.py --batched` | `p300` | **PASS**, 784 comparisons 0 failures. Tile plan 7 launches vs 1939; `g1_s`/`g2_s` bit-exact (0/283 648 per layer); **tile vs per-row: 3 687 424 elements, 0 differ** |
| `mlp_gemv/run.py --tokens` | `p64` | **PASS**. `stage R` exact; F1 `gate_pre` ≤ 1 ULP (worst 3/3584); F1 `up_pre` ≤ 2 ULP with every outlier inside the 3e-05 band; F2 `act` ≤ 2 ULP, worst 4/3584 (L00), 0 large elements over tolerance at all four layers; F3 ≤ 1 ULP; F4 (`out` from *our* `act`) up to 6.25 % ndiff, worst large element 44 ULP at 1.7e-03 of the tensor max — the same shape the decode-stage gate reports; **batched vs `k=1` on sampled tokens: 23 040 elements, 0 differ at every layer** |
| `mlp_gemv/run.py --tokens` | `p300` | **FAIL, one check, not caused by batching** — see below |
| rows=1 stays green | `dec8` | both gates' decode-stage (rows=1) halves pass bit-for-bit inside the same runs, and `--negative`'s six injected bugs are still each caught where the bug lives and nowhere else |

**The one red line, measured and reported rather than tuned.** `mlp_gemv/run.py --tokens p300` fails
`L03 r032 F2 act: 1 large element(s) differ by >2 ULP (worst 3 ULP at |ref|/max 7.8e-03)`. Three controls,
all with the gate's own comparator:

1. **the pin is correctly paired** — torch rebuilding `act` from the pin's own `gate_pre`/`up_pre` matches the
   pinned `act` at that row in all 3584 elements (the gate's stage R), so §10.5's last-writer-wins trap is
   not in play;
2. **batching is exonerated** — the shipped single-token kernel run on exactly that row produces the identical
   3 elements, same 3-ULP outlier, and 0 elements differ between the tile's row and the single-token row;
3. **the outlier is the propagated consequence of a legal input flip** — at `idx 1650` the kernel's
   `gate_pre` is **1 ULP** from the pin (legal under F1, whose band is 1 ULP) and `up_pre` is exact, and
   `act = round(round(silu(g)) · u)`, so a legal input ULP can arrive as 3. The row's other two deviations
   have the same shape (2 ULP with a 1-ULP `up_pre`; 1 ULP inside the cancellation band).

So this is a floor-survey question, not a kernel question: F2's 2-ULP `act` band was sized by
`floor_probe.py` on the *decode* population, and this is the first time ~1.1 M prefill elements have been
put through it. Per plan §6.1 the honest fix is a number inside a band, so **nothing was touched**: no band,
no margin, no `route_allowance.py`, no re-pin. Handing the decision up: either the prefill population joins
the floor survey (which may legitimately move F2's `act` tolerance for prefill pins, and nothing else), or
`p300`'s MLP stage is judged by the aggregate rules only. Until then that one command is red, and
`engine/results.md` says so here rather than in a TODO.

### What a worker ran on the device, and what it did not

Correctness-only, each under 60 s wall, one at a time, under `tools/gpu_lock.sh`, **no timing taken** and no
number here belongs in `bench/performance-status.md`. Util was the usual contended 92–94 % at acquire and
7–36 % at release; per `bench/performance-status.md` §6 it is recorded and discounted.

```
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire … ; python engine/gate.py --fixture {dec8,dec64f,dec64t,dec300f,mt32f}   # 119/601/598/1107/342, 0 failures
python prototype/glue/run.py --batched p64      # 4.0 s, rc 0
python prototype/glue/run.py --batched p300     # 4.1 s, rc 0
python prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py --tokens p64  --no-time --tile 0   # 14.3 s, rc 0
python prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py --tokens p300 --no-time --tile 0   # 15.6 s, rc 1 (the one line above)
python prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py --negative    --no-time --tile 0   # 10.8 s, rc 0
```

Two things worth keeping. `mlp_gemv/run.py --no-time` **does not** suppress the cold-check burst, because that
block is gated by `--tile`, not by `--no-time` — a first pass at these gates ran the tile path and printed ms
figures; they are not reported anywhere and are not ledger material, and the correctness-only form is
`--no-time --tile 0`. And the two probes that produced the three controls above are throwaways under `/tmp`
(reproduce with `prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py --tokens p300` plus a single-row `--mode gateup` call on
`L03.mlp.in` row 32); they are not in the tree because nothing about them needs to persist.

**Not run by a worker, by rule:** any timing, the TTFT curve, and every number that would enter the ledger
(`bench/performance-status.md` §6). The command for the main agent's timing pass is the same
`engine/build/decode_run … --wave-rows {512,1}` pair used above, run blocking with zero subagents; the launch
counts above are counts precisely so that they stay valid whatever that pass measures.

**Open after P4b.** (1) The `p300` F2 decision above. (2) GDN conv/gates and the nine projections are still
one row at a time inside the wave — P5/P6, and (h)'s counters will show them the same way. (3) Generation is
still one token per wave by construction, so the whole-run ratio (12 513 vs 41 031) is much flatter than the
prompt ratio; shortening it is P9's argument, not this one. (4) `wide520f` still cannot exercise a wave: the
512-lane attention ceiling is P7, and `kMaxWaveRows = 512` is a scratch-sizing constant, not the attention
cap — the two are unrelated and both are named.

## Q5 / P5: the nine projections batch over the wave, and the batched form is the only form

P4b left item (2) open, and this closes the projection half of it. `submit_wave_body` had been launching
`in_proj_qkv` 295 times for layer 0 of a 295-token wave; it now launches it once, reading the `[n, 1024]`
norm tile and writing `[n, 6144]`, and the per-token mixer reads row `t` of what it wrote. Same for `q/k/v/o`
and `out_proj`. §06.2's "one GEMM per projection" is what the plan asked for and what the counters now prove.

**One kernel body, at two tile widths.** `proj_gemv(weight, x, out, rows, in, tokens)` instantiates only
`proj_gemv_tokens<IN, TPB, false>`: `TPB = 4` at `in = 1024`, `2` at `2048` (16 KiB of shared per block, the
budget `prototype/mlp_gemv` settled on), and `TPB = 1` when the wave has one token — which is the k=1 launch
shape and the k=1 arithmetic. That is a deliberate difference from `kernels_mlp.cpp`, whose launcher branches
between a batched kernel and a single-token kernel and therefore has two bodies to keep honest. Here decode
and prefill share one, so there is no possibility of the decode path being the one that was never measured.
`planned_launch_counts()` still says `proj=114`, and section (d) is untouched: 114 is launches per BODY at
any width, which is exactly what it always meant at n = 1.

**Counted, on both widths.** A fourth family in `engine/launch_stats.{h,cpp}` — `LAUNCHSTAT family=proj` —
recorded after the launch and on the one-token branch too, because the failure this instrument exists to catch
is a wave that quietly took the one-token path, and that is invisible unless the one-token path reports itself.
Section (h) gained `proj` in the counted families plus two explicit checks, and the arithmetic needed nothing
new: `wave_expect`'s per-body bill of 114 is correct for a body of 295 tokens and for a body of one.

**The A/B, counted** (`dec300f`, 295-token prompt + 128 steps, `--wave-rows 512` vs `1`):

| | batched wave | `--wave-rows 1` | the count, not a duration |
|---|---|---|---|
| proj, whole run | **14 706** calls, 114 batched, rows_max **295** | **48 222** calls, 0 batched, rows_max **1** | `rows_total` **48 222 both sides** |
| proj, prompt wave alone | 114 | 33 630 | **295× fewer** launches for a 295-token prompt |
| glue+proj+MLP, prompt wave | 211 | 62 245 | 295×; whole run 27 219 vs 89 253 — 128 generation steps are one token wide either way |
| sampled ids / logits | 423 rows | 423 rows | ids equal, **423 files, 420 157 440 bytes, 0 differing** |

`dec8` (17-token prompt) reads 211 vs 3587 prompt-wave launches, **17×**, `rows_total` 2850 both sides; the
ratio is the prompt length on every fixture, because a wave costs 211 glue+proj+MLP launches however wide it
is — 97 of them were P4's and 114 are this one's.

**Five fixtures, margin preflight (§6.0), before against after.** The deficit is the gate's own per-step `cos`
deficit; "filled" is that deficit over the allowance the step is judged against (the flat G5 floor for `dec8`,
which prices no route allowance). The binding step is the largest ratio, and it is the same step and the same
bits on both sides:

| fixture | worst cos step | deficit / allowance | filled | before → after | worst meanrel step | filled |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dec8` | 7 (flat G5) | 3.2365e−05 / 5.0000e−04 | 0.0647 | **x1.0000** | 7 (flat G5) | 0.1460 → **x1.0000** |
| `dec64f` | 32 | 2.1555e−03 / 2.4844e−03 | 0.8676 | **x1.0000** | 54 | 0.7962 → **x1.0000** |
| `dec64t` | 54 | 2.3281e−04 / 2.3322e−04 | 0.9982 | **x1.0000** | 4 | 0.9789 → **x1.0000** |
| `dec300f` | **86** | 4.6322e−04 / 4.6358e−04 | **0.9992** | **x1.0000** | 72 | 0.9997 → **x1.0000** |
| `mt32f` | 19 | 3.7257e−04 / 3.7358e−04 | 0.9973 | **x1.0000** | 3 | 0.9594 → **x1.0000** |

The plan's risk case was `dec300f` at step 86 — 0.08 % of allowance left, the thinnest margin in the set, and
the one place where a projection batch that legitimately moved one bit could have turned the fixture red. It
did not move: the step, the deficit and the meanrel are equal to five significant figures, because the batched
form's output is the per-row form's bits (138 400 elements per fixture, 0 differing, `prototype/proj_gemv/run.py
--tokens`) and not merely close. Check counts after the package: **124 / 606 / 603 / 1112 / 347**, zero
failures — the +5 per fixture over P4b is section (h)'s two new `proj` checks, its `both sides cover the same
proj rows`, and the two explicit batched/one-row projection checks.

The table is `tools/margin_delta.py <before-prefix> <after-prefix> dec8 dec64f dec64t dec300f mt32f` reading
the two sets of gate logs; it is committed so that the "filled" column is re-derivable rather than remembered.

**Why the scratch grew, and by how much.** The projections' inputs and outputs became row-family buffers
(`a_q` 4096, `a_k`/`a_v` 512, `g_raw`(+F32) 6144, `g_z` 2048, `g_a`/`g_b`(+F32) 16, `block_out`(+F32) 2048,
per wave row): ≈31 MiB per request at `kMaxWaveRows = 512`, on top of P4's ≈18 MiB. Against a 1.55 GB
checkpoint and a ~34 GiB device-visible pool that is AGENTS.md's "capacity is not the constraint for this
model", stated rather than discovered. What did NOT grow is the mixer's own scratch (`g_u`, `g_o`, `g_g`,
`g_beta`, `conv_new`, the attention stage buffers) — those are produced and consumed inside one token's mixer
call, and a token axis for them would be unused memory.

### What a worker ran on the device (correctness only, no timing taken)

`prototype/proj_gemv/run.py` (self-locking, run without holding `tools/gpu_lock.sh`): baseline `--negative`
PASS on the pre-P5 binary, then post-change `--negative` (136 comparisons, 0 failures, all eleven negatives
caught) and `--tokens p64` / `--tokens p300`, both PASS. `engine/gate.py --selftest --dry-run` (CPU): all
needles, including the two grown ones. `engine/gate.py --fixture {dec8, dec64f, dec64t, dec300f, mt32f}`: all
five PASS, 3 / 5 / 6 / 15 / 8 s wall respectively, `gpu_lock.sh util` 47–61 % at the time of each. **No timing
taken and none of the above is a duration** — the gate's own `ms_per_step` by-product line is not quoted here
for that reason.

**Not run by a worker, by rule:** any timing, the GB/s of the batched form, the `TPB` sweep, and every number
that would enter `bench/performance-status.md`. The commands for that pass: `engine/build/decode_run …
--wave-rows {512,1}` (the pair above), and for the kernel family `prototype/proj_gemv/run.py --mode burst
--plan … --tokens 295 --tpb {1,2,4}` — `Job::bytes()` already divides weight bytes per TILE, which is the
divisor that makes a batched GB/s mean anything. Two things that pass are worth pricing there: `TPB` above the
16 KiB shared budget (8 rows at `in = 1024` wants 32 KiB and halves occupancy), and the `in = 2048` side,
where `o_proj`/`out_proj` take `TPB = 2` while every 1024-wide projection takes 4.

**Open after P5.** (1) `TPB` is a budget choice, not a measurement (above). (2) `in_proj_a`/`in_proj_b` are
16-row launches and still separate ones; packing them is the ≈0.12 ms/token plan change `results.md` already
names, not this one. (3) The per-row pin judging for the batched form lives in the prototype gate; the engine's
own equivalence is carried by the logits `cmp` and the five-fixture margins. (4) GDN conv/gates/rec and the
attention row are still one token at a time inside the wave — P6/P7's, and (h)'s counters will show them the
same way once they batch.

## Q5 / P6: the GDN mixer's three token-parallel launches batch over the wave, and the carried conv window is proven

P5 left the mixer itself running one token at a time inside `submit_wave_body`: conv, gates, recurrence
and gated norm, four launches per GDN layer per token, 72 per token, plus a D2D copy of the layer's
conv window per token. Three of those four are token-parallel — the conv is causal *within* the tile
and reads the window the last wave left, the gates are per (token, head), and §04.5's norm is per
(token, head) — so they now go **once per GDN layer per wave**, and the window copy goes once per wave
instead of once per token: 18 copies per wave where a 295-token wave used to pay 5 310. The fourth,
`rec_kernel` (§04.4's scan), stays per token by design; giving it a width is P9's chunked form.

`gdn_conv/gdn_gates/gdn_gated_norm` take a `tokens` argument (`engine/kernels.h`), the kernels live in
`prototype/gdn_decode/gdn_decode_kernels.h` and
`prototype/gdn_gated_norm/gdn_gated_norm_kernels.h` and are gated there, and `g_u`/`g_o`/`g_g`/`g_beta`
moved from one-row scratch into the wave row family (`decode_step.h`'s note says which buffers are in
which family and why `conv_new` is in neither). At `tokens == 1` every one of the three is the same
grid it was, which is why the decode path's five fixtures are bit-identical below.

**Counted, like P4/P5.** `engine/launch_stats.{h,cpp}` gained `gdn_conv`, `gdn_gates` and
`gdn_gated_norm` — one launch per GDN layer per body whatever the body's width, so section (h)'s
existing per-body arithmetic prices a wave of them with no new formula. `gdn_rec` is deliberately
absent: its launcher has no row width, and a family that can only ever report `rows=1` is a check that
cannot fail. On `dec64f` (17-token prompt + 64 steps): batched side `calls=1170, calls_batched=18,
rows_total=1458, rows_max=17`; one-row side `1458 / 0 / 1458 / 1`; carry side (below)
`1224 / 72 / 1458 / 5`. Fewer launches, identical work — `rows_total` equal on every side.

**The window is now carried across a wave boundary in a shipped gate.** That is the one thing a
512-row wave cannot show: every shipped fixture is a single wave, so the window is read once at the
zeros a request starts on and written once, and every bug in the carry is invisible. Section (h) now
runs a **third side at `--wave-rows 5`** and requires (a) its counters to match §07's arithmetic for
waves of 5, (b) the three GDN families to report `rows_max=5` there — so a window really was carried —
and (c) its logits to be **byte-identical** to the single-wave run. It is not vacuous by construction:
the check refuses a side that produced one wave.

| fixture | interior wave boundaries crossed at `--wave-rows 5` | logits A/B against the one-wave side |
|---|---|---|
| `dec64f` | 3 (4 waves over a 17-token prompt) | 81 files, 80 455 680 B, **0 differing** |
| `dec64t` | 5 (6 waves / 27) | 91 files, 90 388 480 B, **0 differing** |
| `mt32f` | 46 (47 waves / 232) | 264 files, 262 225 920 B, **0 differing** |
| `p128` | 25 (26 waves / 128) | 136 files, 135 086 080 B, **0 differing** |
| `dec300f` | 58 (59 waves / 295) | 423 files, 420 157 440 B, **0 differing** |

5 is chosen because it divides none of the fixtures' prompt lengths, so the prompt end lands mid-chunk
rather than on a boundary the wide run already covers. Per-op, the same claim is made at kernel level in
`prototype/gdn_decode/check.py --tokens`, which compares the window's *contents* to `raw[T−3..T−1]` and
shows that a zero-padded carry moves exactly rows 0–2 of the following wave.

**Five fixtures, margin preflight (§6.0), before against after** (`tools/margin_delta.py`):

| fixture | checks before → after | worst cos step | filled | before → after | worst meanrel | filled |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dec8` | 124 → **161** | 7 (flat G5) | 0.0647 | **x1.0000** | 7 (flat G5) | 0.1460 → **x1.0000** |
| `dec64f` | 606 → **643** | 32 | 0.8676 | **x1.0000** | 54 | 0.7962 → **x1.0000** |
| `dec64t` | 603 → **640** | 54 | 0.9982 | **x1.0000** | 4 | 0.9789 → **x1.0000** |
| `dec300f` | 1112 → **1149** | 86 | 0.9992 | **x1.0000** | 72 | 0.9997 → **x1.0000** |
| `mt32f` | 347 → **384** | 19 | 0.9973 | **x1.0000** | 3 | 0.9594 → **x1.0000** |

Every margin is x1.0000 at the same binding step and the same deficit to five significant figures: the
wide GDN launches produce the per-token launches' bits, not approximations of them. The check count
rose by 37 per fixture — the three GDN families' counters on three sides, their six explicit
batched/one-row shape checks, the carry side's own counters and anti-vacuity check, and the byte A/B.
`p128` (153 → 190) and the pre-existing `chunk64b` failures (the prompt-end sample `1596` vs the pin's
`760`, and step 0's tie) are unchanged: same four lines before and after, so nothing here made them
better or worse.

**(g) did not move either.** Every conv/state distance line in every fixture log is identical before

No tolerance, band or allowance was touched, and no fixture was re-pinned.

**What a worker ran on the device (correctness only, no timing taken).**

The commands for that pass are the two `decode_run --wave-rows {512,1}` sides and
