attention block, which is still one token wide (P7/P8).

## Q5 / P6b: D-7 is refuted — the prefill row was pinned all along, and one red line cannot be satisfied

**One sentence:** the bare `logits_last.npy` is the prefill row on every `--decode N` fixture, so
`chunk64b`'s four red lines were never a missing pin — one of them is an exact BF16 tie the shipped
policy already covers but was never asked about (D-8, now fixed), one is a pinned winner 2 BF16 ULP
ahead of the runner-up against an engine element error larger than that margin, which no legal bf16
route can satisfy as shipped (D-9, left red on purpose), and `wide520f`'s two are the 512-lane ceiling
that P7 exists to remove; **nothing was re-pinned, no band or margin was touched, and the engine's
prefill is not wrong for a ragged prompt end.**

**Why the pin is the prefill row.** `tools/reference_dump.py::Recorder.pin` never prefixes
`logits_last.npy`, and it refuses to let `--logit-steps` drop it — its own comment says §06.5's `y0`
needs it, and a fixture without it is unpairable. Every manifest in `data/refs/` declares
`logits_last: {step: "prefill"}`, and section (0)'s pairing check PASSes on both disputed fixtures:
`argmax(bare logits_last.npy) == greedy_continuation[0]`, 760 vs 760 on `chunk64b`. §10.5's
last-writer-wins trap is real but belongs to `input_ids` and `pre_final_hidden`, which the decode hooks
re-pin unprefixed; `logits_last` is written once, prefill-side. So the prescribed remedy — re-pin under
a distinct name, or skip `y0` loudly — had no premise: a skip would have skipped a pin that exists and
passes, which is the silent skip this file refuses everywhere else.

**`chunk64b`'s four lines, decomposed.**

| the line | what it actually is | disposition |
|---|---|---|
| `the prompt's last token samples greedy_continuation[0]` — 1596 vs 760 | the pinned prefill row is an **exact 2-way BF16 tie**: ids 760 and 1596 both 19.875, payload `0x419f`. The engine's own logit at 1596 is 19.815098, which rounds back to `0x419f`. This is D-1's TIE POLICY case exactly — but §06.5's row was judged by a bare `== greedy[0]` that never called `tie_verdict`, the only judged step with that treatment | **D-8, fixed**: `y0` now goes through `tie_verdict(PREFILL_ROW, …)`. The line reads PASS with a TIE above it |
| `generation step 0 samples greedy_continuation[1]` — 20619 vs 8337 | the pin puts 8337 at 15.625 and 20619 at 15.500 — a gap of 0.125 = **2 BF16 ULP** at that magnitude — while the engine's maxabs at that step is **0.2509**. Its logits pass the band (cos 0.999919, deficit 8.059e−05 against G5's 5e−04, meanrel 1.199 %) | **D-9, left red**: an admitting rule needs a sized denominator (D-6's family). Recorded in `EXPECTED_RED`, not skipped, not tolerated |
| `all 30 generated ids match, in order` | the consequence of step 0 — one shifted sample mismatches the whole order-sensitive list (`unmatched steps [0]`, every other id equal) | follows step 0 |
| `step 0: argmax agrees with the pin` | same as line 2, seen from section (b) | follows step 0 |

The FAIL line that produced D-7 was itself a bug and is fixed separately: section (b) printed *"the pin
is tied but the engine's id is not one of the tied ids"* for **every** unadmitted argmax failure, tied or
not — an assertion the code never evaluated, on the step whose pin has a single winner by 2 ULP. It now
prints what was measured (top1/top2 and their values, the gap in BF16 ULP, the engine's values at both,
and whether its element error exceeds the margin), and `--selftest` requires the negation of the old
string on an untied step. A gate line that asserts an unchecked fact is worse than a silent gate: this
one bought a whole wrong diagnosis.

**The engine's prefill is not wrong.** Against the pins — the engine's dumped row
`tmp/engine_gate/logits119.bin` (F32, the `--prefill-logits last` dump of the wave's last row) against
`data/refs/chunk64b/logits_last.npy` widened from its BF16 payload; a probe, not a gate check:
prefill row cos **0.999893** (deficit 1.07e−04, meanrel 1.57 %), step 0 cos 0.999919, step 1
cos 0.999908 — every one inside §10.1 G5's 5e−04 floor; 29 of the 30 pinned generation samples reproduce
exactly, and the 30th is step 0 above. The only token that differs after the prompt is one the reference
had not decided, and the one before it is a proved tie. This is the same conclusion §10.4 and A-18 reach
from the other direction: bf16 token equality dies at a near-tie, and a near-tie at 2 ULP is inside the
legal error budget of any route.

**`wide520f` never reaches `y0`.** Its two failures are `the planned run fits the attention stage's
lane ceiling` (500 prompt + 24 generation = **524 lanes vs `MAXSC = 512`**) and the `decode_run exits 0
— rc=1` that follows from `kernels_attn.cpp` refusing lane 513. That is P7's package, not a fixture
defect and not an expected-red line: the fixture is inert until the split-K route lands, exactly as P3
recorded. **Seven green is therefore today's ceiling**, and the eight-fixture table below is the agreed
acceptance shape: seven PASS, `chunk64b` red on its three recorded lines, `wide520f` pending P7.

**Eight fixtures, re-run against this tree** (`engine/build.sh` re-run first; correctness only, **no
timing taken** — the `ms/step` the gate prints is a by-product and is not a measurement, and these ran
with the GPU busy serving this session, `util` recorded as read and discounted as usual):

| fixture | checks | verdict | wall | `util` at run |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dec8` | 161 | PASS | 2 s | 93 % |
| `dec64f` | 643 | PASS — 2 steps reported TIE (17, 18) | 7 s | 95 % |
| `dec64t` | 640 | PASS | 7 s | 95 % |

| `mt32f` | 384 | PASS | 11 s | 95 % |

`chunk64b`'s count moved 363/4 → 365/3: +2 checks (the prefill row's tie-policy check and its id-line

**(g) conv_fp, engine against pin, before P6 against now.** The pre-P6 logs are the P5-era pass the P6

So P6's numerics claim survives re-examination from the side the P6 section did not cover: `p128` and

**Coverage holes this pass found, named rather than papered over.**

* **Only `chunk64b` exercises a tied prefill row.** The other seven have single winners at `y0` with
  gaps from 0.5 (wide520f) to 5.25 (dec8), so `--y0-alt` — the admitting half of D-8 — can only be
  exercised on that one fixture. `--selftest` prints a `[SKIP]` naming the fixture that can run it,
  rather than pretending coverage.
* **A pinned tie at `y0` is luck, not design.** P3 pinned `chunk64b` for the 64-chunk carry; nobody chose
  it for its tie. If D-8's admitting path is to stay covered, a future fixture needs a prompt end the
  reference genuinely does not decide, and that is a dumper decision, not a gate one.
* **`wide520f` cannot be gated at all until P7**, so the >512-lane boundary P3 pinned for remains
  unexercised on device — unchanged from P3's own note.
* **The gate never compares the prefill row's logits vector to the pin**, only its argmax. Section (b)
  starts at the first decode step. The cos figures quoted above came from a probe over the dumps, so they
  are not regression-protected. Whether `y0`'s vector should be judged (and at which floor — the prefill
  row has no predecessor to cascade from) is a band question for the main agent, not a change to make
  from here.

**What a worker ran on the device (correctness only, no timing taken).** `engine/build.sh`, then
`engine/gate.py --fixture {dec8, dec64f, dec64t, mt32f, p128, chunk64b, dec300f, wide520f}`, each inside
its own `tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q5p6b` / `release q5p6b` pair (the gate does **not** self-lock), `util`
reading 93–95 % throughout that pass — and a confirmation re-run of `chunk64b` against the final tree
read `util` 4 % and reproduced the same 365 PASS / 3 FAIL, so the counts are not a contention
artifact. Walls 2/7/7/19/11/7/7/2 s — **counts, never durations, and none of these is a duration.**
CPU-side: `engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture {dec8, chunk64b}` (both SELFTEST PASS, 22 s on
`chunk64b`), including the two new cases `--break-y0` (must FAIL, all fixtures) and `--y0-alt` (must be
ADMITTED, `chunk64b` only), and the updated `--break-logits 2` needles.

**Handed to the main agent.** The eight gate commands above are also the timing pass's starting point;
nothing here needs a re-run for correctness. If a margin preflight for `p128`/`chunk64b` is wanted
against a true pre-P6 binary, that needs a `ccbfbff` worktree build (the two fixtures have no pre-P6
device log), which is a main-agent device-window decision. `speccheck/` was not touched and was not run:
nothing in this pass can reach `speccheck/specimpl.py` or a gate file other than `engine/gate.py`.


## Q5 / P7: the `MAXSC` wall is gone — attention takes the streaming form above the staging, and the
## first >512-lane fixture prices its own margin

**What the wall was.** `attn_decode_layer` threw on `lvalid > MAXSC` (512), and `engine/gate.py` refused
the same arithmetic in its own preflight, so `wide520f` — 500 prompt + 24 generated = **524 lanes** —
exited 1 before a single STEP line. Its y0, id, logits, `(g)` and `(h)` rows had never been judged at all.
§07.3 had already decided the replacement and the prototype had already shipped it: `attn_core`'s streaming
`(max,sum)` branch (`prototype/attn_decode/attn_decode_kernels.h:282-305`) and `attn_combine` (`:324`),
the split-K merge with the same rescaling algebra. **P7 is selection, not arithmetic: no kernel line
changed.**

**The rule the launcher applies now.** Per row, by necessity and nothing else:

| lanes in the row | form | why |
|---|---|---|
| `lvalid ≤ MAXSC` | `full=1, split=1`, grid `(N_Q, 1)` | §07.3's decision, bit-exact with the pins, and the route every priced fixture rides. One-pass is the default, never the fallback. |
| `lvalid > MAXSC` | `full=0`, `split = ceil(lvalid / MAXSC)`, grid `(N_Q, split)` + the merge launch | there is no other way to be correct: `full=1` past the staging would make `attn_core` write past `sc[]` |

`ceil` is load-bearing in both directions: it is what guarantees no block is handed more lanes than the
staging width the split was derived from (`ceil(n / ceil(n/s)) ≤ s`), and it is what keeps a 513-lane row
at `split = 2` instead of paying for a split it does not need. One-pass cannot be split at any tile size —
it normalises the weight matrix inside one block (§05.5), which is why the prototype refuses
`--softmax full --split > 1` outright — so the second row of that table is not a tuning choice.

**The ceiling is now a product, and each factor lives where it is obeyed.** The op owns the staging width
(`MAXSC`, a `#define` in `attn_decode_kernels.h`, read by `#include`); the launcher owns the blocks per
row (`kAttnMaxSplit = 8` in `engine/kernels_attn.cpp`), because that constant exists to size an allocation,
not just to refuse. Ceiling = **512 × 8 = 4096 lanes**, which is deliberately the same number as
`decode_run`'s `kSessionDefaultCap`: the attention route now covers exactly the context the engine hands
out by default, and past it `attn_decode_layer` throws rather than writing past a stage. That is a real
boundary, stated plainly: an 8k request is refused today. §07.3's "the engine's shape" sentence covers
both halves now, and raising the cap means raising `attn_part_floats()` with it — which is why the split cap
lives in the file that sizes the scratch.

**The partial scratch is reduction state, so it is scratch.** `attn_part_floats()` =
`N_Q × kAttnMaxSplit × (DH + 2)` F32 = 8 × 8 × 258 × 4 B = **64.5 KiB per in-flight token**, allocated once
in `DecodeScratch` (`a_part`) and freed with it, never per step. It is *not* §08.1 state and does not go in
`RequestState`: `(max, sum, Σ w·v)` for one query row is born and dies inside one attention call, exactly
the A-17 argument `c_dummy` is built on, and the size difference is the punchline — 64.5 KiB against
`RequestState`'s measured 18.66 MiB. The cost of getting it wrong is silent: `attn_core` with `split > 1`
writes **only** `part`, so a launcher that set the flag and passed a null buffer would produce a quiet
no-op instead of an error. Hence the second refusal, `part == nullptr` on a forced-split row.

**Proof the online route actually ran, at engine level.** Three independent things, none of them a timing:

1. `wide520f`'s 12 steps above the staging (decode12…decode23, 513…524 lanes) went from *never executed* to
   *judged and exact*: all 24 generated ids match the pins in order, 0 steps needed the tie policy, and the
   worst logits deficit over the whole chain is 1.14e-04 at step 17.
2. A launcher that skipped the split could not produce that result at all. There is no other way to serve
   513 lanes: `full=1` there is undefined behaviour on a 512-float shared stage, and `part` is the only
   output the streaming branch has — if `attn_combine` were not launched, `a_pre`/`a_gated` would keep the
   previous layer's scratch and every token after the crossing would be wrong. The route cannot be dead
   code while the fixture is green.
3. The prototype's own anti-vacuity (its results.md §10) is a *failing* check, not a printf: an
   `--softmax online` run whose reduction stages come back bit-identical to the pins exits 1, and this run
   moved **67 of 68 cases** against the pins on at least one softmax stage (the 68th is a single-lane row,
   where there is nothing to merge). `--negative` proves the same thing from the other side: `nomax` exists
   only in the online branch and is injected at the run's own split size.

**`wide520f`, before and after** (device, correctness only, no timing taken):

| | before P7 | after P7 |
|---|---|---|
| preflight | refused: 524 lanes vs staging 512 | `PASS` — "524 lanes vs staging MAXSC 512 x kAttnMaxSplit 8 = 4096 lanes" |
| `decode_run` | rc=1, no dumps | rc=0, 524 steps' dumps judged |
| verdict | 78 PASS / **2 FAIL** | **320 PASS / 0 FAIL** |
| route allowance | none possible | its own, derived (below) |

**The shipped fixtures did not move — which is the D-2 condition, tested rather than asserted.** None of
them ever crossed 512 lanes, so none of them can see the new branch; the way to show that is to re-run all
of them and diff. Same check counts (`dec8` 161, `dec64f` 643, `dec64t` 640, `mt32f` 384, `dec300f` 1149,
`p128` 190, `chunk64b` 365 PASS / 3 FAIL), and `tools/margin_delta.py` before-vs-after reads **x1.0000**
filled ratio with the same binding steps and the same worst-deficit bits on every one — `dec300f` step 86
still 4.6322e-04 of its 4.6358e-04 allowance, the tightest margin on the tree. Stronger than the ratios:
md5 over every step's metrics / argmax / `(g)` state-fingerprint line is byte-identical before and after,
per fixture. No fixture was re-pinned, no `BAND` constant in `engine/gate.py` moved, and `--selftest`
(33 injections) is green.

**`wide520f` priced its own margin, per D-2** — never a hand-written number:

```
engine/route_price.py --fixture wide520f --engine-log <gate log> --out tmp/route_wide520f.json
engine/route_allowance.py --table tmp/route_wide520f.json
```

worst engine/route 1-cos ratio **2.11916 at step 17** → `margin 2.12`; worst meanrel ratio **0.38352 at
step 14** → `margin_rel 0.39`; both `event: applied` in `bench/band-history.jsonl` under decision **D-2**
("every fixture prices its own margin"). `route_price.py` also reports 24/24 argmax agreement for *both*
legal reference forms against the pinned chain, and 0 of 24 steps under G5 on either side, so the
allowance is admitting a spread, not pardoning a divergence. It lands at 2.12 against D-3's already-priced
envelope for the online-softmax axis (≤ 0.41 added, `M_wide ≤ 2.40`) — a consistency check on the
derivation, not the reason for it. The four priced fixtures are named by the tool as "not touched by this
run, and structurally cannot be", and no existing margin grew, so §6.2's stop condition never fired.
Binding step is 17 at 0.9996 of its own cap: the self-reference of a derived margin, which the tool's own
docstring names, now visible on a green run.

**Section `(h)`: attention is not a row-batching site, and the counter would not have caught anything
here.** `(h)` counts *token rows per launch* for the four families that can batch over the wave
(`glue/proj/mlp/gdn`), and the harness's own `attn(rows)` placeholder says the attention launcher does not
batch over token rows — that is P8. P7 cut a different axis: `split` divides the **lane** axis inside one
query row, so there is no row width to report and the `(h)` schema would be a lie if it printed one. The
reason `(h)` exists at all — an *optional* batching path that could be dead code because the per-row path
produces identical bits — cannot arise here: the split is mandatory above 512 and produces deliberately
different bits, so its absence is a wall, not a silent no-op. `(h)` is therefore unchanged and still green
(its `wide520f`/`chunk64b` rows pass, all seven counters over the four families `glue`/`proj`/`mlp`/`gdn`
still matching the wave arithmetic on all three sides); the lane ceiling is enforced by
`lane_geometry()`, which greps both factors out of the files that obey them, and by the launcher's throw.
The lane-ceiling check is also not the anti-vacuity proof — it proves a *capacity* bound, not a route; the
route's own proof is the falsifiability of that table above and the prototype's failing anti-vacuity check.

**What a worker ran on the device** (correctness only, no timing taken; each inside its own
`tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q5p7` / `release q5p7` pair, the gate does not self-lock, and
`prototype/attn_decode/run.py` self-locks so the lock was released before it):

```
engine/build.sh                                              # clean
prototype/attn_decode/run.py --softmax online --split 2 --long --negative   # 1380 comparisons, 0 fail
prototype/attn_decode/run.py --softmax online --split 4 --long --negative   # 1380 comparisons, 0 fail
prototype/attn_decode/run.py --long --negative                              # 1764, 0 fail (pinned route)
engine/gate.py --fixture wide520f                # 78/2 before P7, 320/0 after, util 50-58 %, ~18 s
engine/gate.py --fixture {dec8,dec64f,dec64t,mt32f,dec300f,p128,chunk64b}   # counts unchanged
engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture dec8         # SELFTEST PASS
```

`util` read 49–58 % on those runs and is reported as read. No `ms/step`, no bandwidth, no burst: the
decode-vs-context row §07.4 needs is the main agent's, in a window with zero subagents
(`bench/performance-status.md` §6). The gate prints `decode_run reported 2.353 ms/step` on `wide520f` as
a by-product; it is not a measurement and does not enter the ledger.

**Handed to the main agent.** (1) The timing row, on the two commands §07.4 names — `wide520f` now runs,
which is the whole point of the package. (2) If the 4096-lane ceiling should move, the two coupled knobs
are `kAttnMaxSplit` and `attn_part_floats()`, and `gate.py::lane_geometry()` follows them automatically.
(3) `chunk64b`'s three D-9 lines are untouched by P7 and remain the tree's only red. (4) `speccheck/` was
neither touched nor run: nothing here can reach `specimpl.py` or a spec gate.

---

## Q5 / P8 (engine half, P8b): attention leaves the token loop — one site per attention layer for
## the whole wave, and the null read pointer that looked like a device hang

**What landed.** `submit_wave_body`'s mixer no longer loops tokens for attention: `if (attn_layer)`
calls `kernels::attn_decode_layer` once with `nrows = n`, and only the GDN recurrence stays a
per-token loop, because a sequential scan cannot be hoisted and attention can (§06.3, plan §2.3). The
`[n][2048]` widening over `a_gated` is the same elementwise argument P5 used around the projections. The
stage buffers `a_q_rope`/`a_k_rope`/`a_gate`/`a_pre`/`a_gated` became `[wave_rows]` tiles and `a_part` is
`attn_part_floats(wave_rows)` — 32.25 MiB at `kMaxWaveRows`, 64.5 KiB at 1 — because at a wave the
partial staging is one row family like every other one. `decode_step.h`'s two-family note now says what
is genuinely still one row wide (`conv_new`), which P8's own comment had left stale.

**The launcher chooses a plan by row count, not by lane depth.** One row → the fused single `attn_core`
launch P7 shipped, bit-for-bit where it was. A wave → `attn_prep` (norm, rope, gate extract **and the
§05.7 append** for every row at its own position), then `attn_core` with `fused = 0`, then `attn_combine`
when the widest row needs more than one lane block. That is `prototype/attn_decode` §11's conclusion,
not a preference: in the fused form each core block appends only its own row's lane and blocks across the
row axis have no ordering, so a fused wave is "a race that usually works — the worst kind". A row's lane
route is its own (`blocks_for_row`), so one grid legitimately carries one-pass rows and streaming rows
together; `split` still comes from the wave's widest row and is capped by `kAttnMaxSplit` so `part`
cannot be written past.

**The hang, and what it actually was: a null read pointer, not a deadlock.** The parked wiring
(`stash@{0}`, decision log D-10) hung on `p128` — `gate.py --fixture p128` busy-polling ~10 cores for
7 minutes with zero logits files written — and its own `--wave-rows 4` repro had left a device dump:
`Memory Fault Error … kernel: attn_core(CoreArgs)`, `grid=[2048,1,4]`, `faulting addr: 0x3000`. Three
device runs, each under a minute, settled it:

1. **Re-break (positive control).** Delete two lines from the launcher — `core.qrope`/`core.krope` left
   null while `core.fused = 0` — rebuild, run the 4-row wave. Reproduced: `attn_core(CoreArgs)` faults
   at `0x1000`, grid `[2048,1,4]`, one logits file missing, and the process still needed killing at 45 s
   *after* printing `decode_run FAILED: hip: an illegal memory access` at `decode_step.cpp`'s D2H.
   The address is the index: in the staged branch `attn_core` reads `a.qrope[ROW_H * qrow + …]`, so a
   null base plus `ROW_H * qrow * 2` bytes names the row — `0x1000` is row 1, the parked run's `0x3000`
   is row 3, and row 0 would have faulted at 0 (also unmapped). **The hanging launch is the wave's
   `attn_core` (`dim3(N_Q, split, nrows)`); the hanging index is `qrow` in `CoreArgs::qrope`.** It reads
   as a hang because the fault is reported asynchronously while the host sits in a stream sync polling a
   queue that will never drain; a faulted context makes teardown spin too, so it never exits by itself.
2. **The stash as preserved does not hang.** `stash@{0}` had already been cut down to a diagnostic that
   left `c.fused = 1` for waves while still launching prep. Run at the real size — a 128-row wave,
   `p128`'s own 128 ids — it exits 0 in ~1 s and its `logits127.bin` is **byte-identical** to the fixed
   staged plan's. So the wave geometry, the tall tiles and the per-row blocking were never the problem;
   the fault lived entirely in the staged branch's unread inputs. (That form is not a fix either: its
   core re-appends each row's lane behind `stage_head`'s `blockIdx.y != 0` test while other rows are
   reading those lanes, so it is correct by value only because prep already wrote the same bytes, and it
   pays the staging and throws the saving away.)
3. **Fix, A/B on one wave.** Same four ids, `--wave-rows 4` vs `--wave-rows 1`: logits bit-identical
   row for row, and the counters move the way §06.4 demands —
   `family=attn calls=12 calls_batched=6 rows_total=30 rows_max=4` against
   `calls=30 calls_batched=0 rows_total=30 rows_max=1`. Same rows covered, quarter of the sites.

**Why no existing gate could see it.** `prototype/attn_decode`'s host sets both halves of the staged
contract in the same block (`attn_decode.hip.cpp:363` writes, `:368` reads), so the op's `--wave` stage
has never launched a staged core without staged inputs. The engine's `CoreArgs c{}` zero-initialises
`qrope`/`krope`, the fused branch — the only branch the engine had until P8 — never reads those fields,
and `attn_prep` does not read `fused`, so nothing anywhere complained until a wave flipped the plan bit.
A wave-only path with a wrong argument list is invisible to every one-row gate, and on this device it
presents as a hang rather than a clean failure.

**`(h)` now counts attention as a fifth batchable family, in sites.** `count_launch("attn", …)` reports
one unit per *site*, with the blocks-per-launch figure in the slot `rows_per_launch()` reads, so
`rows_max` is wave width and `rows_total` stays wave-independent (counting the real 8–10 blocks per launch
would have made `rows_total 816 vs 816` mean different things for different families; `launch_stats.h`
says so). The checks are the usual set: the batched side must show `rows_max == min(prompt_len,
wave_rows)` and the one-row side `0` and `1` (2), `rows_total` parity against each narrow side (2), and the
per-family wave-arithmetic entry on all three sides (3) — **7 new checks per fixture**, which is the audit
trail that the family is judged rather than mentioned.

**The aimed negative, and the limit of what an A/B injection can say here.** `--break-attn-one-row` forces
the attention family alone back to one row per launch on the batched side while every other family still
batches, so the new checks are seen to bite on their own instead of passing on glue's counts (§10.1's
locality rule). It is a *counter* injection, and that is not timidity: a wave-only **value** bug cannot be
expressed in this harness at all, because patching a wrong row into the prompt wave changes `y0`, which
changes the sampled token, which makes every generated step differ — so the only divergence an A/B
injection can produce in attention is the site-level one. The value claim ("a wave's rows are bit-identical
to those rows launched one at a time") is therefore carried by `prototype/attn_decode` §11's direct
measurement — 0 of 2 397 450 elements — and by the eight fixtures' x1.0000 below, not by an injection.
`gate.py --selftest` runs the injection through the same judge as the engine: **SELFTEST PASS**,
`--break-attn-one-row` included.

**Blast radius: zero bits, measured.** All eight fixtures, before-vs-after on the same machine and
sequence (`tools/margin_delta.py /tmp/p8/before /tmp/p8b/after …`):

| fixture | checks before → after | verdict | worst allowance filled |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dec8` | 161 → **168** | PASS | x1.0000 |
| `dec64f` | 643 → **650** | PASS (2 TIE) | x1.0000 |
| `dec64t` | 640 → **647** | PASS | x1.0000 |
| `mt32f` | 384 → **391** | PASS | x1.0000 |
| `dec300f` | 1149 → **1156** | PASS | x1.0000 (step 86, 4.6322e-04 of 4.6358e-04) |
| `p128` | 190 → **197** | PASS | x1.0000 |
| `wide520f` | 320 → **327** | PASS | x1.0000 |
| `chunk64b` | 365 → **372** | FAIL = the 3 expected-red D-9 lines, unchanged | x1.0000 |

Every one of the five originals is bit-identical to its pre-P8 run (`x1.0000`, same binding steps, same
worst-deficit bits), and so are the two boundary fixtures whose decode steps are still one row wide.
Stronger than the ratios: md5 over the `[PASS]/[expected-red] step N: metrics|argmax` lines of each log is
identical before and after, per fixture (`dec8` 8 lines `ef085ecf1fc1`, `dec64f` 64 `0d5ab66c533c`,
`dec64t` 64 `a06450d0c939`, `mt32f` 32 `77f94ec2bb2a`, `dec300f` 128 `e31ebb993c8f`, `p128` 8
`68666617aa9a`, `wide520f` 24 `50e7fc5fa70d`, `chunk64b` 30 `288d4468fe5a`) — every deciding number, tie
label and token id in all eight logs is the same byte of text.
`p128`'s prompt wave is now **6 attention sites / 12 kernel launches where the per-token form issued 768
single-row launches** (`attn calls=54 calls_batched=6 rows_total=816 rows_max=128`), which is the §06.3
claim counted rather than inferred. Nothing was re-pinned, no band moved, no `EXPECTED_RED` entry changed;
`chunk64b`'s three D-9 lines are the tree's only red and are the same three.

**The 4096-lane ceiling was left alone, on purpose.** Raising `kAttnMaxSplit` from 8 to 16 is one line and
costs 32 MiB/request of `part` scratch — trivial against this box, and the arithmetic is written up in
`bench/plan-prefill-2026-10-12.md` §3.1 together with the two constants that must move together
(`kAttnMaxSplit` and `decode_run`'s `kSessionDefaultCap`, which is what makes the ceiling reachable at
all). What makes it *not* cheap-and-provable is that nothing in the tree has more than 524 lanes, so a
>4096-lane check needs a new pin (P3-class work, and D-2 says a new fixture prices its own margin), and
the cheap self-consistency substitute — same rows at two wave widths, byte for byte — is not valid above
the staging: `split` comes from the wave's widest row, so a row can be handed more lane blocks than it
needs and its `(max,sum)` grouping differs from the same row run alone. The precise condition is a
straddle: agreement holds while a wave's lane span contains no multiple of `MAXSC` strictly inside it above
the staging, which is why 64-aligned waves from position 0 (§2.1's rule) always agree — 512 and 1024 land on
wave *endpoints*, never in the middle — and why the 525-lane row checked in `engine/results.md` matches
across three wave widths. A wave that starts off that alignment (a session prompt resumed at a ragged
prefix) and straddles, say, 1024 inside itself hands its lower rows a 3-deep grouping where they would
have used 2, and must be judged against its pin, never against another wave width — which is §2.6's
standing rule said out loud. That is a fact about the design, recorded rather than fixed.

**What a worker ran on the device** (correctness only, **no timing taken**; each inside its own
`tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire`/`release` pair, and the gate does not self-lock):

```
engine/build.sh                                                  # clean
engine/gate.py --fixture p128                    # 197 PASS / 0 FAIL, ~7 s
engine/gate.py --fixture {dec8,dec64f,dec64t,mt32f,dec300f}      # 168/650/647/391/1156, 0 FAIL
engine/gate.py --fixture {wide520f,chunk64b}     # 327 PASS; 372 PASS + the 3 expected reds
engine/gate.py --selftest --fixture {dec8,dec64f}                  # SELFTEST PASS both
decode_run --ids 1,2,3,4 --steps 1 --wave-rows {4,1}               # the A/B; also the re-break repro
decode_run --ids <wide520f prompt + 25 greedy> --steps 0 --wave-rows {512,500,300,5,1}   # mixed grid
tools/margin_delta.py /tmp/p8/before /tmp/p8b/after <8 fixtures>   # CPU: x1.0000 everywhere
```

The re-break is not a permanent knob and does not need one: delete the two `core.qrope`/`core.krope` lines
from `engine/kernels_attn.cpp`'s wave branch, rebuild, and the 4-row command above faults in about a
second and then needs a kill. The 525-id list for the mixed-grid runs is `wide520f`'s `prefill_ids` plus
the first 25 of its `greedy_continuation`, written out with one `json.load` — no fixture was added to make
them.

`util` read 2–92 % across those runs and is reported as read; it is not a measurement input. Timings were
deliberately not taken: the launch-count claim is a counter, and the TTFT/attention curve rows are the
main agent's, in a window with zero subagents (`AGENTS.md`, `bench/performance-status.md` §6).

**Handed to the main agent.** (1) The timing rows P8 was supposed to earn: `p128`/`dec300f` TTFT before
(P5's numbers) and after, and the attention curve plan §5.2 names — this worker took no timings by rule.
(2) `stash@{0}` is now dead weight and its own message is a misdiagnosis ("kernel deadlock"); it can be
dropped once these commits land. (3) If the 4k-context TTFT row is ever wanted, `bench/plan-prefill-2026-
10-12.md` §3.1 is the ceiling debt: a >4096-lane pin first, then `kAttnMaxSplit` and `kSessionDefaultCap`
together. (4) `speccheck/` was neither touched nor run: nothing here reaches `specimpl.py` or a spec gate.

**One shape no shipped fixture reaches, priced by self-consistency instead of a pin.** `prototype/attn_decode`
§11 ends with the honest admission that "no shipped fixture has a wave that straddles `MAXSC`", which
means the engine's mixed grid — one launch carrying rows that fit the one-pass staging *and* rows that
must stream — was gated at the op level but never once run by the engine: `wide520f`'s prompt wave is 500
rows under the staging and everything above 512 lanes on that fixture is a single-row decode step. Taking
`wide520f`'s own 500 prompt ids plus its 25 pinned greedy continuations as one **525-token prompt** does
the job without a new pin (this is a wave-vs-one-row claim, and §11's identity half needs no reference
values to be checkable). Five runs at `--steps 0`, `--wave-rows 512 / 500 / 300 / 5 / 1`; the two that
actually mix are **500 and 300**, because a wave has to straddle the staging *internally* to mix and a
512-row wave cannot — its rows are lanes 1–512 (all under it) and the ragged 13-row tail is lanes 513–525
(all over it). At `--wave-rows 500` the second wave is lanes 501–525: twelve rows the staging finishes and
thirteen that must stream, in one launch.

```
family=attn calls=12   calls_batched=12  rows_total=3150 rows_max=512   # waves 512+13: neither wave mixes
family=attn calls=12   calls_batched=12  rows_total=3150 rows_max=500   # 500+25: lanes 501-525 mix
family=attn calls=12   calls_batched=12  rows_total=3150 rows_max=300   # 300+225: lanes 301-525 mix
family=attn calls=630  calls_batched=630 rows_total=3150 rows_max=5     # 105 waves of 5
family=attn calls=3150 calls_batched=0   rows_total=3150 rows_max=1     # per token, as before
```

`rows_total` 3150 on all five (§06.4: fewer launches, not less work — 3150 = 6 layers × 525 rows), and the
priced row — the last one, at **525 lanes**, above the staging in every run so it takes two lane blocks in
each — is **byte-identical across all five**, `logits524.bin` and `sampled.txt` both. So one wave can
carry one-pass and streaming rows at once, `attn_combine` skipping the rows the staging finished does not
disturb the rows that did not, and the grouping a row sees does not depend on the company it was launched
with. No pin was created, priced or compared against; `data/refs/` is untouched and no band moved. A first
cut of this note credited the 512-row wave with the mixing; it does not mix, and the narrower claim is the
one worth keeping.

## Q7 (2026-10-13): the ~3.2 ms residue is the memory system treating a fresh window poorly, not a per-launch ramp — and the reporting bug was in the ledger sentence, not the instrument

Again no millisecond in this section is this worker's. Every input is already on disk: the stage-clock
family rows are `tmp/q61/stage128_{1,2,3}.log` and `tmp/q61/stage4032_{1,2,3}.log` as the main agent took
them, the isolated-burst rows and the 237 GB/s probe are `bench/performance-status.md`'s Q6 table, and the
byte counts are recomputed from `engine/state.h`. What was added is one CPU-only comparison tool,
`tools/instep_family_budget.py`, which median-merges those logs, re-derives each family's weight bytes
from the shapes the engine compiles with, and prints the three tests below. It launches nothing and takes
no timing — a device run would have made it a second measurement of a quantity that already has three.

**The question Q6.1 left open.** `Σ exec_in_situ = 12.74 ms` against `E_iso = 9.4–9.6 ms` of isolated
family cost is ≈3.2 ms/step unaccounted, and three stories were on the table: **(a)** a per-launch ramp,
where a small launch spends its first tens of microseconds getting started and a burst hides that behind
the previous launch's tail; **(b)** interleaved traffic and locality, where the step's 424 launches across
~1.5 GB leave every launch's pages, DRAM rows and TLB entries cold in a way a single-kernel loop never is;
**(c)** a mispriced denominator, where the rooflines the bursts are compared against are simply wrong.

**(b) wins, and the deciding number is x1.64 against a control of x1.03.** `mlp` runs the same two
kernels, the same grids and the same **11.01 MB per launch** in its own burst and inside a step, and the
two do not agree:

| same launch geometry | in-step | its own burst | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mlp`, 48 launches of 11.01 MB | 88.8 µs/launch = 8.07 µs/MB = **124 GB/s** | 54.0 µs/launch = 4.91 µs/MB = 204 GB/s | **x1.64** |
| `lm_head`, 1 launch of 508.6 MB | 2199.5 µs/launch = 4.32 µs/MB = **231 GB/s** | 2127.0 µs/launch = 4.18 µs/MB = 239 GB/s | **x1.03** |

A ramp belongs to the launch, so a launch-shape story must show up on both sides of that row — it does
not, at identical bytes, identical grid, identical kernel. What differs is only the address history: the
MLP burst re-walks **one layer's weights replicated 6×** (a 132 MB window, re-read every 6 launches —
`prototype/mlp_gemv/results.md` says the quiet part itself: replicas 1–5 "re-read replica 0's
out-of-cache-but-still-cached bytes"), while a token walks **528 MB of distinct MLP weights once and
never returns to them**. `lm_head` is the control that makes that the reading rather than a guess: its
burst window *is* its whole 485 MiB, so it is equally cold in both worlds and its two numbers agree to
3 %. Same story at 4032 lanes (`mlp` 4.302 vs 4.264 ms/step, 122.8 vs 124 GB/s), so the deficit is not a
context-size or state-size effect — it is the weight walk itself.

**Why (a) fails arithmetically, not just by the control.** Per launch, the in-situ deficit over each
family's own roofline is +42.4 µs (`mlp`, 48 launches), +19.9 µs (`proj`, 114) and +53.7 µs (`lm_head`,
1). Those are not flat, so there is no per-launch constant to find; and they are the *wrong sign* for a
ramp that amortizes with size — the launch 46× bigger than the MLP's carries the same per-launch
deficit while its per-MB deficit is 35× smaller (+0.11 vs +3.85 µs/MB). And scale it up: a ramp of
42.4 µs per launch billed across the step's 424 launches is 18.0 ms, in a step that is 12.74 ms of
kernel time. The flat quantity that does fit the two many-launch families is per byte — +3.85 and
+4.87 µs/MB, i.e. both families stream the step's weights at **124 and 110 GB/s against a 237 GB/s
probe** — and the aggregate agrees: 1504 MB of weights over 12.72 ms of `Σ exec_in_situ` is 118 GB/s.

**Why (c) fails: the denominators recompute.** `tools/instep_family_budget.py` re-derives each family's
bytes from `engine/state.h` (it parses the `constexpr int kX = …` lines rather than retyping them, for
the reason `engine/weights_contract.py` gives) and divides by the measured probe: `mlp` 528,482,304 B →
2.230 ms against the ledger's 2.230; `proj` 466,747,392 B → 1.969 against 1.973; `lm_head` 508,559,360 B
→ 2.146 against 2.146. All three inside 0.2 %. (An earlier hand calculation of mine had `proj` 5 % high;
the error was `o_proj`, which is `[1024, 2048]`, not `[1024, 4096]` — the ledger row was right.)

**The residue has two components, and only one of them is launch-shaped.** The `cast` (115 launches,
0.9216 ms), `glue` (49, 0.6888), `copy` (19, 0.2146) and `gdn` (72, 0.6375) families carry essentially no
weight bytes at n = 1, so their 2.46 ms is traffic-free and therefore per-launch: 8–14 µs per launch
in-situ against the dispatch curve's 2.6–3.8 µs, i.e. ~4–11 µs of launch floor each, of which the
instrument's own exposed submission is a part. That is where fewer launches still buys something, and it
is the smaller part. The traffic families' deficit (+2.03 ms `mlp`, +2.27 ms `proj` over roofline, 4.3 ms
in total) is not: merging launches does not change the bytes, and this is the part Q6.1's 3.2 ms actually
consists of. **Consequence, said plainly: the submission-side levers are pointed at the floor families,
not at the residue.**

### What already ships batched, which is why the obvious cheap case was already taken

The named candidate — gate and up both read the same input — has been shipped since P4: `mlp_gateup`
takes both weight pointers and writes the `act` tile in **one** launch, which is why the MLP's bill is
48 launches (gate+up, down) and not 72. P5's `cast_bf16_to_f32_3` likewise already packs three widenings
into one launch (that is the "one packed qkv+a+b" cast row of §07). Nothing in `mlp` or `glue` is left to
consolidate at n = 1.

What is left, in the one-row path, is exactly two groups of independent GEMVs that share one input:

* GDN: `in_proj_qkv`, `in_proj_z`, `in_proj_b`, `in_proj_a` — all four read `sc.norm_f32` and write
  separate tiles, 4 launches per GDN layer = 72 of the 114 proj launches;
* attention: `q_proj`, `k_proj`, `v_proj` — same input, same independence, 18 of the 114.

Both would be bit-identical by construction: a row's dot walks its own `IN = 1024` bytes in the same
uint4 order with the same reduction no matter which other rows share the grid, which is precisely the
argument `bc61dce` used when it moved `proj` to 32 rows/block and came back byte-identical. **No band
question is needed for any of this, and no band was touched.** But neither can be done cheaply: `kernels_proj.cpp`
#includes `prototype/proj_gemv/proj_gemv_kernels.h` directly, so a multi-weight launch form belongs to
that op and to its gate, and taking it moves the gate-policed plan (`gate.py::launch_plan`'s
`proj = 5·n_gdn + 4·n_attn` = 114) together with §07's table — Q6's own deferred "packed multi-weight
projections (114 → 48)" item, costed there at 0.11–0.57 ms/token as "a plan change with a kernel-signature
change and its own gate, not a constant". Spending a plan change plus a spec table plus an op gate on the
lever Q7 just argued is pointed at the wrong 4.3 ms was not mine to decide, so **this pass ships no
engine change**; the measurement that would justify or kill it is pre-registered as M3 below instead.
If the answer is "take it anyway", the A/B and the proof standard are already written down.

### The reporting bug was real, and it was in the ledger sentence

The ledger's Q6.1 note says the stage clock "prints 323 for `stage` but **42 for `kernel`**, where the
spec says per-launch granularity is ~425 waits". It does not print 42. Every kernel-granularity `TOTAL`
row of every log that window produced — `tmp/q61/stage128_{1,2,3}.log`, `tmp/q61/stage4032_{1,2,3}.log`,
`tmp/q61/on{2,3,4}.log`, nine runs — reads `syncs_per_step=425`, and `grep -r "syncs_per_step=" ` over the
whole tree returns exactly two distinct values, 323 and 425. `tools/instep_family_budget.py` prints both
per log, so the claim can be re-checked without a device. 425 is also what the instrument must produce:
`syncs_` counts closed segments (`stage_clock.cpp`'s `close_segment`), kernel granularity opens a segment
per submission, and the per-step bill is 424 launches + the `host_tail` segment. So the field was right,
the subtraction it feeds was never at risk, and the wrong thing was the sentence in
`bench/performance-status.md` — corrected there, with the pointer to this section, in the same pass that
wrote these numbers. `stage`'s 323 is also correct (it is segments, not launches: 424 launches collapse
into 323 family-boundary segments because same-family neighbours share a segment).

### Deliberately left alone

No kernel, launcher, gate, pin, band, margin or fixture was edited; `speccheck/` was not run because
nothing it checks can be affected by a CPU-only arithmetic tool and two prose files. No timing was taken,
so nothing here is a measurement this worker made: `gpu_lock.sh` was never acquired. The prefill wave paths
(P4b/P5/P8), the rows/block choices (P6, `bc61dce`), the attention staging (P8) and the cast set (P5) were
read, not re-derived. `tmp/q61/*.log` are cited as committed evidence and were not regenerated.

### Pre-registered measurements, expectations written before any of them ran

All four belong to the main agent's window: three are timing, and the one that is not (`M0`) is cheap
enough to include for the cross-check it provides. `M1` is the decisive one. Quote medians of three, and
void rather than discount any run with a contender — a contended sync inflates exactly the direction that
would be read as locality (Q6.1's own failure-mode row).

**M0 — CPU-only, re-derive the table this section is made of.** Expectation: reproduces the numbers above
exactly, and the 4032-lane logs show `mlp` within 1 % of the 128-lane row while `attn` grows ~6.5×.

```bash
python3 tools/instep_family_budget.py tmp/q61/stage128_{1,2,3}.log
python3 tools/instep_family_budget.py tmp/q61/stage4032_{1,2,3}.log
```

**M1 — the locality test the story actually predicts: grow the burst's window, change nothing else.**
Same kernel, same grid, same bytes per launch; only the replicated window grows from 132 MB (`--tile 6`,
what the committed 2.594 ms row was taken with) to 528 MB (`--tile 24`, the same size as one token's real
MLP traffic). Pre-registered: **(b)** predicts the per-token MLP figure rises from 2.594 ms toward the
in-step row of **4.26 ms** — call it confirmed at ≥3.6 ms — because the burst loses the warmth that the
story blames; **(a)** predicts it barely moves, because launch geometry is untouched, and a flat answer
below ~2.8 ms refutes (b) and re-opens the ramp story under a different name. The existing
`--tile 6`/`--iters 60` ladder is the shape to copy, and the run must validate the LAST replica as usual.

```bash
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q7-window && tools/gpu_lock.sh util
for t in 6 12 24; do
  .venv/bin/python prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py --layers all --fixture dec8 --mode all \
      --rows-sweep 8 --tile $t --iters 30
done
tools/gpu_lock.sh release q7-window
```

**M2 — re-take the stage-clock family rows at 128 lanes, three runs, same prompt recipe as Q6.1.**
Expectation: `mlp` ≈4.26 ms, `proj` ≈4.24, `lm_head` ≈2.20, `cast` ≈0.92, `TOTAL` ≈14.4 at `stage` and
≈15.0 at `kernel`, `syncs_per_step` 323 and 425, `plan_match=1`, both `STAGEPARITY` flags 1 — i.e. the
rows above within ~5 %. Anything outside that band, or a `syncs_per_step` that is not 425, voids this
section rather than updating it. If M1 or M3 changes the engine, this is also the A/B: same command,
before and after, and the diff is the claim.

```bash
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q7-stage && tools/gpu_lock.sh util
ids_for() {
  .venv/bin/python -c "
import sys; sys.path.insert(0, 'tools')
from context_sweep import build_prompt
from transformers import AutoTokenizer
tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained('models/Qwen3.5-0.8B')
print(','.join(str(t) for t in build_prompt(tok, $1)))"
}
IDS128=$(ids_for 112); mkdir -p tmp/q7
for i in 1 2 3; do timeout 900 ./engine/build/decode_run --ids "$IDS128" --steps 16 \
    --time-stages > tmp/q7/stage128_$i.log 2>&1; done
tools/gpu_lock.sh release q7-stage
python3 tools/instep_family_budget.py tmp/q7/stage128_{1,2,3}.log
```

**M3 — the A/B for the only shipped change this section could have made, held until it is authorized.**
Packed projections (GDN 4→1, attention 3→1, proj 114→48). Pre-registered under (b): the step must improve
by roughly the removed dispatch and nothing else, **≈0.1–0.25 ms**, with `proj`'s in-situ row dropping by
about that much and `mlp`'s unchanged; if instead `proj` drops by ≥1 ms, (b) is wrong about the traffic
families and launch count is the lever after all. The proof standard is the one already in force, and it
is not negotiable from this side: byte-identical across all `dec8` outputs, identical gate counts on all
eight fixtures, `tools/margin_delta.py` ×1.0000, plus `plan_match=1` after §07 and `gate.py::launch_plan`
move together. Not taken here because it edits the spec table and the gate's plan, which is the main
agent's call, not a worker's.

**M4 — the contradiction check Q6.1 already specified: the captured step.** Under (b) it must buy
**≈0.06 ms** (the §1 eager-vs-captured per-kernel figure), and anything ≥5 % of the step contradicts this
section and is the result, not the section. Same window as M1/M2 if the stream-handle work is done.

## Q10 (2026-10-13): the gate/up weight walk is switchable in the engine — `--mlp-map`, bit-identical on all eight fixtures; the projections have no such knob, and the residual there is not a mapping

§Q9 ended with a named next package: read `gate` and `up` as two contiguous passes instead of
interleaving their rows, at the usual proof standard — byte-identical outputs, `margin_delta` ×1.0000,
gate counts unchanged, and a measured A/B against the **4.264 ms** in-step `mlp` row it is meant to move
(§Q6.1's row; roofline **2.230 ms**, so the family sits at 52 %). This section lands the switch and its
correctness proof. The timing A/B is pre-registered at the bottom and belongs to the main agent's window.

### What ships

One switch, default = what has always run. The walk itself lives in the op header — one spelling, the rule
in `prototype/README.md` — and the engine only selects an instantiation of it:

| file | what it adds |
|---|---|
| `prototype/mlp_gemv/mlp_gemv_kernels.h` | `MlpMap` (`MAP_INTERLEAVE`, `MAP_CONTIG`), one shared `accum_word()`, and both walks as a template parameter on `mlp_gateup<IN, MAP>` / `mlp_gateup_tokens<IN, TPB, MAP>` |
| `engine/kernels.h` | `enum class MlpGateMap {kInterleave, kContig}`, `parse_mlp_gate_map` / `mlp_gate_map_name` (unknown words **throw** — this flag decides which side of an A/B a run is on, so a silently ignored typo would price the wrong thing), and `mlp_gateup(…, MlpGateMap map = kInterleave)` |
| `engine/kernels_mlp.cpp` | `launch_gateup_single_kernel<IN, MAP>` and the batched equivalent, chosen by a host branch. The shipped mapping compiles exactly as it did before; nothing was added to its inner loop |
| `engine/decode_step.h` | `DecodeScratch::mlp_gate_map`, defaulting to the shipped walk — it rides on the scratch for the same reasons `wave_rows` does |
| `engine/decode_step.cpp` | the **one** `mlp_gateup` call site passes `sc.mlp_gate_map`, so the single-token and the wave paths cannot disagree about which walk ran |
| `engine/decode_run.cpp` | `--mlp-map interleave|contig`. It prints `MLPMAP gate=…` **only when the flag was given**, so a default run's log stays byte-stable for anything that diffs these logs |

`mlp_down` is deliberately uninvolved: one weight stream, no mapping to choose. Both the prototype binary
and the engine refuse `--mapping`/`--mlp-map` together with a down-only shape rather than ignoring it, so a
null A/B can never be a mis-typed flag wearing the costume of a result.

### The proof

All of it is bit-comparison, all of it correctness-only, each command under the lock, `util` 91–93 % at
acquire (the serving engine, discounted per `AGENTS.md`), **no timing taken** — the by-product `ms` the
harnesses print were ignored and nothing here entered `bench/performance-status.md`.

1. **The switch is not inert — the compiler says so.** Same template, two instantiations, and their weight
   loads differ in program order (`IN=1024`, gfx1151; command in `prototype/mlp_gemv/results.md` §Q10):

   ```text
   mlp_gateup<1024, interleave>: 8 × global_load_b128, 2 live address pairs, alternating v4,v2,v4,v2,…
   mlp_gateup<1024, contig>:     8 × global_load_b128, 1 live address pair, re-based mid-kernel
   ```

   That is "both streams side by side" versus "one stream, then the other", read off the ISA. It matters
   here more than usual precisely because the outputs are identical: the outputs cannot witness the switch.
2. **Bit-identity in the prototype, all 24 layers** — `--mapping-identity`: `act` 0/3584, `gate_pre`+`up_pre`
   0/7168, `out` 0/1024, batched `act` 0/7168 differing, at every layer; the shipped F1–F4 gate then passes
   under each mapping. wall 19.7 s. The batched form is bit-identical to k=1 under `contig` too (23 040
   elements per layer, 0 differ; wall 13.8 s), and the six `--negative` injections still localise to where
   each bug lives and nowhere else under `contig` (wall 10.6 s). Details and the reasoning in
   `prototype/mlp_gemv/results.md` §Q10.
3. **Byte-identical engine outputs.** Same binary, same prompt, same 8 steps, only the flag differs:

   ```bash
   tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q10-engab && tools/gpu_lock.sh util
   IDS=$(cat /tmp/q10_ids_only.txt)          # dec8's 17 prompt ids
   ./engine/build/decode_run --ids "$IDS" --steps 8 --out tmp/q10/A_inter
   ./engine/build/decode_run --ids "$IDS" --steps 8 --mlp-map contig --out tmp/q10/A_contig
   tools/gpu_lock.sh release q10-engab
   (cd tmp/q10/A_inter && md5sum *) > tmp/q10/md5_A_int.txt
   (cd tmp/q10/A_contig && md5sum *) > tmp/q10/md5_A_con.txt
   diff tmp/q10/md5_A_int.txt tmp/q10/md5_A_con.txt   # no output: 10 files identical
   ```

   wall 0.61 s and 0.50 s. `MLPMAP gate=contig` appears in the second run's stdout and not the first.
4. **Gate counts, all eight fixtures, both mappings.** `engine/gate.py` takes `--binary` but has no
   engine-arg passthrough, so the alternative walk was gated through a two-line wrapper that adds the flag
   (`tmp/q10/decode_run_contig.sh`, not committed) — the flag path itself is what got gated, which is the
   stronger of the two ways to do it:

   | fixture | interleave | contig | same? |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | `dec8` | 168 pass, 0 red, PASS | 168 pass, 0 red, PASS | ✓ |
   | `dec64f` | 650, 0, PASS | 650, 0, PASS | ✓ |
   | `dec64t` | 647, 0, PASS | 647, 0, PASS | ✓ |
   | `dec300f` | 1156, 0, PASS | 1156, 0, PASS | ✓ |
   | `mt32f` | 391, 0, PASS | 391, 0, PASS | ✓ |
   | `p128` | 197, 0, PASS | 197, 0, PASS | ✓ |
   | `wide520f` | 327, 0, PASS | 327, 0, PASS | ✓ |
   | `chunk64b` | 372 pass, **3 D-9 red**, FAIL as committed | 372 pass, 3 red, FAIL | ✓ |

   Stronger than the counts: md5 over each fixture's per-step decision lines (argmax + cos-deficit +
   meanrel) is **identical between the two log sets for all eight fixtures**, and `tools/band_history.py
   --check` — which the gate runs before it reads a fixture — stayed green throughout, i.e. no band, pin or
   plan was touched. Walls per fixture run: 2.9–18.4 s per mapping.
5. **`tools/margin_delta.py tmp/q10/int tmp/q10/con <all eight>`: ×1.0000 on every column of every
   fixture**, including the tree's tightest lanes (dec64t cos filled 0.9982, dec300f 0.9992/0.9997,
   wide520f 0.9996/0.9833). The worst step per column did not even change identity.
6. **`engine/gate.py --selftest --dry-run`: SELFTEST PASS** (3.0 s, CPU-only, no device) — the injected-bug
   needles the gate depends on are still caught, which is what licenses reading item 4 as a real pass.

Nothing was loosened: no gate, band, pin, allowance or fixture was edited, and `speccheck/specimpl.py` was
not touched (the change cannot reach it — `speccheck` is the torch reference and never sees a HIP kernel).

### The other shapes: same mechanism, and is the same fix available?

§Q9's three ratios say the exposure is not uniform, and reading the launchers says why: **the interleave
takes two weight streams in one launch, and the engine has exactly one such launch** — `mlp_gateup`. Every
`proj_gemv` call site passes one weight tensor (`PACK_X = false`, one `layer_dev(...)` per launch), so
there is nothing for `contig` to un-interleave anywhere in the projection family; the prototype's
two-weight `proj_gemv` form is not what the engine launches.

| shape (decode path) | weight streams | is the Q10 mechanism present? | is the Q10 fix available? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `gate_proj` + `up_proj` (24 layers) | **2** | yes — this is the 0.470 pair | **yes, and it ships here** |
| `down_proj` (24 layers) | 1 | no interleave; its 0.629 is the bare row-walk spread plus the row tail | no, not without moving bits — see below |
| `in_proj_qkv` 6144×1024, `in_proj_z` 2048×1024, `q_proj` 4096×1024, `k_proj`/`v_proj` 512×1024, `o_proj`/`out_proj` 1024×2048 | 1 each | no | no, same reason as `down` |
| `in_proj_a` / `in_proj_b` 16×1024 | 1 | no — and their deficit is not walk-shaped at all | different lever entirely, see below |

Two things follow, and they are the useful part of this verdict.

* **For `down` and every projection, a "contiguous" mapping would not be bit-neutral.** With one stream, the
  only way to make a warp's walk more contiguous is to change which lanes own which elements of the row (or
  split a row across wavefronts). That reorders the F32 accumulation of that row, which moves BF16 bits,
  which is a **band question** — the margins are ledger-policed (`tools/band_history.py --check` runs ahead
  of every fixture) and it is not a worker's call. The bit-neutral geometry knob in this family is the one
  Q6 already established — rows per block, bit-identical by row ownership — and it is already swept and
  shipped (8 for the MLP, 32 for the projections). The grid's instantaneous footprint is another way to say
  the same constraint: `grid × rows_per_block = rows`, so any geometry that keeps one wavefront per row has
  the whole weight region live at once, and no mapping does anything about that. If the pre-registered rows
  below come back null for `down`-shaped traffic, that is the door this section could not open, and the
  next package is a reduction-order change priced as such, not another walk.
* **`in_proj_a`/`in_proj_b` are a separate, smaller, and cheaper question.** They are 16 rows at
  `kRowsPerBlock = 32`: `grid.x = 1`, and half of every block's warps have no row. Their row in
  `bench/performance-status.md` §4 is 5 % of probe for 32 KiB of weight per launch, which is launch and
  occupancy, not walk pattern. The two available levers are the packed-projection change already costed as
  M3 in §Q7 (a spec-table and gate-plan change, the main agent's call) or a per-shape width for the 16-row
  launches (bit-identical by Q6's argument). Neither belongs in this commit, and neither is a mapping.

### Pre-registered measurements, expectations written before any of them ran

Every row is the main agent's: timing, exclusive window, blocking, zero subagents (`AGENTS.md`,
`bench/performance-status.md` §6). Quoting medians of three, and voiding rather than discounting a run with
a contender, is the rule this series has used since Q6.1.

**Q10-M0 — the validity ceiling that binds all rows below.** This box's measured read roofline is
237 GB/s; **any `contig` row above ~240 GB/s is skipped work, not speed** and must be treated as a broken
measurement and re-taken, not booked as a win. The change moves bytes between two allocations, it does not
reduce them, so a row that beats the roofline has found a bug in the measurement or in the kernel.

**Q10-M1 — prototype burst, both mappings, both shapes; `down` as the built-in control.**

```bash
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q10-m1 && tools/gpu_lock.sh util
for t in 6 24; do for m in interleave contig; do
  .venv/bin/python prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py --fixture dec8 --step decode0 --layers all \
      --mode all --rows-sweep 8 --tile $t --iters 30 --mapping $m
done; done
tools/gpu_lock.sh release q10-m1
```

Expectation, `--tile 6` (the window the committed cold line was taken with): `interleave` reproduces
`results.md` §Performance's 195.2 GB/s (82 % of probe) within a few %, and **`contig` reaches toward the
contiguous line Q9 measured at 92.7 % — call it confirmed at ≥ 215 GB/s (90 %), partially confirmed in
201–215, null within ±3 % of the `interleave` row (≤ ~201)**. `--tile 24` is the same comparison at
Q7-M1's window size, which is the row that behaves like the step; read it as a ratio, not as an absolute.
`--mode down` must be unchanged between the two runs (±3 %) — it is the control that shows the flag did not
touch anything it should not have, and it also prices how much of `down`'s 0.629 was ever walk-shaped.
Honest caveat, stated before the run: the prototype burst already reaches 84 % of probe at this shape, so
it has far less headroom than the engine's in-step 52 %. **A null here does not refute Q9**; M2 is the
decisive row.

**Q10-M2 — the decisive row: the stage-clock family `mlp` figure, in-situ, 128 lanes, three runs per side.**
Same prompt recipe and command as Q7's M2, with `--mlp-map contig` added on the A side.

```bash
tools/gpu_lock.sh acquire q10-m2 && tools/gpu_lock.sh util
ids_for() { .venv/bin/python -c "
import sys; sys.path.insert(0, 'tools')
from context_sweep import build_prompt
from transformers import AutoTokenizer
tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained('models/Qwen3.5-0.8B')
print(','.join(str(t) for t in build_prompt(tok, $1)))"; }
IDS128=$(ids_for 112); mkdir -p tmp/q10/stage
for i in 1 2 3; do timeout 900 ./engine/build/decode_run --ids "$IDS128" --steps 16 --time-stages \
    > tmp/q10/stage/int128_$i.log 2>&1; done
for i in 1 2 3; do timeout 900 ./engine/build/decode_run --ids "$IDS128" --steps 16 --time-stages \
    --mlp-map contig > tmp/q10/stage/con128_$i.log 2>&1; done
tools/gpu_lock.sh release q10-m2
python3 tools/instep_family_budget.py tmp/q10/stage/int128_{1,2,3}.log
python3 tools/instep_family_budget.py tmp/q10/stage/con128_{1,2,3}.log
```

Baseline **4.264 ms/token** (52 % of its 2.230 ms roofline). Bands, against the same roofline the row is
published against: **null ≥ 4.10 ms** (within 4 % — nothing moved); **partial 3.30–4.10 ms**; **confirmed
≤ 3.30 ms** (≥ 23 % of the gap closed); **landing 2.40–2.75 ms**, where the cold-burst figure says a fully
streaming walk should sit. Anything **below 2.230 ms is better than its own roofline** and is a measurement
error, not a result (Q10-M0). Witnesses required on both sides or the A/B is void: `plan_match=1`, both
`STAGEPARITY` flags 1, identical `syncs_per_step`, and the contig logs carrying `MLPMAP gate=contig` while
the interleave logs carry none. If the two sides differ in any witness, this row does not exist yet.

**Q10-M3 — the step-level consequence.** With Δ = 4.264 − (M2's `contig` row), predict the step at 128
lanes moves from **12.399 ms → ≈12.399 − Δ**, and the same Δ at 512 and 1024 lanes, with the *fractional*
gain shrinking as the lane count grows (the non-MLP part of the step grows there). Read from
`tools/context_sweep.py`. The null band is the step's own reproducibility, ≈ ±0.6 ms at this size: a Δ
under that is not established by this row and should not be reported as a win. If the gain instead *grows*
with lane count, the mechanism is not the weight walk (weight bytes per token are divided by `TPB = 4`
regardless of lane count) and the result must be written up as a contradiction rather than a win.

**Q10-M4 — cross-check that this is weight-walk-shaped and not x-shaped: prefill.** The wave path reads
the weights once per 4-token tile, so whatever the walk costs, it costs about 4× less per token there.
Predict **|Δ TTFT| < 2 %** on `tools/prefill_sweep.py` between the two mappings. A TTFT move over 5 % — in
either direction — means the mapping is reaching into the x/L2 traffic rather than the weight stream, and
that is a finding about the probe's model of the box, not about the MLP.

**Q10-M5 — only if M1 confirms and M2 is null: price the mapping in the instrument that found it.** That
would be the first disagreement in this series between the probe and the step, so the conclusion is not
"the fix is inert"; it is that `tools/mixed_bw --pattern engine-mlp` (which produced the 0.470) has to grow
a contiguous-walk mode of its own — same bytes, same grid, one stream per pass — and say whether `contig`
reaches `plain` there at all. That is a probe change with its own byte-accounting selftest cases, not a
quick flag.

**And the decision this section deliberately does not make.** The two mappings are bit-identical, so if M2
confirms, making `contig` the default is a one-word change with no re-pin and no margin re-price; if M2 is
null, the switch stays as an A/B instrument and the walk hypothesis is dead for this kernel, which sends
the MLP's residual back to the row tail — and the row tail is not bit-neutral. Either way the default flip
is the main agent's call after the numbers, not a worker's now.

---

## Q10b (2026-10-13): the gate/up walk now follows the row count — `contig` for a one-row body, `interleave` for a wave; `--mlp-map` stays the explicit override

§Q10 ended with the flag shipped and the default left alone, because the two paths wanted opposite walks
and nothing in the code could say which one to run: `contig` recovered **0.945 ms** of the MLP's 2.034 ms
in-step gap at 128 lanes of decode (**4.340 → 3.395 ms**, M2) and took the step from **12.699 → 11.607 ms**
(+9.5 %, M3), while TTFT at a 1024-token prompt went the other way, **1043.8 → 1097.0 ms, +5.1 %** (M4).
The decision on the record is that the engine may use different settings on the prefill and the generation
path, so the split became a code decision instead of a global flag: **one-row body → `contig`, wave body →
`interleave`, chosen at the call site, with `--mlp-map` still able to pin either one.**

### The rule as it ships

One enum value, one function, one call site. Nothing else about the MLP moved: `mlp_down` still has one
weight stream and no mapping, rows-per-block is still 8 for both forms, the token tile is still `TPB = 4`,
and the kernel source is byte-unchanged (`prototype/mlp_gemv/mlp_gemv_kernels.h` is untouched — both walks
already existed there from Q10).

| file | what it adds |
|---|---|
| `engine/kernels.h` | `MlpGateMap::kAuto` (a *request* for the row-count walk, not a third walk) and `resolve_mlp_gate_map(want, rows)`, which is the whole rule in three lines: an explicit walk is returned unchanged, else `rows == 1 → kContig`, `rows > 1 → kInterleave`. `mlp_gateup`'s map argument no longer has a default — a caller has to say which walk it means — and since default arguments must be trailing, `tokens` lost its default with it (one caller, and it passes both) |
| `engine/kernels_mlp.cpp` | the definition of `resolve_mlp_gate_map`, `auto` accepted by `parse_mlp_gate_map` (still throws on anything else), and `mlp_gateup` **throws** if `kAuto` reaches it, so the split cannot silently degrade into "always interleave" |
| `engine/decode_step.h` | `DecodeScratch::mlp_gate_map` now defaults to `kAuto` |
| `engine/decode_step.cpp` | the one `mlp_gateup` call site resolves against `n`, the body's row count — the same seam attention uses to pick its plan (§Q5/P7+P8; the sentence in `engine/kernels_attn.cpp` is "TWO PLANS, and the thing that chooses between them is the row count"). The launcher stays plan-blind |
| `engine/decode_run.cpp` | `--mlp-map auto\|interleave\|contig`; the default is `auto` and, as in Q10, a run that names no flag prints no `MLPMAP` line, so existing log diffs stay byte-stable. Naming `auto` prints `MLPMAP gate=auto`, which makes a ledger row self-describing |

Two things the rule deliberately does **not** try to be. It is not "decode vs prefill": the input is the
body's row count, so a prefill whose last body is one token (`prefill_wave` splits at `kMaxWaveRows`)
gets `contig` for that body — a one-row body is a one-row body wherever it appears. And it is not a
per-path settings framework: the mapping is the only knob here, because it is the only one with two
measured numbers pulling in opposite directions (see the queue note at the end for the only other
candidate, which is not this package).

### Why the two paths want opposite walks — the one hard fact, and where the explanation stops

The compiler gives one structural fact, from the engine's own TU at `-O3`/gfx1151 (nothing device-side):

```bash
R=$PWD; mkdir -p tmp/q10b/isa-eng && cd tmp/q10b/isa-eng
hipcc -O3 -Wno-unused-command-line-argument --offload-arch=gfx1151 --save-temps \
      -I"$R" -I"$R/engine" -c "$R/engine/kernels_mlp.cpp" -o k.o
S=kernels_mlp-hip-amdgcn-amd-amdhsa-gfx1151.s
# per instantiation: one body, then its weight loads' destination <- address pair, in order
body() { awk -v k="$1" 'BEGIN{f=0} f&&/^[A-Za-z_].*:[ ;]/{exit} $0 ~ ("^"k){f=1;next} f{print}' $S; }
for k in _Z10mlp_gateupILi1024ELi1E _Z10mlp_gateupILi1024ELi0E \
         _Z17mlp_gateup_tokensILi1024ELi4ELi1E _Z17mlp_gateup_tokensILi1024ELi4ELi0E; do
  printf '%s ' "$k"; body "$k" | grep global_load_b128 \
      | sed -E 's/.*b128 ([^,]*), ([^,]*),.*/\1<-\2/' | tr -d ' ' | tr '\n' ' '; echo
done
```

| instantiation | weight `global_load_b128`s in the loop the compiler left | live weight words |
|---|---|---|
| `mlp_gateup<1024, interleave>` (one row) | 8 loads off **two** bases, alternating `v[4:5]`, `v[2:3]`, `v[4:5]`, … | ≥ 2 |
| `mlp_gateup<1024, contig>` (one row) | 8 loads off **one** base `v[17:18]`, offsets `0/512/1024/1536` per stream | 1 (dest reused within a stream) |
| `mlp_gateup_tokens<1024,4, interleave>` (wave) | `v[21:24] ← v[21:22]`, then `v[25:28] ← v[12:13]` — two dests, two bases | **2 in flight** |
| `mlp_gateup_tokens<1024,4, contig>` (wave) | `v[21:24] ← v[4:5]`, then `v[21:24] ← v[2:3]` — **the same destination twice**, so the second load is WAR-blocked behind the first's consumers | **1 in flight** |

So, answering the question §Q10 left open: **the template parameter is not inert on the tokens path.** It
changes the emitted schedule — under `contig` the two weight loads collapse onto one destination quad and
the second cannot land until `TPB = 4` tokens' worth of FMAs have drained the first; under `interleave`
both weight words are live at once. That is also why the wave loses something the one-row body does not
miss: the batched kernel is the only one of the two whose whole per-iteration job is to hold a weight word
still while four tokens consume it.

The rest is a candidate, not a finding, and it is written as one. `contig` trades one kind of memory
behaviour for another: it halves the instantaneous weight footprint a resident block set touches (one
stream marched at a time instead of two 7.2 MiB allocations alternated 16 bytes at a time — the shape Q9
measured at 0.470 of a contiguous sweep, and weights are 99.4 % of what a token reads), and it pays for
that with less memory-level parallelism per iteration (one weight word in flight instead of two, visible in
every row of the table above). A one-row body is the case where the first is worth most — the walk is the
bottleneck there — and a wave body is the case where the second costs most and the first is worth least,
because `TPB = 4` divides its weight bytes per token by four. The measured sizes fit that story in an
instructive way: prefill moved **less** than decode (5.1 % against 9.5 %) and in the opposite direction,
which is the same factor-of-four asymmetry arriving from the other side.

**One sentence for the next reader, so nobody mistakes this for a law of the hardware: the mechanism is
not established by anything measured here** — the destination-collapse is a fact about LLVM's `-O3`
schedule on these two shapes, the footprint argument is inherited from Q9 rather than re-derived, and the
split is encoded on two measured timings, not on this explanation. The falsifiable version, when someone
wants the real reason: take a timed burst of the prototype at both shapes and both walks
(`prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py`, `--tile` rows for k=1 and `--tokens p64` for the batched form). If the
ordering flips between the two shapes — `contig` ahead at k=1, `interleave` ahead at `TPB = 4` — the story
above has legs; if both shapes prefer the same walk in the prototype and only the engine disagrees, the
story is wrong and the asymmetry lives in the engine's launch context, not in the walk.

### The proof

All bit-comparison, correctness-only, each command under `tools/gpu_lock.sh` (`util` 92–93 % at acquire,
the serving engine, discounted per `AGENTS.md`), one device run at a time, **no timing taken** — the `ms`
the harnesses print as a by-product were ignored and nothing below entered `bench/performance-status.md`.
The three driver scripts live under `tmp/q10b/` (`engab.sh`, `run_gates.sh`, `counts_compare.sh`) and are
not committed; every command they run is spelled out here.

1. **Byte-identical engine outputs, old default vs new default.** Old binary = `engine/build/decode_run`
   rebuilt at `ac248fd` (default `interleave` everywhere), new = this change. Same prompt (dec8's 17
   ids), same 8 steps: **all 10 output files md5-identical**, and `--mlp-map auto` identical to both while
   being the only one of the three that prints `MLPMAP gate=auto` (default runs print nothing, so old logs
   still diff clean). wall 0.62 / 0.46 / 0.50 s. The last three edits to this change were comments and
   this document; the rebuilt binary was then re-checked on this same comparison and on `dec8`'s gate
   (168 pass, 0 red, PASS, wall 2.53 s) so the gated build and the committed source agree.
2. **Gate counts, all eight fixtures, new default**, against Q10's `interleave`-default logs
   (`tmp/q10/int_*.log`, taken from the binary at `3411539`; the only commit since touched
   `bench/performance-status.md`, so those logs are still valid old-side witnesses and were cited rather
   than re-made):

   | fixture | old default | new default | same? | wall |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | `dec8` | 168 pass, 0 red, PASS | 168, 0, PASS | ✓ | 2.54 s |
   | `dec64f` | 650, 0, PASS | 650, 0, PASS | ✓ | 6.18 s |
   | `dec64t` | 647, 0, PASS | 647, 0, PASS | ✓ | 6.47 s |
   | `dec300f` | 1156, 0, PASS | 1156, 0, PASS | ✓ | 16.61 s |
   | `mt32f` | 391, 0, PASS | 391, 0, PASS | ✓ | 10.00 s |
   | `p128` | 197, 0, PASS | 197, 0, PASS | ✓ | 6.10 s |
   | `wide520f` | 327, 0, PASS | 327, 0, PASS | ✓ | 16.34 s |
   | `chunk64b` | 372 pass, 3 red, FAIL | 372, 3, FAIL | ✓ | 7.07 s |

   Stronger than the counts, as in Q10: md5 over each fixture's per-step decision lines (argmax +
   cos-deficit + meanrel) is **identical old vs new for all eight**, and `chunk64b`'s three red lines are
   the same three D-9 steps ("chunk64b: 3 of 3", step 0) with its committed FAIL intact.
3. **`tools/margin_delta.py tmp/q10/int tmp/q10b/new <all eight>`: ×1.0000 on every column of every
   fixture**, with the worst step per column unchanged in identity — including the tree's tightest lanes
   (`dec64t` cos 0.9982, `dec300f` 0.9992 / 0.9997, `wide520f` 0.9996 / 0.9833, `mt32f` 0.9973).
4. **`engine/gate.py --selftest --dry-run`: SELFTEST PASS** (3.15 s, CPU-only) — the injected needles the
   gate is trusted to catch are still caught, which is what licenses reading item 2 as a real pass.
5. **`tools/band_history.py --check` green** (15 band values against `bench/band-history.jsonl`): no band,
   pin, allowance, plan or fixture was touched, and `speccheck/specimpl.py` was not touched (it cannot be
   reached — `speccheck` is the torch reference and never sees a HIP kernel).
6. **The wave body is bit-neutral under *both* walks**, which is what makes a default that mixes them per
   body byte-neutral: `prototype/mlp_gemv/run.py --fixture dec8 --no-time --tile 0 --tokens p64
   --mapping interleave` and the same with `--mapping contig` — each **23 040 elements compared, 0 differ**
   against the k=1 kernel at layers [0, 3, 11, 23], with the F1–F4 gate PASSing under each. wall 14.41 s
   and 14.19 s.
7. **The split is live, and would fail loudly if it were not.** Bit-identical outputs cannot witness a
   mapping change — that is the whole reason the two walks are legal — so the witnesses are: the ISA table
   above, taken from the engine's own TU, which shows both instantiations present on both paths (
   `mlp_gateup<1024|3584, 0|1>` and `mlp_gateup_tokens<1024,4,0|1>` / `<3584,2,0|1>`), and the throw in
   `mlp_gateup` that fires if an unresolved `kAuto` ever reaches a launcher. **The confirming witness is
   C1 below**: if the in-situ `mlp` row does not move, the default is inert, and the place to look is the
   row count reaching the call site, not the memory system.

### Pre-registered: the three numbers, with what a miss would mean (written before any of them ran)

All three are the main agent's: exclusive window, blocking, zero subagents, medians of three, per
`bench/performance-status.md` §6. Same commands as Q10-M2/M3/M4 with the `--mlp-map` flag simply absent.

| # | row | prediction | if it misses |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C1** | stage-clock family `mlp`, 128 lanes, new default | **≈ 3.395 ms/token** — Q10's `contig` row, because a generation body is one row and now selects `contig` | ≥ 4.10 ms = the default is **inert**, not physically surprising: check that `n == 1` reaches `submit_wave_body` on the generation path and that the `--mlp-map` default really is `auto`. Landing between 3.30 and 4.10 while `--mlp-map contig` still reads 3.395 means the row count is right but something else differs between "auto" and "contig", which should be impossible (they are the same instantiation) and would be a bug. Below 2.230 ms is better than its own roofline and is a measurement error (Q10-M0) |
| **C2** | step burst, 128 lanes, new default | **≈ 11.6 ms/token** (Q10 measured 11.607 with `contig`) | ≈ 12.7 ms = the one-row half did not take effect (same failure mode as C1). Below ~11.0 ms means something besides the MLP moved and this row cannot attribute it |
| **C3** | TTFT, 1024-token prompt, new default | **≈ 1044 ms**, i.e. Q10's `interleave` side (1043.8) — the regression is gone, not traded away | ≈ 1097 ms = the wave half did not take effect. Anything in between is the one case worth chasing: it would mean a prefill body of exactly one row picked up `contig`. A 1024-token prompt at `kMaxWaveRows = 512` is two bodies of 512, so this fixture should be clean; a prompt length ≡ 1 (mod 512) is the shape that would legitimately mix the two walks inside one TTFT |

**And the ledger consequence, stated plainly: a row-count-dependent default makes Q10's single-flag A/B
rows non-comparable.** Q10's `interleave` row and `contig` row are each one global mapping; the new default
is a third configuration that runs `contig` on the generation bodies and `interleave` on the wave bodies.
So the new default's step/TTFT/family rows must be **measured from scratch and published as new rows**, not
compared to or spliced into Q10's rows, and C1–C3 above are predictions against Q10's per-mapping rows
precisely because those are the only two measurements that decompose it.

What this leaves unexplained, named: even at C1's 3.395 ms the MLP still sits **1.09 ms above its 2.230 ms
roofline**, which is the part of Q9's penalty the mapping was not — the row tail, and whatever `down`'s
0.629 was made of (§Q10's "other shapes": not reachable by a bit-neutral mapping). One queue note:
`kRowsPerBlock` (8, shared by both `mlp_gateup` forms) is the only other knob the same per-path argument
could reach — Q6 established it is bit-identical by row ownership and swept it for the one-row shape only —
but it has no second measured number pulling against it today, so it stays a queue decision rather than a
package.


## Q11 (2026-10-13): D-9's near-tie becomes a **sized** rule — `chunk64b` leaves the expected-red list as a green that names its own tie, and the size comes from the band the gate already polices

D-9 (`bench/plan-2026-10-12.md`) kept `chunk64b` step 0 RED for one honest reason: the pin puts **8337 at
15.625** and **20619 at 15.500** — a unique winner by **0.125**, which is **2 BF16 ULP** at that magnitude —
while the engine's legal element error at that step is larger (measured maxabs **0.2509**). Demanding the
pinned id there is asking a bit-tolerant fixture to be bit-exact. D-9's second half said the fix must not be
a number invented to fit: *"the step where the rule starts admitting tokens needs its own `D-x`, its own
band, and the same provenance as D-1 had — until then the line stays red and the reason stays written."*
This is that derivation, and the reason is now replaced by a threshold with a source.

### The bound, and the one place a number like this can come from

Per judged step *j*:

```
bound(j) = meanrel cap in force at step j  ×  mean|pin_j|
```

`meanrel` is defined (§10.2, and `gate.py::metrics` implements exactly that) as `mean|Δ| / mean|ref|`, and it is
policed per step by the check named `step j: meanrel <= 5 % (§10.2)` — or, on a priced fixture,
